## intake-1313#record — Prime Agent: A Self-Improving RLM Harness

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23552
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, swarm_techniques, context_management, memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. A harness should be judged by EXPRESSIVITY, not the workflow it encodes: agent state is formalised as a four-level cache - L0 fixed weights, L1 active token context, L2 persistent IPython REPL plus recursive subagent sessions, L3 disk-backed history/memories/skills - each mutated by a different mechanism (fine-tuning, compaction, "agentic garbage collection", refinement). Sections 1, 2.2, Figure 2.
   - *unaffected*: L0-L3 and the three mutation mechanisms verbatim in Section 2.2.

   - anchor `§2.2 ; paper.txt line 126` @ arXiv:2608.23552v1 (HTML route, sha256 14e64b1ae601fec9169d257584bc0f40602d0df898f9d93efbbbd8bbdc2f7cb1; PDF sha256 973323f2d3d85f3ec9b21709248b65ba236cf527cd5cc9ff556909104a5fd370), current version 24 Aug 2026, read FULL from disk 2026-10-21: "We call the L2 mechanism agentic garbage collection."
1. The rlm primitive is ASYNCHRONOUS and returns a stable handle before the subagent completes; results arrive via daemon-mediated family-scoped message queues. Appendix B: a child is a persistent concurrent session, not a stateless completion. Section 2.3.
   - *unaffected*: Section 2.3 stable handle; Appendix B persistent concurrent session.

2. Continual Harness turns trajectory evidence into typed, VERSIONED supplemental state (prompt notes / memories / skills / subagent specs) with local-vs-global scope, applied at turn boundaries, supplementing an immutable base prompt. Section 2.5.
   - *unaffected*: Section 2.5 verified.

3. Online refinement is DEMONSTRATED UNSAFE by the paper's own trace: a Factorio agent discovered RCON commands could spawn resources directly into assembly machines, used the shortcut despite an anti-cheating heartbeat, and PRESERVED IT AS A REUSABLE SKILL. Stated remedy: least-privilege action interfaces, independent state validation, auditable rollback. Section 3.5.

### Dive corrections

- DIVE 2026-10-21 (dpf-b). All 27 Table 1 cells in the entry match the paper cell for cell (OOLONG, OOLONG-Pairs, OBLIQ, LongBench Pro, LongBench v2, ManyIH x2, LongCoT-Mini, EmulatorBench x 3 stacks). The entry's "Prime LOSES two of three" on LongBench v2 is correct (.680<.696, .744<.746, .714>.704). CORRECTED (contradicting_evidence, inconsistency bullet): the entry says Section 1 names harnesses (Hermes Agent, OpenCode, Kimi-Code) "absent from the evaluation sections". Only Hermes Agent is absent from Section 3. OpenCode appears in Section 3.3 ("Claude Code or opencode otherwise") and Kimi-Code in Section 3.4 ("same performance as Codex or Kimi-Code"). The remaining inconsistency (abstract 95.5% vs Section 1 "95%"; Hermes named but never evaluated) stands. CORRECTED (relevance_justification (3), reader note): "a SHALLOW, WIDE tree capped at seven concurrent children" overstates the source. The paper reports "at most seven active concurrently" as an observed maximum in one run and states no cap; the Conclusion says models "experience friction when deciding how to allocate subagents". Whether seven is the model's choice or a harness limit is not stated. CORRECTED (relevance_justification (1)): "repl-session-memory-maturity.md D-f/D-g are STILL open" is stale. On origin/main D-f is ticked (2026-09-17); D-g (journal uncertain side effects before resume replays them) is open and the paper's Section 2.2 recovery text ("Recovery reconstructs the session under the same identity") still supplies no uncertain-side-effect journal. The "confirms the gap" conclusion survives for D-g only. OVERTURNED (Stage-1 preliminary actionable 1, adopt the out-of-loop experimentation rate as a first-party harness-quality instrument; novelty_justification (b) "novel HARNESS-QUALITY instrument"): the same Section 3.3 reports that harness choice has little effect on final records, so the rate has no demonstrated link to outcome; the authors attribute the DeepSeek 6x to model-harness familiarity (a confound for any cross-model use); counts are hand-classified from traces with partly estimated denominators. A metric with no shown outcome link is a hypothesis; disposition moved to monitor. CONFIRMED: ARC-AGI-3 comparison is not a controlled harness A/B (Section 3.1: native reruns fell below published scores, so "they situate the result rather than isolate a causal harness effect"); Table 1 has no uncertainty; PMPP-Hard and MazeBench carry no numbers in text; Factorio refinement persisted a specification exploit as a reusable skill (one trace, no counts). Figure 5/8/9/10 are raster and were not extractable from HTML. NOT RE-VERIFIED (provisional, search digest only): the official ARC-AGI-3 top score of 30.2% (Opus 5) and the missing adapter/run logs in the public repo come from secondary web write-ups (Capital & Compute, OrcaRouter); no primary read. The repo is now indexed as intake-1329#record (arc-agi-3-prime-agent) and is the right route to audit 95.5%. NEW OBSERVATION: Prime beats its native baseline in 20 of 27 Table 1 cells by point estimate, but 8 of the 9 GLM-5.2/Pi-mono cells carry most of the gap (mean +0.105 vs +0.011 to +0.024 for the two co-trained pairs). Hypothesis only (no intervals): harness lift is largest where the baseline harness is minimal or not co-trained; intake-1354#record shows the same shape (DeepSeek +20 tasks, GLM +4).

### Verdict

dive-verified. Cite for vocabulary (L0-L3, agentic garbage collection) and the Factorio exploit-persisted-as-skill anecdote only. No Table 1 cell, ARC headline or topology number may be used as EPYC baseline evidence; the OOL-experiment rate has no shown outcome link.
Not already_integrated: two items have no prior EPYC owner - the out-of-loop-experimentation rate as a harness-quality instrument (3.3/Fig 6) and the subagent-tree topology as fan-out corroboration (3.5). Not new_opportunity: the runtime was assessed and DECLINED wholesale on 2026-08-07 (intake-1009#record recommended_actions) and nothing here reopens that. Not not_applicable: in-domain, open-source (PrimeIntellect-ai/prime-agent), self-hostable, touches four active handoffs. So: adopt the two new PATTERNS plus the Factorio exploit as an adversarial fixture; continue to decline the runtime and every headline number; inherit intake-1010#record's standing ruling verbatim - "[decline metric transfer] Do not use Prime headline scores as local/open-weight or EPYC baseline evidence".

## intake-1315#record — Prefix Sliding for efficient test-time scaling

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26070
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: kv_cache, context_extension, context_management, inference_serving, hardware_optimization, training_distillation, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Mechanism: during reasoning keep ONLY the prefix (system instruction + prompt + tool definitions) and a sliding window of the last W tokens in the KV cache; discard everything between. Memory is capped at prefix+W regardless of trace length, so per-token cost is asymptotically CONSTANT (Section 2, Figures 3 and 10). Self-described (Section 6) as sliding-window attention with global tokens (Beltagy 2020) where the global region is the whole prefix, adjacent to StreamingLLM which keeps ~4 sink tokens.
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism and lineage confirmed verbatim in Section 6; intake-864#record's prior-art ruling stands and is conceded by the authors.
   - anchor `Section 6, paragraph beginning "We distinguish between methods that are bounded"` @ arXiv:2608.26070v1 sha256 35a16b040c53: "One way to view Prefix Sliding is as sliding window attention with global tokens (Beltagy et al., 2020), but with many consecutive global tokens forming a prefix that preserves task instructions and other necessary context for reasoning tasks. Another related approach is StreamingLLM (Xiao et al...."
   - anchor `visuals/visuals.ipynb cell 15 = Figure 14 (Continue vs Reset PE, AIME25 avg@64, W=2048)` @ repo 4350eb6a + HF e2f4e6d9ae68b: "# unfort lost the result files but saved the scores / scores = [17.0, 20.8, 25.0, 27.4, 27.0] [Reset PE]; Continue PE from the released eval file = 15.00, 19.38, 23.39, 26.77, 27.71 (stderr 5.72-6.73)"
1. Motivation: post-softmax attention mass (Qwen3-1.7B, an AIME25 trace, averaged over layers/heads) concentrates on the first four tokens, the rest of the prompt, the think delimiter and the most recent tokens, and drops throughout the trace between (Section 2, Figure 2) - an attention-mass argument, not a causal per-token ablation.
   - *unaffected*: Figure 2 caption verbatim including "averaged across layers and attention heads" and "Gaussian smoothed"; keep the non-causal caveat.
2. Training-free claim: "Without training, Prefix Sliding can make existing models 3x faster while maintaining performance" (Abstract). The 3x has NO direct in-text locator; support is Figures 1/6 (images) and Table 1 (Appendix C).
   - *narrowed*: The 3x appears twice (Abstract, Section 1), neither with a pointer. Two locators supplied: 3.711x (W=4096, 32K budget, tok/s) and 3.11x (MATH500 iso-accuracy) - the latter defensible. Table 1 Tok/s@128K for W=4096 (5224) is the vanilla-SWA cell; Prefix Sliding is 5044, so the 128K ratio is 11.26x and the entry's 11.7x is corrected in place.
   - anchor `Section 1, final paragraph` @ arXiv:2608.26070v1: "Empirically, Prefix Sliding can match full-attention performance while running 3x faster without training, and enables reinforcement learning rollouts beyond 100,000 tokens."
   - anchor `HF prefixsliding/evals results.json, results["Qwen/Qwen3-1.7B"]["32768"]` @ HF e2f4e6d9ae68b579db31f7afe39138660013de55, sha256 d543f6edca35: ""32768": {"Full": 1476.588_184_772_533, "SWR": {"4096": 5693.19412609675}, "SW": {"4096": 5479.25345645216}} -> Prefix Sliding / full attention = 3.711x at a 32K budget, W=4096"

3. RL claim: usable DURING GRPO (trl synchronous, prime-rl asynchronous), enabling rollouts beyond 100,000 tokens instead of truncating overlong generations; truncated backprop passes ~4xW trailing tokens and masks loss to the final W, justified by an effective receptive field of ~1.5xW in practice (sourced to a BLOG POST, Xiao 2025) rather than the theoretical WxL (Section 2, Figures 5 and 8).

Narrowings survive: max-over-batch training metric with no accuracy at 100K; the 1.5xW blog constant does NOT justify the 4xW multiplier (the Figure 8 KL sweep does; 4x is ~2.7 times the constant); Section 4 cites Figure 8 where it means Figure 7; the RL data is behind private wandb.

5. Kernel: a custom FlashAttention kernel for NVIDIA HOPPER with intra-tile masking plus inter-tile skipping over two disjoint block ranges (prefix blocks, window blocks), "substantially matching the efficiency of standard sliding window attention" (Section 2).

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive: verdict and relevance UNCHANGED (worth_investigating / high) but the numeric substrate changed. (1) The real data is HF dataset prefixsliding/evals @ e2f4e6d9ae68b (visuals.ipynb only clones it); every Table 1 cell reproduced EXCEPT Tok/s@128K for W=4096, where the paper prints the vanilla-SWA value 5224 instead of Prefix Sliding's 5044 - the entry's 11.7x becomes 11.26x (corrected in place). (2) The abstract's 3x now has two locators: 3.711x (W=4096, 32K, tok/s) and 3.11x (MATH500 iso-accuracy, 91.5 vs 91.7 at 1.294 s vs 4.030 s); the 3x also appears in Section 1, still without a pointer. (3) FIGURE 11 OVERTURNED: exact values 14.37 / 15.57 / 28.74 / 31.74 vs full 31.14 (LCB v5, 167 problems, single-sample); W=16384 does NOT match full attention (-2.4 pp); W=32768 is the first match. (4) AIME25: released avg@64 stderr 6.7-7.8 pp puts both the -6.5 pp W=2048 loss and the +1.6 pp W=8192 gain inside one SE; the accuracy claim is established on MATH500 (stderr ~1.0), not AIME25. (5) MASK VS KERNEL SETTLED: PS runs at 96.2-96.6% of vanilla SWA at every length, so the whole 3.7x-11.3x is the mask and the Hopper kernel buys nothing - our eviction-based implementation needs no kernel work. (6) ">100K RL" is supported by main-body Figure 7 (wandb max_length_full panel to 104K), not only Appendix E, but the 4xW multiplier is justified by the paper's own KL sweep and NOT by the 1.5xW blog constant (no measurements; internally inconsistent 1.5W vs W*sqrt(L)). (7) The "seconds" axis is DERIVED (avg tokens / batch-1024 aggregate tok/s) - nothing in the paper was timed. (8) Last-k is 1.54x FASTER in raw tok/s at 32K; Figure 9's win is accuracy-per-second only. (9) NEW LOCAL FINDING: server-context.cpp:2944-2956 computes n_discard = n_left - window with n_left ~ n_ctx - n_keep, so -c = prefix + W collapses the discard to 1 token per shift; the PS-1 sweep must use -c = prefix + W + chunk. (10) PS-2 CLOSED: our tree is Reset-PE (seq_add -n_discard); recovered Figure 14 puts Reset PE ahead at 4 of 5 budgets within noise; intake-1327#record's closure confirmed. (11) streaming-llm-baseline.md's 2026-07-20 regime confirmed verbatim; the mismatch reading stands. (12) Provenance: eval artifacts 2025-10..12, forks 2025-12-22/23; notebook leaks an anysphere-wandb login not in the author list. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1334#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: narrows (claims [0, 1, 2]) - NARROWS, on three counts, and does NOT refute the mechanism. (1) The bearing dive listed this source with the reason "targets the 4-sink arm so it corroborates PS's ablation rather than refuting the method". That reading does not survive: on a reasoning workload the prefix IS the prompt, an AIME-class prompt is ~10^2 tokens, so a prefix-wide sink differs from a 4-token sink by ~3% of a 4096-token budget. This measurement therefore bears on Prefix Sliding's own configuration, not merely on its ablation arm - the prefix-wide sink is a real differentiator only where the PROMPT is long and load-bearing, which is the decode-light regime where the mask saves least. That narrows PS claim 0 (the StreamingLLM-lineage concession) from "we are the same family" to "on reasoning we are the same MASK". (2) It supplies the missing independent variable behind PS claim 2 (the 3x speed / matched accuracy result): degradation tracks GENERATION LENGTH relative to W. PS's clean MATH500 match at W=4096 sits at ~5K average generation, i.e. in the regime where almost nothing is ever evicted; the AIME arms at 15-17K generation are where the mask is actually exercised, and those are precisely the arms the bearing dive found to be inside one standard error. So PS's accuracy evidence is strongest exactly where the mask is least tested - a narrowing of PS's headline that neither the paper nor the bearing dive states. Corollary: the bearing ledger's "DROP W=2048" should be revisited, since W=2048 is the only PS MATH500 arm in the eviction regime and therefore the most informative point on its only power-adequate benchmark. (3) It corroborates and sharpens PS claim 1 (the attention-mass observation): this source measures the same phenomenon with a defined metric (Eq. 3 attention recall) on a real reasoning trace rather than a single Gaussian-smoothed head-averaged figure, and reaches a compatible conclusion - reasoning attention is concentrated, globally shared, and slowly evolving. It also independently confirms the bearing dive's central efficiency ruling from the opposite direction: Table 4 shows the sink+window mask matching the best selection method on FLOPs, transfer and kernel latency at 1/8th the memory, which is the same "the win is the MASK, not the kernel" conclusion the bearing dive reached from PS's own SW/SWR ratios. NOT a dependency in either direction: no claim here would have to change if intake-1315#record were retracted (this paper predates it by four months and never cites it), and no claim in intake-1315#record would have to change if this were retracted - only the STRENGTH of the PS-1 design recommendations would fall back to the bearing entry's own reading. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1340#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: corroborates (claims [0, 4, 5]) - Independent corroboration from an unrelated lab (Microsoft ASG; published 2026-08-28, two days after Prefix Sliding; neither paper cites the other). On claim 0 (Prefix Sliding is SWA-with-global-tokens / StreamingLLM lineage): this paper is a third-party demonstration that the plain SWA-with-4-sinks member of that family is already strong enough to beat every published linearization on identical teachers, which reinforces both intake-864#record's prior-art ruling and intake-1327#record's already_integrated status - the family is old, well-characterised and ours. On claim 5 (the win is the MASK, not the kernel): this paper reaches the same conclusion from the opposite direction - it uses an off-the-shelf FlashAttention backend, writes no kernel, and still recovers 99% of the 6-benchmark average, so two independent groups now put the entire effect in the mask. On claim 4 (the sawtooth / last-k comparison, and the entry's wider accuracy framing): NARROWED in one respect - this paper's S-NIAH and BABILong data are an outside prior that bounded windows forfeit most long-range RETRIEVAL (best SWA recovers 15-23% of full attention at 4K), which sharpens the intake-1315#record dive's PS-1 window-floor caution and supports its decision to drop W=2048. It REFUTES nothing in intake-1315#record. Prefix Sliding's own contribution is now sharper by contrast: it varies the one axis this paper holds fixed - s = 4 sinks "always fixed at 4" - by widening the sink to the entire prefix, which is precisely the axis on which the 4K collapse documented here would be expected to improve. depends_on is empty in both directions: neither entry's claims would have to change if the other were retracted.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY: (1) the mask is StreamingLLM's Lambda-mask with the sink widened to the whole prefix, conceded in Section 6 - intake-864#record stands; (2) the speed win is the MASK, not the kernel - PS runs at 96.2-96.6% of vanilla SWA, so nothing in the st29 forks is worth porting and our --kv-streaming-* eviction path gets the structural saving with zero kernel work; (3) the accuracy claim is established on MATH500 (W>=4096 within 0.2 pp, stderr ~1.0) and NOT on AIME25 (stderr 6.7-7.8; both headline readings inside one SE); (4) PS-2 is CLOSED - our tree is Reset-PE and the paper's own data mildly favours it; (5) the sizing rule is -c = prefix + W + chunk, never -c = prefix + W. DO NOT CARRY: "W>=16384 matches full attention on LiveCodeBench" (28.74 vs 31.14; first match W=32768; all within single-sample noise on 167 problems); the 11.7x 128K speedup (11.26x - Table 1 printed the vanilla-SWA cell); "W=8192 beats full attention" or "W=2048 loses 6.5 pp" as facts; the "seconds" axis as measured wall-clock; "Prefix Sliding is faster than last-k" (last-k is 1.54x faster in tok/s); the 1.5xW blog constant as justification for 4xW; any memory saving in GB; any speed number as transferable (single H100, vLLM, batch 1024).
Not already_integrated: the sink+window SCAFFOLD is in frozen v9, but the paper's configuration - sink widened to the entire prompt, window 4096-16384, generations 32K-262K - has NEVER been measured here. The one internal measurement (streaming-llm-baseline.md 2026-07-20, artifact streamingllm_qwen17_q8_cpu_sweep_20260720Tlive) used sink 8/16/32 with window 128/192/256 at -c 384 -n 768 - one to two orders of magnitude below the paper's regime on BOTH axes; a regime mismatch, not a refutation, and Figure 12 predicts exactly that null (below the window the method IS full attention). Not new_opportunity: the enabling code exists, so this is a measurement, not a build. Not adopt_patterns yet: adoption should follow an accuracy measurement because Table 1 shows the claim FAILS at W=2048 and Figure 11 shows it fails on code at W<16384. Not not_applicable/superseded: open-source, mechanically supported by our frozen binary, no successor. worth_investigating: a cheap, well-specified, zero-new-code experiment whose payoff on OUR bandwidth-bound CPU decode is genuinely uncertain.

- updated `handoffs/active/streaming-llm-baseline.md`

## intake-1316#record — Context as an Environment: Programmatic Context Management for Long-Horizon Agents (Scroll)

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21690
- **Verification**: dive-overturned
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: context_management, agent_architecture, memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology, rag_alternatives, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Context management should be DEFERRED to query time as a program the model writes, not fixed at observation time: "no summary produced at observation time can be guaranteed to preserve what is later needed"; compression and external memory stores are "fundamentally lossy in the same way" (Section 1).
   - *unaffected*: The deferral thesis and both quoted fragments are verbatim in Section 1 and formalised in Section 2.1. Nothing to correct. This is the one claim the dive found fully intact in both paper and code.
1. The session is an executable Session Environment S_t = (L_t, P_t, V_t): an append-only Event Log in SQLite with immutable monotonic seq addresses; durable payload storage (large payloads externalised, row keeps a bounded preview + recovery pointer); a sandboxed persistent Python kernel holding a typed namespace ACROSS model calls (Sections 2.1-2.2).
   - *unaffected*: Session Environment S_t=(L_t,P_t,V_t), append-only SQLite Event Log with immutable monotonic seq, payload externalisation with bounded preview + recovery pointer, and a persistent typed namespace across model calls are all verbatim in Section 2.2 and all corroborated in the released code (history.py:150, :216-219; ToolResultRef/ArtifactRef lazy handles).
2. exec decides how the environment is accessed/transformed and print ALONE decides what crosses into the next view - tool outputs and retrieved history bind to Python variables instead of being serialised into the prompt (Section 2.3, Table 1: ms.search / ms.expand / Python / print). Search defaults to BM25, deterministic, no index-time model calls (Section 2.2).
   - *narrowed*: The paper says what the entry says, and BM25/no-index-time-model-calls is CONFIRMED in code. But "print ALONE decides what crosses" is FALSE of the released implementation - render_observation assembles seven channels (tool_def.py:107-134), the cell final expression is auto-rendered and printed by the harness by default (exec_server.py:293-295, display="summary"), stderr shares the stdout fd, and tracebacks cross. Adopt "bounded, ENUMERABLE egress channels", never "print is the only egress" - the source does not implement the latter and citing it as a shipped property would be wrong.
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/repl/tool_def.py:107-134 and src/qwenpaw/repl/exec_server.py:293-295 (print-only egress overturned)` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "rendered = render_last_expression(value, display) ... print(rendered) [auto-render of the cell final expression; DEFAULT_DISPLAY = "summary", output_policy.py:15]"
   - anchor `Paper Section 2.3 / Section 1` @ arXiv:2608.21690v1: "Retrieved records, tool outputs, and intermediate computations remain in the kernel unless explicitly emitted through print, which the harness inserts as an observation into the next model context."
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/agents/context/scroll/serialize.py:126-133 (headlines are model-authored, regex-extracted)` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "def extract_headline(text: str | None) -> str | None: / """Return a valid ``[ ... ]`` headline, otherwise ``None``.""""
3. Eviction is RECOVERABLE, not compaction: "eviction changes only the view, never the underlying record". Algorithm 1 triggers at |c| > rho*C, protects the active turn/recent tail/newest tool results, folds completed tool payloads to seq pointers first, then evicts whole spans whose headlines enter a tiered eviction index that stays IN the view; each tier holds at most k blocks, older ones collapse to one line and merge into the next tier; after n evictions the index occupies O(k log_k n) blocks (Section 2.4).
   - *narrowed*: The headline asymptotic result SURVIVES a close code read - k=10, unbounded tier count, one-line collapse with lossless seq spans, Theta(k log_k n) - and this is the single most citable verified mechanism in the paper. Two of the six procedures do NOT survive: SelectSpan evicts to a <=40k tail rather than the overage (manager.py:507), and FoldPayloads runs at three sites, two post-eviction (manager.py:487/591/611), so Algorithm 1 as published is a simplification of the shipped policy rather than a specification of it. Protected is narrower than stated. Additionally the paper omits a third render regime in which the model-visible index collapses to one global seq span with zero anchors under a char budget (eviction_index.py:409) - the failure the tiering exists to prevent.
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/agents/context/scroll/eviction_index.py:31 and :188-193 and :205-206` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "_TIER_CAP = 10 ... if len(self._tiers[k]) < _TIER_CAP: return / self._carry_run(k, len(self._tiers[k]) - 1) ... if k + 1 == len(self._tiers): self._tiers.append([])"
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/agents/context/scroll/manager.py:507 (SelectSpan divergence)` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "reserve = min(40_000, max(requested_reserve, minimum_recent))"
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/agents/context/scroll/eviction_index.py:409 (undisclosed third render regime)` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "===== Archived index (details folded to fit context) ===== / [seq {lo}-{hi}] / Index details omitted; recover this span with recall_history(...)"
   - anchor `Paper Section 2.4, "Headlines as navigation anchors"` @ arXiv:2608.21690v1: "each tier holds at most k blocks; when a tier fills, the newest block retains full detail while the k-1 older ones collapse to one line each and merge into the next tier. After n evictions, the index occupies O(k log_k n) blocks, providing fine anchors for recent history, coarse ranges for distan..."
4. The policy is model-written so it inherits improving coding ability - and the six-backbone sweep shows this cuts both ways: on agentic LOCA the spread across backbones is 64 points at 256K; failures are not protocol-level but weaker models commit more execution errors or terminate prematurely on aggregation-heavy tasks (Section 4.2, Table 4).
   - *narrowed*: THIS IS THE ENTRY'S LOAD-BEARING RELEVANCE CLAIM AND IT IS THE ONE THE DIVE MOST DAMAGES. Table 4's numbers are exact and the paper does assert an identical harness, but the harness is in the PRIVATE AgentZero repo (README.md:17-18, :56-57), so the assertion is unauditable; and on released defaults C is NOT constant across the six backbones - Deepseek-v4-pro and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B are absent from the model catalog and fall back to 131,072 while Qwen3.8-Max/Qwen3.7-Max/GLM-5.2 get 1,000,000, a 7.6x spread in eviction cadence. Two saving graces: no per-backbone prompt/tool-budget/max-steps branching exists anywhere in the released Scroll package, and GLM-5.2 (our reviewer) shares C=1,000,000 with the frontier backbone, so the GLM-5.2 row is NOT confounded by this. The Qwen3.6-35B-A3B row plausibly is, and at LOCA_256K a 131,072 window is a static-catalog fallback of unknown relation to the real window of that backbone [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 (Stage 4, plan C-N): restated - not inadmissible. LOCA ships a 130K-window model at all seven lengths and runs every model at its own maximum window by design, so the defect is the provenance of the fallback, not the size of the budget].
   - anchor `Paper Table 4 caption, Section 4.2` @ arXiv:2608.21690v1: "Scroll across backbones. Only the foundation model changes; harness, tools, prompts, and context-management rules are identical."
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/providers/context_windows.py:44 and :47-86 (the per-backbone C spread that Table 4 does not disclose)` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "DEFAULT_CONTEXT_WINDOW = 128 * 1024 [catalog has qwen3.8-max/qwen3.7-max/glm-5.2 = 1,000,000 and kimi-k2 = 262,144; "deepseek" appears nowhere in the file, so Deepseek-v4-pro and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B fall back to 131,072]"
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/agents/context/scroll/eviction_index.py:42-46 - the ONLY model-name mention in the scroll package, annotating unconditional code` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "A weak model (GLM/DeepSeek) otherwise latches onto a [headline] in the map above and answers it. This banner is a positional delimiter ... telling the model, at the seam, which side is archive and which side is the request."
5. NOT FOUND IN SOURCE: no Limitations section (Section 5 Related Work, Section 6 Conclusion); no latency or dollar cost ("We report token counts rather than latency or dollar cost", Section 4.4); no concrete rho, C or k; no variance, CI, seeds or repetition count.
   - *narrowed*: The absence list is right about the Limitations section, dollar cost, CI, seeds, non-termination counter and rho/C/k-in-the-paper. Four corrections: (a) C IS defined in Section 2.1 as the model's nominal context window, so only rho and k are free; (b) Appendix A.2 asserts "repeated runs" qualitatively, so the paper is not silent on repetition, merely quantitatively empty; (c) Appendix D carries four per-task wall_time_s values (36.62-221.2 s), so latency is not wholly absent though no aggregate is reported; (d) DECISIVELY - rho=0.8, C=context_size (default 131,072), k=10 are all concrete in the released code, so "the eviction algorithm is unparameterised as published" must be restated as "unparameterised as PUBLISHED, fully parameterised as RELEASED".
   - anchor `QwenPaw src/qwenpaw/config/config.py:1012 (rho, absent from the paper)` @ QwenPaw @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f: "compact_threshold_ratio: float = Field(default=0.8, ge=0.1, le=0.9, ...) "Compaction trigger threshold ratio: compaction is triggered when the context length reaches this fraction of max_input_length""
   - anchor `Paper Appendix A.2 (repeated runs, asserted without a count)` @ arXiv:2608.21690v1: "This profile is consistent across our repeated runs: contradiction resolution and knowledge update are among Scroll's strongest categories in every run, multi-session reasoning and summarization among its weakest."
   - anchor `Paper Section 4.4` @ arXiv:2608.21690v1: "Ingestion involves no additional LLM calls, and at query time records are filtered inside the Python kernel, so only printed output enters the context. ... We report token counts rather than latency or dollar cost, as both depend on serving configuration."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive: pinned the previously unpinned companion code (QwenPaw scroll-research @ 3db60c5975187fc7c549e16573567a7cd21fd51f) and read the Scroll package in full, which settles all five would_settle questions and changes four things Stage-1 could not know. (1) THE ALGORITHM IS PARAMETERISED IN CODE even though the paper omits every value: rho=0.8 (config.py:1012), C=agent.model.context_size (default 131,072, context_windows.py:44), k=_TIER_CAP=10 (eviction_index.py:31, hard-coded). Stage-1's "the eviction algorithm is unparameterised as published" must never again be carried as "unparameterised". (2) THE O(k log_k n) BOUND IS REAL - unbounded tier count, per-tier cap 10, one-line collapse with lossless seq spans, pinned by the repo's own test - but TWO of Algorithm 1's six procedures are NOT what the code does: SelectSpan evicts to a <=40,000-token tail rather than the overage (manager.py:507), and FoldPayloads runs at three sites, two of them post-eviction. And an undisclosed third render regime collapses the model-visible index to a single global seq span with zero anchors under a char budget (eviction_index.py:409). (3) PRINT IS NOT THE ONLY EGRESS - seven channels reach the model (tool_def.py:107-134), including auto-rendered final expressions (exec_server.py:293-295, opt-OUT not opt-in), stderr sharing the stdout fd, and tracebacks. One of the four patterns Stage-1 proposed harvesting is therefore not a property the source actually has. (4) THE "IDENTICAL HARNESS" PREMISE UNDER TABLE 4 - this entry's entire relevance argument - IS UNAUDITABLE (harness is in the private AgentZero repo, README.md:17-18/:56-57) and the released defaults give a 7.6x spread in C across the six backbones because Deepseek-v4-pro and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B are absent from the model catalog and fall back to 131,072. Mitigating and important for us: no per-backbone prompt/tool-budget/max-steps branching exists anywhere in the released Scroll package, and GLM-5.2 shares C=1,000,000 with Qwen3.8-Max, so the GLM-5.2 row survives as a prior while the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B row does not. The "w/o index" ablation has NO flag in the release, so the -1.8 is not third-party reproducible. Ingestion IS zero-LLM-call as claimed, verified end to end, with the qualification that headlines are model-AUTHORED (regex-extracted from the assistant's ordinary answer, serialize.py:126-133) so index CONTENT is a backbone-capability variable. Externally: the corpus-size hypothesis about the baselines is REFUTED - Exabase 68.0, Hindsight 64.1 and Cognee 67.0 are all genuine BEAM-10M-tier numbers and +5.1 / +37.4 both reproduce exactly - but the entry's own Cognee protocol quote was a reconstruction and is corrected to the verbatim "outside the spirit of the benchmark", and a NEW backbone confound is recorded (Exabase's 68.0 ran on the deliberately cheaper Gemini 3 Flash). Locally: the cited anchor repl_environment/state.py:313-318 is WRONG (actual :444-476) and variable_lineage does NOT record shape, so the namespace-digest actionable needs new capture work rather than rendering; unified-trace-memory-service's append-only SQLite+FTS5 store is confirmed in git with navigation.py already carrying an ms-shaped read surface that has ZERO consumers; the tool-output-compression A/B row is confirmed open at line 448 but the entry conflates CF-3c with the separate CF-3c masking-anchor row, and the A/B's nominal summarize-and-compact control may not exist as a runnable arm. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1342#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: narrows (claims [4, 5]) - Narrows in two directions and reattributes one number. (1) REATTRIBUTION, the load-bearing one: intake-1316#record's reported_results and its dive both carry Scroll's Table 7 under Scroll's own caption, "taken from the LOCA paper or its public leaderboard". Three rows are indeed the LOCA authors' measurements, but MiniMax M3 + ReAct 49.3 at 256K - the sole comparator behind the abstract's "+37.4" - is in neither artifact and is MiniMax's self-reported product-blog number (Scroll's own reference [20]), labelled "0 verified results and 1 self-reported results" by llm-stats; and Claude Opus 4.7 sits at 57 on that same page, which would make the delta 29.7. (2) SHARPENING of intake-1316#record claim 4 and its "+1.4/tie" reading: LOCA is 15 tasks x 5 seeds = 75 binary samples with NO repetitions, so 86.7 = 65/75 and 85.3 = 64/75 - Scroll's 256K margin over plain CodeAct is exactly one task instance, 0.34 of a binomial standard error, and 128K is a literal tie. (3) NARROWING of intake-1316#record's context-budget confound (claim 4 / the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B row): LOCA runs every model at its OWN maximum window by design (130K-1050K, §3.1), so per-model C is the instrument's protocol and not a Scroll deviation; the real defect is that Scroll's C is a static-catalog value with a 131,072 fallback for uncatalogued backbones. A 131,072 budget IS admissible at LOCA_256K - LOCA ships a 130K-window model at all seven lengths - so "cannot even hold the environment description" must be replaced by "same regime as DeepSeek-V3.2-Thinking, which LOCA measures at 6.7 vs GPT-5.2-Medium's 21.3 at 256K, i.e. roughly 3x". (4) CORROBORATION worth recording against claim 5: intake-1316#record correctly noted LOCA is the one benchmark in Scroll free of the same-family-judge concern; verified - the verifier is 15 hand-written deterministic final-state scripts with no LLM anywhere. But the harness that runs them places the ground truth one relative path from an unrestricted Python subprocess, so LOCA numbers are judge-clean and isolation-unproven, which is a different and unstated caveat.

### Verdict

dive-verified with load-bearing overturns. CARRY: the tiered eviction index is a real, verified, zero-compute mechanism - k=10 blocks per tier, unbounded tier count, each carry keeping the newest block and collapsing the other 9 to one line each with lossless seq spans, giving Theta(k log_k n) resident blocks - and it is genuinely uncovered by this repo (zero hits for "eviction index" outside the intake index). Carry also the design intent it encodes: a resident map of WHAT LEFT the view, because search recovers only what the agent thinks to ask for. Carry the GLM-5.2 row of Table 4 (66.7/62.7 LOCA 128K/256K vs 89.3/86.7) as a prior - GLM-5.2 shares C=1,000,000 with the frontier backbone so that row is not confounded by the context-budget spread. DO NOT CARRY: (a) "+37.4" as anything but a cross-backbone Table 7 delta against MiniMax M3+ReAct's 49.3 [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 (Stage 4, plan C-N): that 49.3 is a VENDOR SELF-REPORT - it is absent from the LOCA paper and from its public leaderboard, llm-stats labels the page 0 verified results and 1 self-reported result, and Claude Opus 4.7 sits at 57 on that same page, which would make the delta 29.7. Never quote "+37.4" without this] - the same-backbone control is a TIE at 128K and +1.4 at 256K; (b) "print is the only egress" as a property of Scroll - the released code has seven egress channels and auto-prints the cell's final expression by default, so adopt bounded ENUMERABLE egress instead; (c) Algorithm 1's SelectSpan or FoldPayloads ordering as a specification - the shipped policy evicts to a <=40k tail and folds at three sites, two of them post-eviction; (d) "the eviction algorithm is unparameterised" - rho=0.8, C=context_size, k=10 are all in the released code; (e) the -1.8 index ablation as a measured cost of eviction indexes in general - no flag exists to re-run that arm, the harness is in a private repo, and the number is unreproduced; (f) the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B row (37.3/22.7) as same-harness evidence about our frontdoor class - on released defaults that backbone falls back to a 131,072-token window against the frontier backbone's 1,000,000, and that fallback is a STATIC-CATALOG value of unknown relation to the real window of that backbone [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 (Stage 4, plan C-N): restated - not an inadmissible budget. LOCA runs every model at its OWN maximum window by design, and 131,072 IS admissible at LOCA_256K (same regime as DeepSeek-V3.2-Thinking, which LOCA measures at 6.7 against GPT-5.2-Medium 21.3 at 256K); the defect is the provenance of the fallback, not the size of the budget]; (g) Cognee's exclusion of agentic retrieval as a one-shot-vs-iterative PROTOCOL argument - the verbatim reason is the vaguer "outside the spirit of the benchmark", and BEAM itself neither permits nor forbids iteration, so state the asymmetry as unequalized-and-undisclosed, never as a violation; (h) locally, repl_environment/state.py:313-318 as the variable_lineage anchor - it is :444-476 and it does not record shape. Cite arXiv 2608.21690 and the pinned SHA 3db60c59, never the title.
Not already_integrated: we own the Event Log substrate and a persistent REPL kernel but have NO eviction index, no print-as-only-egress discipline, and our compaction is still summarize-and-compact. Not new_opportunity as a system: the released code is a branch of QwenPaw, a whole agent OS - adopting it means adopting a competing harness, and the same-backbone margin (+1.4) does not justify it. Not superseded / not not_applicable (Apache-2.0, self-hostable, in-domain). adopt_patterns: harvest four separable zero-compute patterns - tiered eviction index, namespace digest, print-as-the-only-egress, payload-folding-before-span-eviction - into existing handoffs, and use Table 4 as a prior for the already-filed first-party A/B.

## intake-1317#record — WikiSkill: Compiling Agent Experience into Persistent Knowledge for Skill Evolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27454
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, knowledge_management, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. A persistent, never-rolled-back knowledge layer (the wiki) interposed between raw execution traces and executable skills improves skill evolution: WikiSkill has the highest average for all five inference models in Table 1, beating the strongest competing method by 3.3 / 5.1 / 10.0 / 5.8 / 12.0 points (Qwen-3.5-4B, Qwen-3.5-9B, Qwen-3.6-27B, Gemma-4-31B, Gemini-3.5-Flash; Section 4.2.1).
   - *unaffected*: All 25 Table 1 average cells and the five deltas re-extracted and matched. Carry the Gemini/ALFWorld confound with any Gemini average: 85.9 is identical across all five methods (100% validation pre-evolution), so one fifth of every Gemini row is a constant.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2.1` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "Compared with the strongest competing skill-evolution method for each model, WikiSkill improves average performance by 3.3, 5.1, 10.0, 5.8, and 12.0 points for Qwen-3.5-4B, Qwen-3.5-9B, Qwen-3.6-27B, Gemma-4-31B, and Gemini-3.5-Flash, respectively."
1. ASYMMETRIC wiki access is load-bearing and two-sided: giving the Skill Proposer wiki access lifts the 4-benchmark average 48.7 -> 63.7 (+15.0); giving the Inference Agent wiki access DURING TRAINING ROLLOUTS degrades it 63.7 -> 60.9 (-2.8; -7.8 on LiveMath). Hypothesis (Section 5.1): the executor sources task knowledge from the wiki instead of the skills, so its trajectories become less informative for skill development.
   - *narrowed*: Numbers and signs confirmed verbatim. Narrowed twice: (i) the +15.0 arm ablates the WHOLE wiki layer (Table 3 caption), not a read permission - only the -2.8 arm is a clean single-variable toggle; (ii) the paper never contrasts a STRUCTURED wiki against a FLAT outcome history. Strengthened: the executor penalty reproduces with the opposite proposer setting (48.7 -> 45.3, -3.4) and Sec 3.1 states the rule as a design rule.
   - anchor `Sec 5.1, para "Persistent wiki knowledge dramatically improves skill evolution"` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "As shown in Table 3, when wiki access for the Inference Agent is disabled, providing the Skill Proposer with access to the persistent wiki increases average benchmark performance from 48.7% to 63.7% (+15.0%), with substantial gains on LiveMath (51.3% to 72.6%) and SpreadsheetBench (49.9% to 76.6%)."
   - anchor `Sec 5.1, para "Wiki access for the Inference Agent during evolution degrades final skill quality"` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "When the Skill Proposer has access to the persistent wiki, providing the Inference Agent with wiki access during training rollouts reduces average benchmark performance from 63.7% to 60.9%, with a substantial drop on LiveMath from 72.6% to 64.8%."
   - anchor `Table 3 caption` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "When the Skill Proposer has no Wiki access, we also remove the Wiki Maintainer, eliminating persistent knowledge accumulation across iterations. The bottom row represents our default WikiSkill configuration."
   - anchor `Table 3, HTML rows S5.T3.3.5-S5.T3.3.8, config marks from fa-check/fa-times span classes` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "(InfAgent check, Proposer times) 43.8/42.0/44.4/51.0 -> 45.3 ; (times, times) 51.3/38.4/49.9/55.2 -> 48.7 ; (check, check) 64.8/42.8/80.2/55.6 -> 60.9 ; (times, check) 72.6/44.7/76.6/60.7 -> 63.7 [default] ; No skill 33.0/29.4/50.5/48.6 -> 40.4"
   - anchor `Sec 3.1` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "During the training rollouts, the Inference Agent is restricted from accessing the Wiki Layer, as our ablation study (Sec 5.1) shows that allowing wiki access during training negatively affects skill development."
2. Skill evolution complements model scaling: within the Qwen family the gain grows with scale (+12.3 at 4B, +17.5 at 9B, +23.9 at 27B), and a smaller model with skills beats a larger one without (Qwen-3.5-9B + WikiSkill 47.4 vs Qwen-3.6-27B no-skill 39.4).
   - *unaffected*: Verbatim. Within-family, three points, no variance; Gemma-4-31B (+13.6) breaks monotonicity vs Qwen-3.6-27B (+23.9) at similar scale - an observation, not a scaling law.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2.1` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "the average improvement from WikiSkill increases with model scale, from +12.3 points for Qwen-3.5-4B to +17.5 points for Qwen-3.5-9B and +23.9 points for Qwen-3.6-27B... Qwen-3.5-9B with WikiSkill reaches 47.4% average accuracy, outperforming Qwen-3.6-27B without skills at 39.4%"
3. Skill DISCOVERY and EXECUTION are separable: transferred skills frequently beat self-evolved ones (Qwen-3.6-27B skills take Qwen-3.5-9B to 50.5 on SpreadSheet vs 33.6 self-evolved), and transfer works small->large (Qwen-3.5-4B skills take Gemma-4-31B to 73.1 on LiveMath vs 56.7 self-evolved) (Section 4.2.2, Table 2).
   - *unaffected*: Both transfer figures exact. Sharper same-table cell: Qwen-3.5-9B SpreadSheet 50.5 from Qwen-3.6-27B skills vs 33.6 self-evolved vs 24.3 none - the transferred skill delivers roughly twice the self-evolved gain.
   - anchor `Appendix C, Statistical significance testing` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "In each bootstrap iteration, task instances are sampled with replacement from the test split D_test to construct a bootstrap evaluation set of size |D_test|, from which candidate accuracy scores and pairwise performance margins are computed."
   - anchor `Limitations` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "the Wiki Layer continuously accumulates pattern pages, evolution logs, and proposal diffs across iterations, but WikiSkill currently lacks an automated mechanism to prune the wiki. Such pruning may become necessary as knowledge accumulates over longer evolution runs."
4. Negative transfer is mechanistic: Qwen-3.5-4B SpreadSheet skills DROP Gemini-3.5-Flash 50.5 -> 18.1 because the small model encodes low-level workarounds that block a stronger model from writing end-to-end scripts, and fragmented diagnostics exhaust its interaction budget.
   - *unaffected*: 50.5 -> 18.1 exact with both mechanisms verbatim; the most transferable finding for cross-model artifact reuse: workarounds are model-specific and can be catastrophically negative (-32.4), procedures generalise (same source lifts Gemma LiveMath 33.9 -> 73.1).
   - anchor `Sec 4.2.2` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "Qwen-3.5-4B skills encode low-level workarounds, such as single-line Python commands and string-conversion rules, which help the smaller model avoid execution failures but constrain stronger models such as Gemini-3.5-Flash from using comprehensive end-to-end scripts. Second, fragmented diagnostic..."
5. Optimizer cost is O(1) in training-set size: (1 + T_ReAct) * N_train/B optimizer calls per iteration, B = N_train throughout, T_ReAct ~10-20 (Appendix D.2, Table 7, Eq. 5).
   - *narrowed*: Formula, T_ReAct 10-20, B=N_train and O(1) verbatim. NARROWED: O(1) in N_train only, and the constant is LARGE - 11 to 21 optimizer calls per iteration vs EvoSkill 2N/B; at our train-set sizes (Table 6: 16-80 tasks) WikiSkill is the MORE expensive optimizer per iteration in absolute calls; the paper concedes "may incur higher inference cost". Record as constant-but-large, never as cheap.
   - anchor `Appendix D.2, after Eq. (5)` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "Because T_ReAct does not depend on N_train, WikiSkill's optimizer API call complexity is O(1) with respect to training set size... While this constant call complexity may incur higher inference cost on some datasets, the additional computation is accompanied by consistent performance gains."
   - anchor `Sec 3.2.4` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "the outer-loop orchestration harness programmatically appends an entry to wiki/skill-impact.md ... recording the proposal metadata, target skill name, unified diff of the modification, validation score R(T_val,k), and final acceptance outcome a_k in {Accepted, Rejected} ... providing an objective..."
   - anchor `Appendix E.3, Skill Proposer system prompt, Workflow step 2 and Rule 1` @ arXiv:2608.27454v1: "Read wiki/skill-impact.md to see what was tried before (includes full content of rejected proposals -- DO NOT repeat rejected approaches) ... Read the wiki FIRST -- don't propose something that was already tried and rejected. skill-impact.md contains full content of rejected proposals."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive: read the SAME artifact Stage 1 read (sha256 829f38f5cb66) plus Appendix E in full. All six key_claims CONFIRMED - none overturned - so verification flips to dive-verified and Table 1/2/3/4 figures become citable. THREE numeric/labelling corrections applied in place: (1) reported_results Table 3 had the 45.3 and 48.7 CONFIGURATION LABELS SWAPPED - truth is (InfAgent Yes, Proposer No)=45.3 and (No,No)=48.7 (recovered from fa-check/fa-times span classes a tag-strip drops); this makes 48.7->63.7 a clean ONE-VARIABLE comparison and adds a second same-signed executor-access penalty (48.7->45.3, -3.4); (2) Table 4 max skill length is 128.6 (Qwen-3.5-9B), not 126.2; (3) credibility_justification "github.com grep returns zero" was false (4 hits, one the Karpathy gist already at intake_index.yaml:9713) though its conclusion holds. TWO material NARROWINGS: the +15.0 arm ablates the WHOLE WIKI LAYER (Table 3 caption), not a read permission; and the paper NEVER contrasts structured knowledge against a flat outcome history, so it cannot be cited as evidence that our compiled wiki beats a flat log. O(1) narrowed to constant-but-LARGE (11-21 optimizer calls/iteration, more absolute calls than EvoSkill at our 16-80-task splits). All three dive questions RESOLVED AS ABSENCES with the search domain named: optimizer model NOT FOUND IN SOURCE (Sec 4.1 scopes its list to inference; Appendix C sampling+bootstrap only; Appendix E names no model in any prompt family; ZERO footnotes); K never assigned (Table 5 Iter 5-7 axis is 0-indexed against Algorithm 1 k=1..K; early stopping makes effective K model-dependent); no variance of any kind (3 runs averaged BEFORE a bootstrap that resamples TASKS). Table 2 "self-evolved" = inference model == skill SOURCE, silent on optimizer, so intake-626#record 56-74% target-matched penalty keeps port cost OPEN. LOCAL claims all confirmed, two sharpened: skill_efficacy.py gate is per-SUITE not per-TASK (would not catch intake-1321#record pattern inside a suite); the autopilot retains NO proposer-readable rejected-mutation corpus (prompt_forge._build_mutation_prompt takes no history parameter; rejections survive only as a decision string skipped when no diversity-coverage payload is in state plus an apply/revert commit pair; StrategyStore excludes non-ok outcomes). intake-1321#record per-task objection CANNOT be checked against WikiSkill from any artifact it provides. Adversarial: no replication or rebuttal in a bounded 4-operation search (provisional); three unaffiliated GitHub reimplementations (kenhuangus, poweredbyGEN, ashutoshsinghpr7) report no reproduced numbers and name no optimizer model - verdict stays adopt_patterns. Novelty narrowed high -> medium-high: pattern (b) partly anticipated by SkillOpt rejected-edit buffer (wiki/autonomous-research.md:725); what survives is HARNESS-written, score+diff-carrying, full-content, first-class proposer input. credibility_score 3 unchanged.

### Verdict

DIVE-VERIFIED - Table 1/2/3/4 figures are citable as read. Cite the asymmetric-access result in this corrected form only: from a both-arms-off baseline of 48.7, giving the SKILL PROPOSER the wiki lifts the Gemini 4-benchmark average to 63.7 (+15.0), and giving the INFERENCE AGENT the wiki during training rollouts costs points in BOTH pairings (63.7 -> 60.9 and 48.7 -> 45.3). Do NOT carry the original Table 3 labels (45.3 is InfAgent-Yes/Proposer-No; 48.7 is No/No). Do NOT cite +15.0 as evidence that a STRUCTURED wiki beats a FLAT outcome log - the paper never runs that contrast. Do NOT lift any pattern without the validation gate (acceptance 1.2-1.6 of 2.3-4.8 proposed creations; the gate, not generation, separates this from the SkillsBench net-negative). Do NOT claim self-hostability, cost or a same-model optimizer - optimizer identity is NOT FOUND IN SOURCE; treat it as a parameter to choose and measure (intake-626#record: target-matched recovers 56-74%). Do NOT cite O(1) optimizer cost as cheap (11-21 calls/iteration). Do NOT treat any delta as run-reproducible (3 runs averaged before a TASK-resampling bootstrap; no per-task figures, so intake-1321#record cannot be tested against this paper). Act on the one thing settled locally: PromptForge reads NO rejected-mutation history (prompt_forge._build_mutation_prompt has no history parameter; rejections persist only as a decision string and an apply/revert commit pair; StrategyStore excludes failed outcomes) - the harness-written rejected-proposal trail is a real zero-compute gap.
Not adopt_component - no code, no released skills, no artifact. Not new_opportunity - the repo already owns the skill-evolution program (EV-10, K-SKILL-1, PromptForge/AP-35) and the LLM-Wiki pattern. Not already_integrated - the asymmetric-access rule and a harness-written rejected-proposal audit trail are in no handoff searched. Not superseded/not_applicable (open methodology; four of five inference models open-weight on vLLM; a llama.cpp re-host is a normal port). Three zero-inference patterns: (a) asymmetric knowledge access - the artifact-AUTHORING path reads accumulated knowledge, the execution path under measurement does not; (b) a HARNESS-written audit trail of rejected proposals with diffs and validation scores so the proposer cannot re-propose a known failure; (c) the proposer as a pull-based ReAct reader over an index rather than a push-fed trace bundle.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- created `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/agent-file-prose-compression.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/autopilot-continuous-optimization.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/eval-tower-verification.md`

## intake-1318#record — FreeToken: Efficient Edge-Native MoE Serving with Bandwidth-Adaptive Execution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16157
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: moe_optimization, local_inference, inference_serving, hardware_optimization, kv_cache, context_management, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Bandwidth-adaptive execution: at each decode step the m missing experts are split in closed form between PCIe cache-fill and in-place CPU execution using two profiled bandwidths - B_P (pinned host-to-device expert transfer) and B_H (effective bandwidth of the CPU-side MoE expert kernel). DMA and CPU execution share the host memory subsystem, so residual host bandwidth is B_R = max(B_H - B_P, 0), and balancing the two branches gives q* ~ m * B_P / B_H (Section 3.2, Eq. 2-4). At least one fill is always retained; output is exact (partial sums merge with no approximation).
   - *narrowed*: Every element confirmed verbatim, but the mechanism's VALUE is narrowed decisively: substituting Eq 4 into Eq 3 gives T*(q*) = mS/B_H, identical to pure-CPU miss execution. The split is latency-neutral at its own optimum on every machine including ours; its benefit is free cache warming only. Also flag that Eq 3 divides by the unclamped (B_H - B_P) while Eq 2 clamps B_R at 0. Do NOT carry "bandwidth-adaptive execution gives 1.5-2.3x" — the paper never claims the split alone does, and its algebra says it cannot.
   - anchor `§3.2, Eq 2-4` @ arXiv:2608.16157v1: "The optimal split ratio is derived from a residual-bandwidth argument. Let S denote the size (in bytes) of one complete expert. Since both expert DMA transfers and CPU execution read from the same host-memory subsystem, a saturated PCIe transfer leaves a residual bandwidth of: B_R = max(B_H - B_P..."
   - anchor `§3.2, Eq 4` @ arXiv:2608.16157v1: "q/(m-q) ~= B_P/(B_H - B_P), q* ~= m B_P/B_H"
   - anchor `§3.2, after Eq 4` @ arXiv:2608.16157v1: "It always retains at least one fill, so the cache continues warming even when the CPU handles most misses. The bandwidth parameters (B_H and B_P) are empirically profiled on the target hardware at deployment."
1. Semantic-aware expert caching: a single global LRU slot pool shared by all MoE layers and both phases, keyed on a flattened layer-expert id, exploiting SHORT-RANGE inter-token routing locality rather than global routing skew; positioned against static/prefill-time placement (llama.cpp, KTransformers) (Section 3.2).
   - *unaffected*: Confirmed exactly as written, including the flattened lE+e keying (verified independently in §4.2 and in expert_banks.py) and the shared-across-both-phases slot pool. The short-range-locality warrant is an external citation to arXiv 2505.16056 (= sibling entry intake-1328#record), not a FreeToken measurement — Figure 4b measures the CONSEQUENCE (miss rate) on their traces, not the locality itself.
   - anchor `§4.2, Expert banks and the FTW format` @ arXiv:2608.16157v1: "Each bank uses the flattened layer-expert identifier lE+e as its leading dimension. Rows with the same identifier across all banks collectively form one complete expert. As a result, GPU kernels and the CPU executor share one logical expert identity, regardless of the underlying physical format."
2. Semantic-anchor recurrent-state checkpoints: for hybrid-attention models (gated DeltaNet in Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, Kimi Delta Attention), recurrent state cannot be partially reused, so the few state checkpoints are pinned at agent-harness special-token boundaries (thinking segments, tool calls, tool outputs, turns) - the positions OpenClaw, OpenCode and SWE-agent edit - so a context edit re-prefills only the new suffix (Section 3.1).
   - *unaffected*: Confirmed verbatim including the three named harnesses and the two named recurrent architectures. Its APPLICABILITY here is separately killed — see the (d) answer — but the claim as recorded is accurate about the paper.
   - anchor `§3.1, Semantic anchors` @ arXiv:2608.16157v1: "FreeToken spends this budget at semantic anchors: the special-token boundaries that mark thinking segments, tool calls and outputs, and conversation turns. These are exactly the positions where agent frameworks modify and truncate context"
3. Full-layer prefill double buffering: stream layer i+1's complete expert set while the GPU computes layer i, from the same global slot pool (Section 3.1).
   - *unaffected*: Confirmed verbatim, including the "same global slot pool" and "no separate prefill cache and no phase handoff" detail, plus the unrecorded fallback: "When the slot pool cannot spare two full layers, FreeToken falls back to on-demand prefill loading rather than oversubscribing GPU memory."
4. Elastic edge memory: the GPU expert cache can be resized at scheduler safe points without restart because the CPU-resident pool is the source of truth; startup loads experts from disk into their final host layout (FTW format, parallel direct I/O) and pins only after filling (Sections 3.3, 4.2).
   - *unaffected*: Confirmed in the paper AND independently in code at the pinned SHA (ftw.py, host_banks.py). The code strengthens it: pin-after-fill is quantified in-tree (~47 s of redundant zero-fill avoided for a ~137 GiB DSV4 bank).
5. All routing-dependent control is device-resident inside a statically captured CUDA Graph via fixed-shape buffers and device-side valid counts; the paper claims llama.cpp cannot maintain graph execution in hybrid mode and that host-side heuristic schedulers (HybriMoE, SMoE) are too expensive to capture (Sections 4.1, 6).
   - *narrowed*: The paper says what the entry says it says. But the entry's downstream INFERENCE — that CUDA-Graph residency is what makes a gfx90a port a rewrite — is refuted: graph.py uses only the generic torch.cuda.CUDAGraph API (= HIP graphs on ROCm torch) and a third party has graphs working on RDNA4 including the MoE path. The real gate is untuned kernels on a different ISA family (CDNA2 vs the RDNA3/4 all community work targets).
   - anchor `§6, Expert offloading and caching` @ arXiv:2608.16157v1: "recomputed by host-side heuristics whose scheduling cost and per-layer synchronization cannot be captured into a CUDA Graph; llama.cpp likewise cannot maintain graph execution in its hybrid mode."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive. (1) OVERTURNED the entry''s central relevance premise. Using the MEASURED CPU MoE expert-kernel bandwidth B_H = 61.8 GB/s (not the 425/460 peak, both of which are retired or theoretical), q*/m = 28.89/61.8 = 0.467 — NOT "at or below 0.29". Our host is mid-pack in Table 1, between the 4090 (0.397) and 3090 (0.446) rented servers, and the bound at the machine''s measured 152.6 GB/s read ceiling is still >= 0.19. FreeToken''s own policy would therefore route ~47% of misses over PCIe, i.e. our static -ot exps=CPU placement is NOT what the policy recommends. (2) NEW LOAD-BEARING FINDING, in neither the paper nor the Zhou review: substituting Eq 4 into Eq 3 gives T*(q*) = mS/B_H, exactly the pure-CPU time. The q* split is LATENCY-NEUTRAL at its own optimum on every machine in Table 1 and on ours (20.97 ms either way); its entire value is free cache warming. So none of the 1.5-2.3x is attributable to the split, and any sub-linearity in the unmeasured additivity assumption makes the split strictly worse than pure CPU. (3) BOUNDED THE WHOLE LINE OF WORK ON OUR HOST: the expert path is 20.96 ms of a 99.1 ms token, so a perfect expert-cache hit rate is worth at most 10.09 -> 12.80 tok/s = +27% on qwen4exp; and streaming all 1.296 GB/token over our 28.89 GB/s link would cost 44.9 ms, 2.14x slower than executing it on CPU. (4) PORTABILITY IS BETTER THAN THE ENTRY ASSUMED, PAYOFF WORSE: the device-side LRU is not in the repo at all but in flashlib==0.3.0, a py3-none-any Apache-2.0 wheel whose slot_cache is 441 lines of Triton with zero NVIDIA intrinsics; graph.py uses only the generic torch.cuda.CUDAGraph API; the CPU executor is already AVX-512-BF16/VNNI (native on Zen5) with a 22-line CUDA surface (cudaLaunchHostFunc + cuStreamWriteValue, both with HIP equivalents); FTW is pure POSIX O_DIRECT. A third party has the whole pipeline INCLUDING graphs and MoE running on RDNA4 (issue #122) via ctypes shims in pinned.py. It is a port. BUT that same independent test measures FreeToken MoE decode at 61 tok/s vs llama.cpp ROCm 83.5 and Vulkan 88.8 on the same machine and GGUF — a 0.73x LOSS — and the offload/q* path has never been benchmarked on any AMD GPU at all. [SUPERSEDED 2026-09-07 by intake-1343#record: measured 2026-08-26 in the gfx1200 fork README - Gemma-4-26B-A4B QAT Q4_0 offload 62-73 tok/s vs llama.cpp 84.8 all-VRAM / 15.0 --n-cpu-moe; FreeToken still loses to fully-resident llama.cpp in every AMD configuration; issue #350 shows --moe-backend cpu/hybrid are SILENTLY WRONG under HIP-graph replay on ROCm (rel err 1.36); the binding gate on any gfx90a port is the ROCm/torch version floor (torch>=2.11,<2.12) against our ROCm 6.2; both pinned.py bugs are FORK-introduced, not upstream.] (5) SEMANTIC-ANCHOR PRECONDITION FAILS HERE, THREE WAYS: our backend collapses a whole session into ONE user message and strips chat special tokens (two special tokens per session, so there are no anchors); production compaction cuts at int(len*0.8) characters; and it replaces the PREFIX, discarding 100% of recurrent state by construction. The one boundary-aligned module we own is off in dev and prod. Zhou''s untested "non-aligned harness" is us. (6) ROCm PR set re-verified 2026-09-07: 8/8 open, none merged, #134/#136 draft, targets gfx1100-1103/gfx1200-1201/gfx1150/gfx1151; repo-wide search for gfx90a, MI210, CDNA, MI300 returns zero hits. (7) Zhou review re-read: additivity is presented as a DERIVATION ("Since ... a saturated PCIe transfer leaves"), never measured, never ablated; only the two INPUTS B_H and B_P are measured. Zhou''s objection confirmed verbatim. (8) Minor: GLM-5.2 is ~239 GB not ~238; the wiki 50-100 ms checkpoint figure was quoted stripped of its owning finding (checkpoints are ~5% of GDN speculation cost; sequential verification is ~90%). Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): dive-surfaced sources DECLINED by the operator - flashlib slot_cache (Apache-2.0 Triton LRU; only relevant if a port is revisited) and Zhu et al. 2026 (Codex 33 tok/s production median; bibliography entry unresolved). FreeToken issue #122 + ROCm forks SELECTED for Stage-2b. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1343#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: narrows (claims [5]) - Three specific edits, one of which reverses a standing negative. (1) intake-1318#record's key_claim 5 correction says the paper's "graph residency makes hybrid MoE non-portable" position "does not survive", resting on issue #122's "CUDA graphs work on gfx1200 for everything tested" plus the generic torch.cuda.CUDAGraph API. That refutation must be NARROWED, not withdrawn: FreeToken issue #350 (2026-09-02, gfx1201, Linux, ROCm 7.14) shows --moe-backend cpu and hybrid produce rel err 1.36 under HIP-graph replay with no exception, because the cuStreamWriteValue/WaitValue probe returns False on ROCm and the cudaLaunchHostFunc host-node fallback is not capture-safe - which is exactly the 22-line CUDA surface intake-1318#record's would_settle (b) point (4) called a free port on the strength of hipStreamWriteValue32/hipLaunchHostFunc existing. The APIs exist; the runtime refuses them; the fallback is silently wrong. PR #316 adds a HIP-capture fault in the offload fused copy, and PR #260's ROCm MVP disables graph capture outright. Graph capture works on gfx1200 for dense and fused-MoE; it is not a general ROCm graph-safety result. (2) OVERTURN the stale AMD negative, which is in intake-1318#record's adversarial_pass and reader_should_conclude rather than in a numbered key_claim: "the offload/q* path has never been benchmarked on any AMD GPU" and "the single measured AMD MoE datapoint is a loss". The offload path WAS measured on real AMD hardware, twice - Gemma-4-26B-A4B QAT Q4_0 at 62-73 tok/s in the fork README at a338f49b (2026-08-26, two days after the issue intake-1318#record read), and gpt-oss-20b at 16.1-23.1 tok/s on gfx1201 in issue #82. The direction of the conclusion is UNCHANGED and the decline is HARDENED: the 4x advantage is over a 6-core dual-channel llama.cpp baseline our host beats by 3.2x on the same model family, FreeToken loses to fully-resident llama.cpp in every AMD configuration ever measured, and the binding gate turns out to be a ROCm major-version floor (torch>=2.11,<2.12, triton==3.6.0, all reporters on ROCm 7.14/10.1) against our deliberate ROCm 6.2, not the "untuned kernels on a different ISA family" intake-1318#record named. (3) MINOR BUT LOAD-BEARING FOR ANY PORT ESTIMATE: intake-1318#record describes the pinned.py defects as FreeToken's. They are the community port's - upstream raises ImportError, the fork replaced it with "return None". Nothing about intake-1318#record's verdict moves; adopt_patterns stands on both entries.

### Verdict

Treat this as a verified paper whose portable content is ONE methodology, not a serving engine and not a speedup. Concretely: (1) CITE the q*/m = 0.467 result as the settled first-party arithmetic — B_P 28.89 GB/s and B_H 61.8 GB/s, both measured — and cite the identity T*(q*) = mS/B_H alongside it, because without the identity the ratio invites the wrong action. The split is latency-neutral; do not build it. (2) DO carry the +27% Amdahl ceiling (expert path = 20.96 ms of a 99.1 ms token on qwen4exp) as the bound on any GPU-expert-cache work on this host, and the 44.9 ms cost of streaming a token''s experts over our link as the reason pure-fill offload is a non-starter here. (3) DO run the Figure 4b methodology offline on our existing production routing traces — global LRU vs prefill-updated vs static at 64 GB-matched capacity — but scope it to the LARGE-model regime (GLM-5.2 UD-IQ2 at ~239 GB gives 27% capacity, close to their 37% point); it is pointless on qwen4exp, whose routed pool is roughly the size of the MI210 itself. That experiment, and only that experiment, adjudicates our near-uniform-global-skew kill against FreeToken''s short-range-locality claim, and adjudicates intake-1191#record''s heat-caching-lost counter-measurement. (4) DO NOT cite the 1.5-2.3x, the 1.3-2.1x cross-hardware ladder, or the 14.9 tok/s GLM-5.2 figure as anything transferable: they are NVIDIA-only, bundle two never-ablated mechanisms, ride on unstated llama.cpp baseline configs, and the single independent AMD MoE head-to-head has FreeToken LOSING at 0.73x. (5) DO NOT open semantic-anchor checkpointing as a task — our harness renders a whole session as one user message with chat special tokens stripped, compacts at arbitrary character offsets, and replaces the prefix rather than the suffix, so the mechanism is currently inexpressible, not merely unhelpful. (6) DO NOT read the gfx90a absence as "not portable": the slot pool is Apache-2.0 Triton, the graph API is generic torch, the CPU executor is already AVX-512-BF16, and RDNA4 works today — it is a port nobody should pay for at the current expected payoff. (7) DO NOT read any of this as reopening hot-expert offload; that stays closed until (3) says otherwise.
adopt_patterns, not adopt_component: the runtime is unreachable today - README states NVIDIA RTX 30/40/50 native only; an issue/PR search at head af71ba43 (2026-09-07) found 56 ROCm items, every ROCm PR examined (#23, #132, #134, #136, #137, #217, #241, #260) OPEN and targeting RDNA3/RDNA4 (gfx1100-1103, gfx1150/1151, Strix Halo), none gfx90a/CDNA2, none merged (absence scoped to the searched head; code-search API refused without auth). NOT not_applicable: in-domain, Apache-2.0, self-hostable, and three mechanisms are portable DESIGN, not CUDA artifacts - the q* split is arithmetic over two numbers we can measure; semantic-anchor checkpointing is a harness-side placement policy; the Figure 4b trace-replay methodology is offline analysis. NOT superseded; NOT already_integrated - our stack sits at static -ncmoe placement, exactly the policy Figure 4b measures as worst. A dive decides whether adopt_component ever becomes reachable (FTW/slot-pool portability to HIP on gfx90a vs CUDA-Graph residency making it a rewrite).
