## intake-1320#record — JIT-Agent: Scaling Harness Intelligence via Just-in-Time Harness Evolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25593
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, cost_aware_routing, benchmark_methodology, training_distillation, context_management, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. The harness - memory, planning, action protocol, capability/tool orchestration - is a first-order determinant of agent capability; agent intelligence is a property of the model-harness pair (Sections 1, 3.1).
   - *unaffected*: Re-read verbatim; the one part of the entry with independent external corroboration (Harness-Bench, Terminal-Bench 2.0, CORE-Bench, intake-753#record).
1. A harness factors into a fixed four-module protocol h = (M, P, A, F) with runtime order M->P->F->A over a frozen backbone, turning harness construction into generation of structured executable modules (Section 3.1, Eqs. 2-3).
   - *unaffected*: Factorisation and M->P->F->A order verbatim. The release only exercises 11 of the 13 coordinate points, and the two omitted are the range endpoints.
   - anchor `Section 3.1, prose following Eq. 3` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "M constructs a view of realized history, P forms a local directive, F activates relevant capabilities, and A updates the controller state and emits the next action."
2. Harness synthesis can be amortised into a trained generator: JIT-Agent-27B (trained on Qwen3.6-27B) emits a task-specific harness just in time, vs ahead-of-time approaches that precompile one durable artifact (Section 1; Appendix A Table 4).
   - *narrowed*: THE RELEASED WEIGHTS ARE NOT THE PAPER'S MODEL: the model card says jit-27b is a Stage-I model distilled from the final checkpoint, with zero published numbers of its own; a local run cannot reproduce, confirm or refute Table 2/3. The most consequential change to the entry's practical value.
   - anchor `huggingface.co/JIT-Agent/jit-27b model card, Checkpoint Overview` @ HF model card retrieved 2026-09-07: "This repository contains the initial research release of JIT-Agent-27B. The checkpoint builds on the Stage-I harness-customization model and is further trained through distillation from the final research checkpoint."
   - anchor `v2 end matter, Contributors block (NEW IN v2, absent from v1)` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "Affiliations: National University of Singapore, EverMind AI, Nanyang Technological University"
3. Three-stage training: Stage I teacher-distilled SFT plus DPO over pairs admitted only when reward strictly improves and neither latency nor cost degrades (Eq. 4); Stage II imitation of repair patches executable within at most two rounds (Eq. 5); Stage III Evo-GDPO normalising reward/latency/cost advantages independently with w_rew > w_lat + w_cost, retaining a candidate only if it matches or exceeds the reward frontier AND strictly improves one frontier dimension (Section 4.1, Eqs. 6-7).
   - *narrowed*: Eq. 4 has FOUR conjuncts - the entry dropped "at least one efficiency gain is strict". JIT therefore has two admission rules of different shape (Eq. 4 training-pair gate; Stage-III bank retention), and Eq. 6 gates the efficiency channels behind I[r >= b_r]. Everything else in the claim stands.
   - anchor `Section 4.1, Stage I, Eq. 4 and its introducing sentence` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "A preference is retained only when reward improves, neither efficiency axis degrades, and at least one efficiency gain is strict: h+ > h- <=> r+ > r- AND l+ <= l- AND k+ <= k- AND (l+ < l- OR k+ < k-)"
   - anchor `Section 4.1, Stage III, bank retention` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "a candidate is retained only if it matches or exceeds the current reward frontier and then strictly improves at least one frontier dimension - either reward itself, latency, or cost."
4. Two inference modes: static (generate N harnesses in parallel, select one, execute it) and streaming (retrieve from an evolving bank, execute once, update the bank) - harnesses keep improving at test time with frozen generator weights (Section 4.2, Eq. 8). N is NEVER given a value.
   - *narrowed*: Confirmed - the paper gives N no value. RESOLVED from outside: N=3, T=1.0, logprob selector (repo + card); CLI default auto silently degrades to judge. Carry N=3 as a REPO ASSERTION, never a paper statement.
   - anchor `Section 4.2, Static inference` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "JIT-Agent generates N harnesses in parallel, selects one of them, and executes only the selected harness. This increases candidate diversity without increasing the number of environment rollouts."
   - anchor `scripts/eval/metrics.py:68-74 and :102-105` @ github.com/bingreeky/JIT @ ababa06c2f54d799fd9fbc356e5368f61a452260: "return records[-1] if records else {} ... "input_token_count": int(last.get("input_token_count", 0) or 0), "output_token_count": ..., "total_token_count": ..."
   - anchor `jit/README.md:213-222 (Output tree)` @ github.com/bingreeky/JIT @ ababa06c2f54: "generate/ ... report.json prompts, responses, token counts ... execute/ ... scores.jsonl, summary.json the numbers you report"
   - anchor `scripts/run_jit.py:303-306 and :339; jit/meta_agent.py:1458-1479` @ github.com/bingreeky/JIT @ ababa06c2f54: "rollouts=1, ... "result": exec_summary / generation_events = []"
   - anchor `scripts/run_jit.sh:6-7` @ github.com/bingreeky/JIT @ ababa06c2f54: "Everything is overridable by environment variable; the defaults reproduce the published setup (3 harnesses per case at temperature 1, model-selected)."
   - anchor `Section 5.3, Table 3 caption` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "For each fixed backbone, we report task performance (Perf.), average token consumption per case in thousands (#Tokens (K)), and API cost per case in USD."
5. Claimed first: "the first model purpose-built for just-in-time harness generation" (Abstract) - bounded by the paper's own Appendix A Table 4, which credits Harness-R1 (2608.02276) with a trained harness model, learned repair and online evolution; JIT-Agent's exclusive columns are JIT construction and instance-level synthesis.
   - *reattributed*: Table 4 over-credits Harness-R1 with Online evolution under its own caption definition (Harness-R1's online is training-time RL; intake-1323#record). JIT's exclusive columns widen to three; the "first purpose-built model" BOUND survives unchanged (it rides on Harness model, which Harness-R1 earns); the whole Online-evolution column is suspect (Adaptive AH, TTHE, RHI unverified). Cite Construction / Instance synthesis / Harness model; never cite the Online evolution column. [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 (Stage 4, plan B-Q7) - the novelty claim survives only on the words "just-in-time" - the first TRAINED harness model is Harness-R1 (2608.02276), three weeks earlier and far better evidenced, so cite the JIT priority claim only in that narrowed form]
   - anchor `Appendix A, Table 4 caption` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "Instance synthesis, Harness model, Learned repair, and Online evolution indicate whether a method directly synthesizes an instance-specific harness, trains the generator, learns repair from failed execution trajectories, and continues improving after deployment, respectively."
   - anchor `Appendix A, Table 4, Harness-R1 row` @ arXiv:2608.25593v2: "AOT (test-time editing) | Instance synthesis X | Harness model CHECK | Learned repair CHECK | Online evolution CHECK"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive: THE CENTRAL QUESTION IS SETTLED, ADVERSELY, FROM CODE. Table 3 per-case tokens count ONLY the executed harness's final validation round; the 27B meta-model's N=3 generations, the selector's re-prefill of all 3, and every pre-final repair round are structurally outside the number (scripts/eval/metrics.py:68-74,102-105; jit/meta_agent.py:1458-1479; scripts/run_jit.py:303-306,339; jit/README.md:222 "the numbers you report"). NO cost computation exists anywhere in the release and the summaries aggregate no tokens, so BOTH numeric columns of Table 3 are hand-derived by an undisclosed procedure with no price table. Calculator-checkable: on DeepSeek-V4-Flash the baselines imply $0.1374-0.1423/M tokens across fifteen cells while the JIT rows imply $0.1650/$0.1840/$0.2038 (16-44% higher) - the two columns contradict each other. N RESOLVED at 3 @ T=1.0 with the logprob selector (repo + model card; absent from the paper). Baselines: ZERO adapter/config/trace for any of the five; the paper's method statement is one citation sentence. Ablation: NONE on any surface ("ablat" zero times). 11-vs-13 confirmed; the missing two (ReAct, AOrchestra) are the protocol's structural ENDPOINTS. DECISIVE FOR ADOPTION: the released Apache-2.0 checkpoint is NOT the paper's model (card: Stage-I model distilled from the final checkpoint) - no local run of jit-27b can reproduce or refute Table 2/3. Two entry facts corrected: Eq. 4 has a FOURTH conjunct the entry dropped; "no institutional affiliation anywhere" is FALSE for v2 (Contributors block: NUS, EverMind AI, NTU - new in v2). Table 4 reattribution applied to claim 5. All Table 2/3 cells, both averages, column-lead counts and the Figure 4 subset caption re-verified. newer_version_exists corrected to false (v2 is current and was what Stage 1 read). HS-4 re-verified OPEN at harness-selection-and-integration.md:44. Cross-reference repair: intake-1140#record (HarnessOpt-Bench 2608.06301) is already indexed and should be linked. VERDICT CHANGED worth_investigating -> adopt_patterns. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1332#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: corroborates (claims [0, 4]) - CLAIM 0 (harness is a first-order determinant; capability is a property of the model-harness pair) is CORROBORATED independently and at larger scale than JIT itself provides: 5,194 trajectories over a complete 6x8x106 factorial with a released artifact, a 23.8-point aggregate gap, and the verbatim conclusion that capability should be reported at the model-harness configuration level. This is now the second primary-verified external support for the one JIT claim the bearing dive marked as externally corroborated, and it is the stronger of the two. CLAIM 4 (static mode generates N harnesses, selects one, executes one) is CORROBORATED INDIRECTLY AND METHODOLOGICALLY, not empirically: the bearing dive's central adverse finding was that JIT counts only the executed harness's final validation round, structurally excluding the meta-model's N generations and the selector's re-prefill. Harness-Bench demonstrates the correct instrument for the same measurement problem in ~450 lines - a benchmark-owned forwarding proxy that logs the usage block of every response the harness elicits, so internal calls are counted by construction and no harness self-report is trusted. That converts the bearing dive's finding from "JIT chose a narrow denominator" to "JIT chose a narrow denominator when a working total-cost instrument was published three months earlier by a paper in its own literature" - a sharper criticism, and a directly reusable design. CORRECTION OWED TO THE BEARING ENTRY, not to a key_claim but to its adversarial_pass row 3 and its dive_surfaced_sources row 1, both of which mis-describe this source: (a) Harness-Bench contains NO Claude Code and NO OpenCode - zero occurrences of either string in v1, no opencode adapter in the tree; "OpenClaw" (which it does evaluate, and ranks LAST) is a different system and the two must never be conflated; (b) JIT's Hermes/OpenCode ordering therefore cannot replicate here, and independently could not, because NO per-(harness x backbone) result exists anywhere in the paper - the eight backend names occur only in the Table 5 listing; (c) the "a secondary source reports Claude Code strongest for open Qwen models on three of four runs" attribution is unsupported by the primary source and should be struck (the nearest indexed figure is intake-916#record's Terminal-Bench-2.1 winner-flip, a different paper). The bearing dive's own hedge - "different benchmarks; the instrument that could make a contradiction" - was right to be cautious; the instrument turned out to be arXiv:2606.12344, not this one. NO key_claim of intake-1320#record changes, and nothing here depends on intake-1320#record, so depends_on is empty in both directions. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1341#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: independent (claims []) - Surfaced BY intake-1320#record's dive to settle whether harness swapping under a fixed backbone changes agent belief — the failure mode per-instance harness synthesis maximises. It settles that question in the negative for itself: it produces no admissible evidence either way, so it adds no support to and removes no support from any intake-1320#record claim, and neither entry's claims rest on the other's (hence depends_on: []). Three second-order effects worth recording. (a) It CONFIRMS intake-1320#record's dive's own rationing judgement: that dive ranked 2605.27922 Harness-Bench above this source and wrote "cap below Harness-Bench if rationing" — the deprioritised source returned a null and the prioritised one remains unindexed and remains the right instrument. (b) It supplies a SECOND worked example of the same genus as intake-1320#record's accounting finding: intake-1320#record's was "the reported number excluded work the system actually did" (the meta-model's tokens were outside Table 3), this one is "the compared arms differed in more than the named variable" (the reference arm got an empty metadata field, the treatment arms got the answer key). Two independent harness papers, two distinct ways to make a comparison unfair; both are failures our own eval tower could commit, which is why the ledger files a MEASUREMENT-grammar entry for each. (c) Both entries converge on the same directive for HS-4 — do not import external harness comparison tables as measurements — for entirely different reasons, which strengthens that directive rather than merely repeating it.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY: the M/P/A/F factorisation and M->P->F->A order as a coordinate system for the HS-1b/1c/1d/1e audit; Eq. 4's four-conjunct DPO gate and the Stage-III retention rule as ParetoArchive comparators (Eq. 6 gates efficiency behind I[r >= b_r]); the 11 shipped same-interface harness implementations; Table 4's Construction / Instance synthesis / Harness model columns. NEVER CARRY: the 36.0% cost reduction, any Table 3 token or cost cell, the Section 5.4 Pareto argument (token column counts only the executed harness's final round; no cost code ships; JIT rows' implied price is 16-44% above the baselines' - the columns contradict each other). Do NOT read the Hermes/OpenCode rows as product measurements into HS-4 (zero baseline code, zero adapter). Do NOT cite "first model purpose-built for JIT harness generation" without the Harness-R1 bound, and do not cite Table 4's Online evolution column at all. Do NOT plan a reproduction on the released weights (Stage-I distilled derivative). Do NOT assert the paper has no affiliation (v2: NUS, EverMind AI, NTU). Evo-GDPO is unablated - never shown to beat SFT+DPO.
In domain, open-source, self-hostable, pointed at an open local decision - not not_applicable. Not already_integrated: the local meta-harness program is entirely ahead-of-time. Not superseded. Not adopt_component at Stage 1: the headline cost advantage rests on an accounting question the paper never answers. adopt_patterns undersells it because the released 27B checkpoint makes a genuine local trial possible. The central thing a dive must settle: Table 3 reports per-case tokens and API cost for a system that ALSO runs a 27B meta-model generating N harness candidates per case (README default --rollouts 3), and the paper never states whether generation tokens are inside the reported figures; competing harnesses have no meta-model. Section 4.2's own phrasing - best-of-N "increases candidate diversity without increasing the number of ENVIRONMENT rollouts" - is exactly the elision. If generation is excluded, the 36.0% cost reduction and the Section 5.4 Pareto argument are not like-for-like.

## intake-1321#record — The Regression Tax: Decomposing Why Skills Help - and Hurt - LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22520
- **Verification**: dive-verified

- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. THE REGRESSION TAX: across 18 library conditions (3 stacks x 2 benchmarks x 3 skill libraries) 553 gain transitions and 324 regression transitions against the same no-skill baseline - regressions offset 59% of gross gains, net 229; no condition has zero regressions (b ranges 2-41). OfficeQA-Pro 122 gained / 81 broken (66% cancelled); SpreadsheetBench 431 / 243 (56%) (Section 4.1, Table 2).
   - *narrowed*: Arithmetic exact; 59% is raw-grader units, SpreadsheetBench side carries 32 coded grader-artifact regressions and no corrected baseline.
   - anchor `§4.1 The Regression Tax, para 1 ; paper.txt line 506` @ arXiv:2607.22520v1 (HTML route, sha256 b125778095ef7a43a352916e0fbf76d74495221bcf6315597ff076b2ba7196cf; PDF sha256 576ca97fc29336b4b4c7374a55c53b5_565_592_564_552f2d45298d0863f2f2e278), submitted 24 Jul 2026, read FULL from disk 2026-10-21: "Regressions therefore offset 59% of gross gains, leaving a net of 229."
1. THE DECOMPOSITION REVERSES A RANKING: on Claude Code x sonnet-4.6 / OfficeQA-Pro the three libraries gain 10/11/12 but regress 2/4/7; ranking by gains puts the openai library first, ranking by net reverses it - the anthropic library gains fewest yet nets most (+8) by preserving baseline successes (Sections 4.2, 6.1).
   - *narrowed*: +8 vs +7 vs +5 on N=94; one-task gap, CIs overlap; illustrative only.
   - anchor `§4.2, para 1 ; paper.txt line 522` @ arXiv:2607.22520v1 (HTML route, sha256 b125778095ef7a43a352916e0fbf76d74495221bcf6315597ff076b2ba7196cf; PDF sha256 576ca97fc29336b4b4c7374a55c53b5_565_592_564_552f2d45298d0863f2f2e278), submitted 24 Jul 2026, read FULL from disk 2026-10-21: "has the largest net (+8) because it preserves more baseline successes."
2. SKILL-DESCRIPTION OSMOSIS: a skill changes behaviour with its BODY NEVER LOADED because its triggering description sits in the system prompt every step. 14 of 81 OfficeQA-Pro and 70 of 243 SpreadsheetBench regressions are coded osmosis; worked case UID0096 - baseline answers 37.708% and passes, all three separately-authored libraries answer 38.757% with n_invoked = 0. Two-sided and net-helpful: on OpenCode x minimax-m2.7 / SpreadsheetBench a body is engaged on only 5-14% of tasks yet 84-93% of the 45-62 gains are invocation-free (Section 5.1).

A single author assigned these"

### Dive corrections

- DIVE 2026-10-21 (dpf-b). Every Table 2 cell (18 conditions) and both totals (553/324, 122/81, 431/243) recomputed from the table; all 18 exact McNemar p-values reproduce to the printed precision. Tables 3-5 totals reconcile (31/37/13; 115/73/55; Table 5 +Rec sums to 226). NARROWED (key_claims[1]): the "ranking reversal" is anthropic +8 vs Ours +7 (CI of Ours includes 0; one task apart) and openai +5 on N=94. It is illustrative of the gains-versus-net distinction, not a significant reordering. Do not cite it as evidence that any library is better. NARROWED (key_claims[2], help side): the entry and the paper both say 84-93% of minimax-m2.7 SpreadsheetBench gains are invocation-free. The same paper says that stack invokes a skill on only 5-14% of tasks, so 86-95% of ALL tasks are invocation-free: the gain share equals the base rate and shows no enrichment. Same for regressions (minimax SpreadsheetBench: osmosis 45 + Other 54 = 99 of 115 = 86% invocation-free). "Osmosis helps more than it harms" is therefore not demonstrated by the counts; it rests on the coding of influence evidence. NARROWED (key_claims[2], mechanism): the influence evidence is that all three libraries flip the same task the same way. All three treatment arms are paired against ONE single-run no-skill baseline (Section 3.6: one run per task-condition, no seed replication), so one lucky baseline pass produces three correlated "regressions". The cross-library convergence is therefore not independent evidence of a presence effect. UID0096 (37.708 vs 38.757 in 3/3 libraries) is consistent with osmosis and with a baseline draw from a two-attractor answer distribution; the paper concedes the labels are observational (Conclusion) and single-coder (Section 5). NARROWED (key_claims[0], key_claims[4]): the 59% tax is computed under the value-only SpreadsheetBench grader the paper shows mis-scores formulas; Table 4 codes 32 of 243 SpreadsheetBench regressions (13.2%) as grader artifact. Table 5 corrects only the nine treatment arms and publishes no corrected baseline, so a corrected gain/regression ledger cannot be recomputed from the paper. Treat 59% as an upper-bound-style raw-grader figure; the OfficeQA-Pro 66% is unaffected by this artifact. CONFIRMED (key_claims[3]): 59 of 81 OfficeQA-Pro regressions are grounding displacement. The Stage-1 caveat stands and is re-verified: no verification-alone regression exists (3 mixed cases); Appendix B.2 files its own verification example under grounding; the Conclusion concedes verification is the hardest mechanism to isolate. Cite two mechanisms. CONFIRMED (key_claims[5]): 5 of 18 nominal p<.05; 3 survive Bonferroni alpha/18; the two anthropic cells fail (adjusted p=0.69 and 0.14). The three survivors are one stack on one benchmark (Claude Code x sonnet-4.6 x SpreadsheetBench) and are not transferable to local open-weight consumers; the open-weight stack (minimax-m2.7) has one nominal hit (anthropic, SpreadsheetBench, +27, p=.008) that does not survive. UNAFFECTED (key_claims[0] arithmetic): the Stage-1 "analyst arithmetic check" is correct; additionally 18 of 18 conditions have net >= +1, so zero cells are net-negative while every cell has b >= 2. This is the crux for P5: a "share of net-negative cells" readout would report 0% on this data while regressions offset 59% of gains. STAGE-1 PREMISE CORRECTIONS: (a) relevance_justification (3) says "this repository is the experimental subject" via ~40 resident Skill descriptions. That applies to the Claude Code DEVELOPMENT harness only. The orchestrator SkillBank injects whole entries (title, principle, when_to_apply) via skill_retriever.py:_format_skill_entry (origin/main), so it has no description/body split to ablate; osmosis there is "injected but not applied" (adoption = 0), which M-23 already measures per role. The paper libraries had 3-23 skills; ours are ~60. (b) EV-10f and its pointer to intake-1321#record already exist on origin/main (suite-granularity limitation stated); only the per-task path is missing. SURFACING NOTE: the paper does not cite SkillsBench (intake-96#record) or SkillLearnBench; it cites Huang 2607.07504 as the data-science precedent (gains and regressions in roughly equal numbers).

### Verdict

dive-verified. Cite the decomposition and the grader-artifact fixture; cite osmosis as a labelled hypothesis (invocation-free shares equal the base rate; shared single-run baseline). Third replication of the regression prior, not new. Never transfer the Claude Code x sonnet-4.6 SpreadsheetBench effect to local roles.
NOT adopt_component: no data, run artifacts, libraries or OfficeQA-Pro subset released; the only code is sentient-agi/meta-skill-creator @ 02cb1db951e5, a 3-script authoring meta-skill that is not the contribution. NOT already_integrated: EV-10a implements the paired-decomposition RECOMMENDATION but no instrument here has a descriptions-only arm or mechanism coding. NOT new_opportunity: the skill-efficacy program exists; this is evidence into it. NOT not_applicable: harness-agnostic measurement methodology, one of three stacks is Claude Code x sonnet-4.6 - our own substrate. Three zero-compute patterns: (a) the THREE-ARM ablation as the correct shape for EV-10a; (b) report paired GAIN and REGRESSION COUNTS alongside net delta; (c) execution-based re-grading before believing any regression.

## intake-1323#record — Harness-R1: Learning to Edit Executable Runtime Harnesses from Agent Failure Trajectories

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02276
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, training_distillation, benchmark_methodology, tool_implementation, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1320#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Claimed first: "the first method, to our knowledge, that makes failure-conditioned, lifecycle-wide editing of an existing executable runtime a learned capability" (Abstract); vs Meta-Harness / AutoHarness / AHE / Life-Harness / HarnessX "the harness proposer usually remains fixed" (Sections 1-2); Harness-R1 "moves the learning target from the resulting harness to the editing policy".
   - *unaffected*: Quotes verified verbatim. Only addition: the paper also names AEGIS as symbolic-editing prior; harmless omission in the entry.
   - anchor `Abstract, para 2` @ arXiv:2608.02276v1 html: "We introduce Harness-R1, the first method, to our knowledge, that makes failure-conditioned, lifecycle-wide editing of an existing executable runtime a learned capability."
   - anchor `Section 1 (Introduction), related-work paragraph` @ arXiv:2608.02276v1 html: "Yet the harness proposer usually remains fixed: outcomes select or iteratively refine patches without directly updating proposer parameters; HarnessX uses cross-harness GRPO to train the task model, while AEGIS retains symbolic harness editing."
1. Mechanism: a SEPARATE 9B "harness engineer" reads a deterministic failure packet compacted from a BATCH of failed episodes of a FROZEN target agent and emits one executable overlay wired as four lifecycle hooks - on_init, make_pre_hint, on_before_action (block-and-reprompt / rewrite_action / force_action), on_post_step. Target weights never touched; invalid patches have no effect (Section 3.1; Appendix C Table C.1).
   - *narrowed*: The three on_before_action effects are NOT uniformly available - Appendix C says "where supported" and states DBBench v1 does not execute SQL rewrites or forced commits. And on_post_step can also return force_action, not only inject_hint (code_runner.py:31-34). Any citation of "four hooks with three action effects" must carry the per-benchmark gating.
   - anchor `Appendix C, Table C.1 caption text` @ arXiv:2608.02276v1 html: "on_before_action: After the target proposes an action but before environment execution; may block and reprompt or, where supported, rewrite or force the pending action. ... DBBench v1 accepts soft guidance and blocking but does not execute SQL rewrites or forced commits."
2. Reward is a real rerun, not a judge: after validation the overlay is installed and the frozen target reruns EVERY task in the batch including ones that originally succeeded; r(B,P) = mean reward change over the full batch, 0 for invalid/no-op/incomplete - so a regressing patch earns a negative reward (Section 3.1, Eq. 1).
   - *narrowed*: LOAD-BEARING for the cost axis. The full-batch rerun is of the PATCHED target only; baselines are cached before optimization and never re-executed, and a reward cache keyed by patch hash makes duplicate candidates free. The reward costs ONE rerun per distinct valid patch, not two. Any cost estimate built on "rerun both arms" over-prices the loop by 2x.
   - anchor `Section 3.1, sentence following Eq. 1` @ arXiv:2608.02276v1 html: "After validation, the overlay is installed and the same frozen target reruns every task in B, including tasks that originally succeeded."
   - anchor `Section 3.2, final sentence before Algorithm 1` @ arXiv:2608.02276v1 html: "Base trajectories, rewards, and failure packets are cached before optimization; online evaluation reruns only the patched target for candidates sampled from the current engineer."
3. Two-stage training: cold-start SFT on 877 GPT-5.5-teacher patches (381 WebShop / 248 ALFWorld / 248 DBBench) filtered to executable + complete + non-negative (Eq. 2; Appendix B.1), then ONLINE GRPO with K=8 candidates per packet over ~1,500 packets from a disjoint split, group-normalised advantages, clipped surrogate, truncated importance weights, zero entropy and zero KL coefficient (Section 3.2, Eqs. 3-4, Algorithm 1; Appendix B.2).
   - *unaffected*: Every hyperparameter verified verbatim against Appendix B.1/B.2. One repo/paper discrepancy recorded separately: released launcher defaults MAX_STALENESS to 4, the paper reports 8.
4. Scope boundary the paper states about ITSELF, load-bearing for the JIT-Agent comparison: the objective is "a same-batch, transductive objective, with no iterative refinement within an instance or persistent patch memory across batches" (Section 3.1). "Online" means online RL during TRAINING; at inference the engineer emits one patch per failure batch and does not participate further.
   - *unaffected*: Verified verbatim, and independently corroborated by the release: no archive, bank or persistent patch store exists anywhere in the tree. The entry's correction of JIT-Agent Table 4's "online evolution" credit stands. 2026-09-16 re-examined against dive_corrections item (13) (MHS-5, orchestrator 4f28e6c3, pending merge): still unaffected. The anti-override generalization that MHS-5 refutes is not this claim; the promptforge-mutation-safety-contract handoff cited it as #04 by mis-anchor (its #NN numbers index dive_actionables_ledger, not key_claims), now repointed to #record.
   - anchor `Section 3.1, sentence following Eq. 1` @ arXiv:2608.02276v1 html: "Using the same tasks before and after editing controls task composition but defines a same-batch, transductive objective, with no iterative refinement within an instance or persistent patch memory across batches."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive: (1) Every Table 1, Appendix E, Appendix F and Appendix G number in the entry verified VERBATIM against the v1 HTML; Appendix F additionally reproduced from the release''s raw per-seed pass counts. No headline number moved. (2) OVERTURNED one NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE sub-claim: the arXiv HTML DOES carry full per-author affiliations (SJTU x5, Xiaohongshu x4, Southeast x1, four corresponding authors) - only the abs page lacks them; the sourcing note "verified from the README byline" must be widened. Credibility arithmetic unchanged. (3) The COST HOLE is now PRICED from the release, which the paper never does: n=10 tasks per failure packet, K=8, rollout_batch=4, NUM_ROLLOUT=174 in the README''s worked command, released checkpoint labelled iter143 - giving <=320 target episode reruns PER ENGINEER UPDATE (= ~1.07 full sweeps of a 300-task suite per optimizer step) and <=55,680 for the reference run (~186 suite sweeps), <=120,000 if the full ~1,500-packet pool is consumed (~400 sweeps), plus ~50 sweeps of cached baseline and >=29 of SFT teacher validation - all per engineer, per frozen target. These are DERIVED UPPER BOUNDS from config constants, not measurements. (4) CORRECTED a premise: "4 iterations per update" PARTITIONS the 32-sequence global batch (actor.py:746), it does not multiply rollouts. (5) NARROWED the reward claim: the baseline is CACHED and never rerun (Sec 3.2 verbatim + metadata + code), and a patch-hash reward cache makes duplicate candidates free - so the loop costs ONE rerun per distinct valid patch, and any "rerun both arms" estimate over-prices it 2x. (6) NEW, from the 23-patch corpus: Harness-R1 emits block_and_prompt ONLY - zero rewrite_action, zero force_action - while both frontier editors use action override, and the two rewrite_action WebShop patches are exactly the -68 / -71 catastrophes (rescuing 2 and 0, breaking 70 and 71). Its hook set is also seed-invariant per benchmark where the frontier editors vary. (7) NUANCE the +8.9 pp: on ALFWorld even Harness-R1 regresses 47-61 previously-passing tasks per seed while rescuing 100-129 - the edge is a rescue:regression RATIO, not regression-freedom. (8) The four validator rejections, all frontier: two bare `Import`, one malformed-YAML-instead-of-JSON parse failure, one >5-helper-functions cap. (9) NARROWED a LOCAL claim the entry asserts: PromptForge''s "AST validation" is ast.parse for SYNTAX ONLY plus a one-level top-level-name check - no node denylist, no builtins restriction, no ast.walk in production, and its import test executes the mutated module unsandboxed in the live repo. We do NOT already have a code-mutation sandbox. (10) NARROWED contradicting_evidence[1]: 2607.12227 v2 (revised 2026-08-27, after this paper posted) contains no occurrence of "Harness-R1" or "2608.02276" - it is a category-level critique on a different benchmark, not a rebuttal. (11) NARROWED local relevance: for Gemma-4-26B-A4B-it the WebShop delta is +0.4 and for Gemma-4-31B-it -0.2; the Gemma gains are ALFWorld/DBBench only. (12) New unframed sub-result: ALFWorld Pick2 regresses 47.5 -> 39.3 in Table 1. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1339#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: corroborates (claims ['rr2', 'rr0', 'rr4']) - CORROBORATES rr2 in direction and NARROWS the rule a reader would draw from it. Corroboration: an independent group (POSTECH), on the same benchmark family (ALFWorld), with open weights and three seeds rather than one closed model, finds the same ordering - post-training dominates harness work in-distribution by a wide margin (Qwen2.5-7B: best frozen-model harness 29.0 vs post-trained band 55.6-86.9), exactly as Harness-R1''s Table 1 shows Agent SFT 59.2 beating harness editing of the vanilla target 53.6. Neither paper frames its own number this way; two papers now do it independently, so the reading intake-1323#record''s dive called analyst-generated is no longer a lone inference. NARROWING, and it is the load-bearing part: the residual harness lever ON TOP of post-training is not a small constant. On the same Qwen2.5-7B, h-low -> h-high adds +22.3 on top of GRPO (55.6 -> 77.9) but only +5.9 on top of GiGPO (81.0 -> 86.9). Its size is set by the training algorithm''s credit assignment. So Harness-R1''s +5.0 (59.2 -> 64.2) must NOT be generalised into "harness work is marginal once you post-train" - it is one point on a curve whose other end is +22.3. EXTENDS rr0 and rr4 rather than touching their numbers: intake-1323#record has no OOD axis at all, and this source supplies it. Harness-R1''s Appendix E transfer is measured by generating a NEW patch per unseen target with the task distribution and tool interface held fixed; this paper holds the harness fixed and moves the tool interface instead, and finds that what determines survival is a design choice made BEFORE training. Together they say the two entries measure orthogonal robustness axes and neither covers the other. NO REFUTATION. Nothing here contradicts any Harness-R1 number, and depends_on is empty in both directions: this source''s claims rest on its own tables, and no intake-1323#record claim would have to change if this were retracted - the corroboration is welcome, not load-bearing. ONE SHARED CAUTION, now stronger: intake-1323#record''s reader_should_conclude says "all three environments are closed-world interactive, so HS-4 must weigh this as METHOD, never as a candidate score." This source is ALSO closed-world, with ONE environment where 1323 had three and nothing above 7B in any training arm. The caution carries over and should be stated once for the pair rather than twice. (13) 2026-09-16 LOCAL EVIDENCE, MHS-5 (orchestrator 4f28e6c3 on branch sub/autopilot-safety-20260916, PENDING MERGE to origin/main as of 2026-09-16; artifact orchestration/datasets/harness_r1_heldout_effect_corpus.json, sha256 09ae7365febc11e0627a26dccfeb500a712c498e9de55a9034578cb301efbc71, derived from upstream examples/heldout_generalization @411bb5489ada, upstream results sha256 cf4ce8c0593ee009111da39b1f5a69161d2f22d0384b8be582_136_734_032c59e2): item (6) NARROWED. The two rewrite_action patches are the worst WebShop regressions, but the single worst valid patch over all 23 is a hint-only CONSTRAIN patch (deepseek-v4-pro, ALFWorld, seed 20260721, -83/490 = -16.9 pp, effect kind inject_hint). The generalization "every catastrophic held-out regression came from an override patch" (promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md MHS-4 rationale, never a key claim of this entry) is REFUTED on this corpus. The ordering survives: REPLACE is 4/4 negative, mean -8.4 pp, rescue:regression 0.21; CONSTRAIN is n=19, mean +3.9 pp, rescue:regression 1.65, with 4 negative (9 of the 19 are from the trained Harness-R1 editor, a confound). An effect-kind prior RANKS risk; it does not certify safety. This item touches none of key_claims 0-4 (claim_corrections unchanged except the claim-4 note). 2026-10-20 Stage-3 (intake-2006#record) - 2607.12227 citations in this entry are the v1/v2 reading (see inline markers); v4 does not engage Harness-R1.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY these, they are now checked against the primary source and the pinned tree: 44.3 -> 53.6 (+9.3); supervised-only 46.4 vs outcome-trained 53.6; Agent SFT alone 59.2 BEATS harness editing of the vanilla target 53.6 and the paper never says so; Appendix F +8.9 +/- 1.5 against -4.3 +/- 2.5 and -0.4 +/- 3.6, which the release reproduces from raw counts; the four-hook taxonomy with its TYPED, PER-HOOK-ALLOWLISTED return effects; the full-batch rerun as a built-in regression check with the baseline CACHED (one rerun per distinct valid patch, not two); and - the strongest new finding - the trained editor emits block_and_prompt ONLY, never rewrite_action or force_action, while the two frontier rewrite_action patches are precisely the -68 / -71 held-out catastrophes. Use Appendix F as the standing caution against PromptForge''s frozen-frontier-proposer contract, and use the sandbox contract (FORBIDDEN_NODES, restricted __builtins__, absolute size caps, per-invocation time and line budgets, closed effect enum, FORBIDDEN_TEXT_PATTERNS anti-leakage) as a concrete comparator - PromptForge today has none of it. DO NOT CARRY: (a) that the affiliations appear only in the README - the arXiv HTML has them, only the abs page does not; (b) "the harness reruns every task before and after" as a cost model - the baseline is cached, so halve any estimate built that way; (c) "4 iterations per update" as a rollout multiplier - it partitions the batch; (d) 2607.12227 as criticism OF THIS PAPER - its v2 does not cite it and it tests a different benchmark; (e) "+8.5 on our served Gemma worker" without saying that its WebShop half is +0.4; (f) "+8.9 pp means no regressions" - on ALFWorld the trained editor still breaks 47-61 passing tasks per seed; (g) any Harness-R1 number into a cost decision - the paper publishes zero cost figures and everything in would_settle Q1 is derived arithmetic from config constants, explicitly NOT a measurement; (h) the entry''s phrase "PromptForge with ... AST validation" as evidence we already have a mutation sandbox - locally that is ast.parse for syntax only. Nothing here bears on coding-agent harnesses: all three environments are closed-world interactive, so HS-4 must weigh this as METHOD, never as a candidate score.
Not not_applicable (in domain, Apache-2.0, a 9B Qwen3.5 derivative that would serve on the MI210 or CPU). Not already_integrated (local program is fixed-proposer). Not superseded - if anything the SENIOR claim relative to JIT-Agent on the trained-editor axis. Not adopt_component: the checkpoints are TARGET-SPECIFIC ("An engineer is only meaningful against the target it was trained for", README pinned), trained on WebShop/ALFWorld/DBBench, none our domain; reproducing training needs 8x H800 plus live reruns. adopt_patterns because the carriable content is mechanism-level and zero-compute: the outcome-grounded rerun reward; the four-hook taxonomy as a coordinate system; rerun-the-WHOLE-batch-including-prior-successes as a built-in regression check; the negative result on frozen frontier proposers as a PromptForge caution; the relocate-the-trainable-object move as a new HS-5 column; the 23-patch three-editor contrastive corpus.

## intake-1325#record — firstmate - Pi supervision branch (docs/pi-supervision-branch.md) and architecture (docs/architecture.md)

- **URL**: https://github.com/kunchenguid/firstmate
- **Verification**: dive-verified

- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. THE SIX OMITTED MECHANISM SECTIONS ARE PRESENT AND ARE THE SUBSTANCE. (1) OFF-THREAD DELIVERY: Pi runs extensions on the one JavaScript thread that draws the TUI, so a synchronous subprocess froze the captain's terminal; subprocess work moved to awaited spawns and an EXPLICIT QUEUE now supplies the ordering the single thread used to give for free (durable append before anything visible, read cursor advanced before the next reader, one ownership activation per generation); the lock's process ancestry is walked uncached every time because reparenting and pid reuse can invalidate a remembered chain.
   - *uncertain*: Pi-branch internals (off-thread delivery, explicit queue, uncached ancestry) NOT re-verified - outside the dispatched Claude-arm scope; only the Pi-only structural anchor at :15 re-confirmed. Retains Stage-1 status.
   - anchor `firstmate docs/pi-supervision-branch.md:15` @ d4eb2280: "The supervision branch itself is Pi-only by construction:"
1. (2) LOST-WAKE OUTCOME BACKSTOP: every main-actor wake drain compares each task's newest non-blank status event against the latest branch outcome that CAUSALLY covers that task's log; an uncovered terminal/captain-facing event prints once under STATUS OUTCOME BACKSTOP even if the queue row was acknowledged. Reads a fixed-size per-task outcome index, inspects at most the final 64 KiB of each log, and states its limits (equal-second migration favours a rare duplicate; a line crossing the 64 KiB window stays SILENT). The answer to "what if the cheap triage brain swallows something" that the secondary write-up omitted.
   - *uncertain*: NOT re-verified (Pi branch). LOAD-BEARING: the "port the lost-wake outcome backstop" actionable rests on this claim, which Stage 1 had not checked against bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh or tests/fm-wake-drain-outcome-backstop.test.sh and which the 2026-09-07 Stage-2b pass has now dive-verified against both @ d4eb2280 [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 (Stage 4, plan B-Q1): the row must no longer be read as carrying an unread premise]. [SUPERSEDED 2026-09-07 by the Stage-2b entry on bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh + the backstop test @ d4eb2280: key_claims[1] CONFIRMED on every sub-assertion; the backstop (one-shot notifier) and the processed-marker machinery (standing obligation) are two separate mechanisms.]
2. (3) TWO-STAGE NOISE FILTER: stage one is the zero-token bash watcher absorbing everything provably fine; stage two is the branch's per-event verdict via fm_branch_report. routine keeps a custom-message path WITH NO FOLLOW-UP TURN; captain appends a versioned fm-branch-visible-outcome entry. Load-bearing: "Pi custom session entries persist in the transcript but do not enter model context, so a stale compaction summary, an unrelated assistant response, prompt caching, or model instruction noncompliance cannot acknowledge or rewrite the outcome." DISPLAY is only half - PROCESSING is a separate obligation: unprocessed captain rows open exactly one hidden typed fm-branch-process turn, closed ONLY by main calling fm_branch_processed with the highest listed sequence; an unrelated, empty or paraphrasing reply does not close it; re-presented at every run boundary with a bounded budget.

Its second assertion (no hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext) is also false: additionalContext is schema-validated on twelve events including Stop and PostToolUse. (2) TWO NEW CONSTRAINTS on the tokenless wake: firstmate's "timeout": 28800 is INERT (docs: timeout not enforced on async command hooks; asyncRewake implies async; nothing clamps it), and asyncRewake only backgrounds when the session is interactive OR has streaming input - a plain `claude -p` actor gets no park. THREE NARROWINGS. So firstmate's budget is a coherent engineering target in a real price model - it remains an unverified budget, but it is no longer an isolated assertion. (2) THE ENTRY'S "NO BASELINE OF OUR OWN EXISTS" IS NOW HALF FALSE: h = 0.9845 measured over 61 Claude Code transcripts and 45,902 usage records is a baseline for the cache half, obtained with zero compute from journals we already hold, and it sits above firstmate's 95% later-call budget. ### Verdict

DIVE-VERIFIED, Claude-arm scope. BUILD ON THE HOOK PATH - the asyncRewake question is closed in firstmate's favour against the installed 2.1.263 and Anthropic's current hooks reference. Carry four facts: (1) a Stop hook with "asyncRewake": true that exits 2 wakes an IDLE Claude session with its stderr, tokenlessly, keystroke-free - a harness-native replacement for tmux_adapter.py doorbell injection; (2) `timeout` is NOT enforced on async hooks (do not copy 28800 believing it bounds the park), and asyncRewake backgrounds ONLY in an interactive or streaming-input session, so `claude -p --bare` headless actors are excluded; (3) hook-sourced liveness is SIX events, not four - UserPromptSubmit/Stop/StopFailure/SessionEnd plus PreCompact/PostCompact, without which the four-hook set reproduces INC-20260812 exactly; use Stop.background_tasks and UserPromptSubmit.source to separate operator turns from machine wakes; (4) a turnless append-into-context EXISTS on our harness (hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext via PostToolUse) - stop describing push-into-history as a harness gap; it is ours. NOT to carry: ~60%/95% as a measurement; the keystroke-echo numbers as anything but host-specific; our fleet_watch.sh as a pane scraper; the lost-wake outcome backstop as verified (key_claims 0 and 4 remain unchecked; key_claims[1], the backstop premise, is CONFIRMED against bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh and tests/fm-wake-drain-outcome-backstop.test.sh @ d4eb2280, so the backstop may be carried as verified) [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 (Stage 4, plan B-Q1): key_claims[1] is dive-verified, not unchecked]. Do not wire into the belief kernel. Before ANY push-into-history design: PostToolUse.classifierContext lets a hook hand the permission classifier a string weighable as USER INTENT - declare it out of bounds first. Claude Code gives the DISPLAY half free and NOTHING for the PROCESSING half (no sequence key, no ack tool, no marker) - the cheap primitive does not solve RC-4's F-33 or C49; the expensive one does.
The earlier pass's reasoning survives contact with the primary but its BOUNDARY was drawn in the wrong place. PI-ONLY, stated by the source and verified structurally: "The supervision branch itself is Pi-only BY CONSTRUCTION - the branch lives in .pi/extensions/fm-branch-supervision.ts, which only a Pi primary ever loads" - the second conversation, mirroring, silent merge, custom-entry renderer, model/effort pin, shared prompt_cache_key. HARNESS-INDEPENDENT is larger than the write-up implied: the bash-side additions are inert without branch state and "a home on ANY HARNESS that already has an outcome store still receives the shared drain compatibility recovery" - the LOST-WAKE OUTCOME BACKSTOP IS PORTABLE (tests/fm-wake-drain-outcome-backstop.test.sh) and needs no second conversation; also portable: the zero-token stage-one filter, durable wake queue, per-task lease + role partition in scripts, the semantic busy-state contract, the away daemon. NOT adopt_component (bash + TypeScript against Pi and tmux; our plane is Python + JSONL bus). NOT new_opportunity (owned P1 with a RATIFIED plan). NOT superseded, NOT not_applicable: one of the two things we most want (the tokenless wake) is demonstrated ON CLAUDE CODE.

## intake-1328#record — Not All Models Suit Expert Offloading: On Local Routing Consistency of Mixture-of-Expert Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16056
- **Verification**: dive-overturned
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: moe_optimization, routing_intelligence, benchmark_methodology, local_inference, inference_serving, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1318#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Two model-side metrics for how much CONSECUTIVE tokens reuse experts, parameter-free apart from segment length m: SRP (Segment Routing best Performance, Section 2.2 + Appendix C.1) - the UPPER-BOUND F1 of any m-length segment estimator over an expert's binary activation series, proven (Appendix C.3) to be a frequency threshold, hence an intrinsic property of expert and m; and SCH (Segment Cache best Hit rate, Section 2.3) - hit rate of an ORACLE cache of rho x (active experts) slots evicting the resident expert used least over the NEXT m tokens (deliberately weaker than Belady).
   - *narrowed*: Definitions and Appendix C.3 exact. "parameter-free apart from m" holds for SRP only; SCH carries rho and every SCH figure is SCH(rho=2). The reference SCH oracle is 201 lines (41_sch_calc.py:12-212), not the "20-line simulation" in verdict_justification (corrected in place).
   - anchor `ljcleo/moe-lrc src/41_sch_calc.py:12-212; src/40_topk.py:20; src/30_srp_calc.py:55` @ 30425e6418a9ff5f6afcb2d14b72975888060d28: "def calc_fsf(seq, num_experts, future_len) ... return torch.tensor(stat).cumsum(0) / seq.numel() / logits.topk(top_k, dim=-1).indices.to(torch.int8) / mask = (rank >= num_experts - top_k).to(torch.int16).transpose(0, 1)"
1. Local routing consistency VARIES SHARPLY ACROSS MoE FAMILIES and the variance is architectural: across 20 real MoE LLMs (3B-57B) on 22,528 x 512-token samples over 11 domains, m=16 SRP spans 78.16 (LLaMA-MoE-v2) to 19.27 (SwitchTransformers decoder), in four bands; at m=4 models are nearly indistinguishable and the spread opens as m grows (Section 3.2, Table 1).
   - *unaffected*: Cell-by-cell against Table 1 / Sec 3.1 / 3.2 - all confirmed.
2. GLOBAL LOAD BALANCE AND LOCAL ROUTING CONSISTENCY CAN COEXIST (abstract; Sections 3.3, 4.2): within one architecture they trade off (TOY: load-balance coefficient 0 / 0.01 / 0.1 -> SRP 56.42 / 43.56 / 36.42) but Qwen3 and GRIN-MoE show HIGH SRP AND MODERATE global balance simultaneously, via DOMAIN-SPECIALISED experts that fire consistently in-domain and go quiet out-of-domain, flattening the corpus aggregate.
   - *narrowed*: Numbers unaffected; the FRAMING must be tightened: the paper trades local consistency against LOCAL load balance ("almost always") and INFERS global balance rather than measuring independence; per-expert (E.7) global imbalance POSITIVELY contributes to SRP - a near-uniform global aggregate is a weak NEGATIVE signal, and the entry's "negatively coupled" has the sign inverted on the global axis.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "We find a strong trade-off between local routing consistency and local load balance, while showing that global load balance can coexist with local routing consistency."
   - anchor `Section 3.3, Load balance` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "Note that models like Qwen3 and GRIN-MoE show high SRP and moderate load balance simultaneously. Since high local routing consistency almost always means low local load balance, we conclude that these models exhibit good global load balance"
   - anchor `Section 3.3` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "Many REAL models with high SRP exhibit imbalanced routing, which largely contributes to their local routing consistency (see Appendix E.7)."
   - anchor `Appendix E.7, Expert level results` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "LLaMA-MoE-v2, Yuan2.0, and PowerMoE have experts with very high activation frequency. These experts naturally have very high local routing consistency and contribute to these models high model-level local routing consistency."
3. SHARED EXPERTS HARM local routing consistency - the single clearest architectural predictor: every model in groups 1 and 2 (eleven) has ZERO shared experts; every model with shared experts lands in group 3 or 4 (Section 3.3, Table 1). TOY ablation: Baseline (0 shared) 43.56, 1ShrExp 41.38, 2ShrExp 38.79.
   - *narrowed*: The eleven-zero-shared fact and TOY numbers stand, but shared experts are SUFFICIENT-not-necessary for low SRP (Jamba-Mini, NLLB, SwitchTF in groups 3/4 with zero shared; Jamba-Mini SCH(rho=2,m=16) 57.5-58.2 sits inside the band the entry called the "shared-expert band"). Group ranges leave GRIN-MoE and Qwen2 unassigned; Share1/Share2 vs 1ShrExp/2ShrExp naming inconsistency.
   - anchor `Section 3.3, Shared experts and expert combination space` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "Among REAL models, all models in groups 1 and 2 do not use shared models; Among TOY models, Share1 and Share2, having similar perplexity levels with Baseline, show significantly lower SRP than Baseline."
   - anchor `Table 1, S:A column` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "XVERSE-MoE 38.58 all 3:32 1:3 | Jamba-Mini 38.08 every 2 1:8 0 | DeepSeek-V2-Lite 37.92 after 1st 3:32 1:3 | Qwen2 36.74 all 1:8 1:1 | Qwen1.5-MoE 30.71 all 1:15 1:1 | OpenMoE 28.77 every 6 1:16 1:2"
   - anchor `/mnt/raid0/llm/models/Qwen_Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-Q8_0.gguf GGUF metadata (header-only 2026-09-07)` @ local checkpoint, read 2026-09-07: "general.architecture = qwen35moe; expert_count = 256; expert_used_count = 8; expert_shared_feed_forward_length = 512; 40x blk.N.{ffn_gate_shexp, ffn_up_shexp, ffn_down_shexp, ffn_gate_inp_shexp}"
   - anchor `/mnt/raid0/llm/models/unsloth/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GGUF/IQ4_XS-uniform-r16/*.gguf GGUF metadata` @ local checkpoint, read 2026-09-07: "general.architecture = qwen4exp; expert_count = 512; expert_used_count = 10; expert_shared_feed_forward_length = 640; 48x blk.N.ffn_*_shexp"
   - anchor `/workspace/handoffs/active/glm52-reviewer-capability-gates.md:231` @ repo working tree 2026-09-07: "| `ffn_*_shexp` (shared experts) | Q5_K / Q6_K |"
4. SCH tracks REAL cache algorithms well and cache size ~ 2x active experts is the recommended operating point: SCH-vs-LRU/LFU/Fixed hit-rate correlation rises with m (m=16: 90.43 / 88.70 / 89.79; m=256: 97.52 / 99.20 / 97.91); only group-1 models show a KNEE in SCH-vs-rho near rho=2; groups 3-4 grow nearly linearly (Section 5.1).
   - *narrowed*: Numbers exact including the rho=2 knee. The undefined "Fixed" column is the clairvoyant bound mislabelled (code computes LRU/LFU/Belady only) - NOT evidence about a static/pinned cache.
   - anchor `Section 5.1` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "Since only group 1 models (with the highest local routing consistency) have turning points on SCH, we claim that in general, rho=2 can balance cache effectiveness and efficiency."
   - anchor `ljcleo/moe-lrc src/50_chr_calc.py:15,46,107,278-281` @ 30425e6418a9ff5f6afcb2d14b72975888060d28: "calc_lru / calc_lfu / calc_beladi -> out[index,0..2]; no other algorithm exists in the repository"
5. The paper's own END-TO-END throughput validation is WEAK and it says so (Appendix F): naive LRU + on-demand offloading on the 1.43B TOY models correlates relative overhead to SCH at r ~ +0.2 in PREFILL (wrong sign) and r ~ -0.3 in DECODE; "the correlation coefficient is not far from 0".
   - *unaffected*: Appendix F verbatim. Balance: naive LRU on 1.43B toys; ReMoE (2605.27081) reports 1.77-1.99x decode under llama.cpp with a co-designed router - carry "this paper did not demonstrate throughput", not "hit rate never buys throughput".
   - anchor `Appendix F` @ arXiv:2505.16056v4: "the relative overhead w.r.t. full GPU inference has different correlations to SCH at the two inference stages: positive during prefilling (r approx 0.2), and negative during decoding (r approx -0.3) ... Nevertheless, the correlation coefficient is not far from 0"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive: all six key_claims about the PAPER survive (four narrowed, none falsified). What the dive OVERTURNED is the entry's own fleet-scoping conclusion. (1) GGUF headers read read-only: Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (qwen35moe, 256/8) and Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (qwen4exp, 512/10) EACH carry exactly one GATED shared expert per MoE block (ffn_gate/up/down_shexp + ffn_gate_inp_shexp x 40 / x 48, expert_shared_feed_forward_length 512 / 640) - S:A = 1:8 and 1:10, NOT zero; Qwen3-Next-80B the same. The paper's group-1 Qwen3-30B-A3B (128/8/0) is a topology we do not deploy, so the entry's "kill does not generalise to Qwen3.x" bound is FALSE and Stage-1 actionable (3) is answered in the negative - the fleet's SRP/SCH is UNMEASURED, not predicted-low (our S:A is 3-5x below the smallest measured shared-expert model). (2) The paper NEVER claims independence between global skew and local consistency; it claims a strong trade-off with LOCAL balance, deduces global balance FROM it, and Appendix E.7 says globally-hot experts positively CONTRIBUTE to SRP - a near-uniform global aggregate is a weak NEGATIVE signal; the entry's "negatively coupled" had the sign inverted on the global axis. (3) Companion repo PINNED at 30425e6418a9ff5f6afcb2d14b72975888060d28: per-layer safetensors traces (int8 top-k indices [samples,512,top_k] for SCH; per-token per-expert router rank for SRP) from a monkey-patched HF router; the SCH oracle is 201 lines (41_sch_calc.py:12-212), NOT the "20-line simulation" claimed. (4) Table 3 "Fixed" = the clairvoyant bound mislabelled (code computes LRU/LFU/Belady only). (5) Traces collected at bfloat16, not full precision. (6) The "shared-expert band" is not a shared-expert band (Jamba-Mini, 0 shared, sits inside); the Qwen3-vs-DeepSeekMoE SCH gap is 19-23 pts and WIDENS with m, not "~18-20 at every m". (7) OpenReview 2XMAUP74ig challenge-gated on five routes and UNREAD; independent corroboration by another route: arXiv 2608.18261 replicates Qwen3-30B locality with its own tool and adds a PRE-REGISTERED NEGATIVE against training routers to be cacheable, in tension with StickyMoE [corrected 2026-09-07 by intake-1336#record: the paper says 'complementary but in tension', and StickyMoE's own abstract reports <4% perplexity degradation - the two agree on magnitude, differ on acceptability]. (8) The GLM-5.2 UD-IQ2_M checkpoint and the /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/expert-routing-skew-glm52-* artifacts are GONE from disk; the shexp fact survives only as the in-repo tensor map (glm52-reviewer-capability-gates.md:231), cross-checked live against GLM-5.3-Flash (glm5next.expert_shared_count = 1). Entry text corrected in place: "20-line oracle", "full-precision", "~18-20 POINTS at every m", "ZERO occurrences", line pointers 223->231 and 230->238, and the relevance_justification effect (3) annotated OVERTURNED. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): dive-surfaced source DECLINED by the operator - OpenReview 2XMAUP74ig reviews (challenge-gated; no authenticated route supplied). arXiv 2608.18261 and 2605.27081 SELECTED for Stage-2b. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1336#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: corroborates (claims [1, 2]) - CORROBORATES on the science and CORRECTS the bearing entry's own reporting of this source. Corroboration: an independent instrument (a C++ eval-callback on an integer-quantized Q4_K_M GGUF) on Qwen3-30B-A3B finds strong temporal routing locality - lag-1 reuse 2.0x a skew-aware chance baseline, decaying monotonically to 1.27x at lag 32 - which is the same qualitative finding intake-1328#record's SRP places in its group 1 for Qwen3, reached with a different metric, a different framework and a different numeric precision. That is exactly the cross-instrument agreement claim 1 (the SRP ladder) and claim 2 (locality vs load balance) needed and did not have. It also SHARPENS claim 2's mechanism: because this paper's "chance" baseline is the marginal-skew-aware sum_e p_e^2 / sum_e p_e = 0.223 rather than k/N = 0.0625, its traces independently confirm intake-1328#record's Appendix-E.7 correction that global skew and local consistency are POSITIVELY coupled per expert - most of the raw adjacent-token reuse IS global skew, with 2.0x the temporal residue on top. CORRECTIONS to intake-1328#record's adversarial_pass and dive_corrections, which described this source second-hand: "in tension with StickyMoE [corrected 2026-09-07 by intake-1336#record: the paper says 'complementary but in tension', and StickyMoE's own abstract reports <4% perplexity degradation - the two agree on magnitude, differ on acceptability]" is wrong (the paper says "complementary but in tension"; the author renamed his repo to avoid a StickyMoE collision; StickyMoE's own abstract reports <4% PPL degradation); "the pre-registered <=1% perplexity gate" is half of a two-armed joint gate; "zero-surgery" is true of the model but not of the tree, which the freeze makes load-bearing; and actionable #5's "int8" is impossible for our 256/512-expert fleet. NEW AND NOT DERIVABLE FROM intake-1328#record: pooling four domains on one model drops Gini from 0.62-0.71 to 0.389 and raises entropy from 0.79-0.85 to 0.945, giving the 2026-07-17 near-uniform kill a second independent defect - corpus mixing - alongside the missing time axis intake-1328#record found. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1338#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: narrows (claims [5]) - Narrows the COUNTERWEIGHT, not the claim. intake-1328#record's key_claim 5 (Appendix F: SCH-to-overhead correlation is r ~ +0.2 prefill / -0.3 decode, "not far from 0 ... should not be the sole decider") was recorded as CONFIRMED and unaffected, but its claim_corrections[5] added a balancing clause built entirely on this source: "ReMoE (2605.27081) [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 by intake-1338#record: llama.cpp was STOCK b8185 (2afcdb9777b1), NOT co-designed and with no expert cache at all - the 1.77-1.99x is OS page-cache paging on a 16 GB Jetson holding a 15.7B model; the only clean expert-cache measurement is +8.4% throughput / -4.5% TPOT under an UNMERGED vLLM PR (#37190)] - so this source CORROBORATES Appendix F rather than counterweighting it; still carry 'this paper did not demonstrate throughput', not 'hit rate never buys throughput'." That clause must change, in two ways. (i) FACTUALLY: nothing was co-designed. llama.cpp was stock release b8185 (commit 2afcdb9777b1bac79fa4bfe284b9bf23085b0469) invoked with -np 1 -n 128 --mmap; that tree contains no expert cache at all, and the companion repo at 701420b6 holds a single README describing an Ollama checkpoint. The 1.77-1.99x is reduced OS page-fault traffic on a 16 GB Jetson holding a 15.7B model whose released GGUFs are 16.7-31.4 GB — the very configuration ReMoE's own Appendix A.4 failure mode 2 excludes from the EOR bound. (ii) IN DIRECTION: the paper's one clean per-layer-expert-cache measurement is +8.4% throughput / -4.5% TPOT (vLLM, RTX 3090, moe-expert-cache-size=6, under the still-unmerged vllm#37190). A +8.4% co-designed result does not counterweight Appendix F's weak correlation — it CORROBORATES it. So this source flips from "the strongest counterweight to Appendix-F pessimism" (as intake-1328#record's adversarial_pass[1] records it) to a second, independent instance of the same finding: a large, real locality improvement buys a single-digit throughput gain wherever an actual expert cache is present. intake-1328#record's claim 5 itself stands unchanged and is now better supported. The other claims (0-4) are untouched: this paper measures a different statistic (EOR, adjacent-step overlap) on a different model (DeepSeek-V2-Lite, 64/6 + 2 shared, S:A = 1:3) and says nothing about SRP/SCH, the shared-expert predictor, the rho=2 knee, or the Table-1 model ranking.

### Verdict

dive-verified for every claim ABOUT THE PAPER; the entry's own fleet conclusion is OVERTURNED. Carry the corrections, not the original wording. (1) OUR ENTIRE DEPLOYED MoE FLEET HAS SHARED EXPERTS - Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next and Qwen3-Next-80B each carry one gated shared expert per MoE block, as does the GLM lineage. The paper's zero-shared Qwen3-30B-A3B is NOT a model we run. Delete the claim that this paper spares Qwen3.x from the 2026-07-17 kill. (2) Do NOT invert that into a prediction: our S:A of 1:8-1:10 is 3-5x smaller than the smallest shared-expert model measured and our combination space far larger - the fleet's SRP/SCH is UNMEASURED on every model we serve. (3) The 2026-07-17 GLM artifact measures the paper's Table-2 LB column and nothing else (extract_imatrix_expert_counts.py reduces counts to one scalar per expert per layer, no token axis); its reopen trigger must read "an SRP/SCH measurement on per-token traces", never "a Zipfian hot set". (4) The paper NEVER asserts independence between global skew and local consistency, and per-expert asserts POSITIVE coupling (E.7): a near-uniform global aggregate LOWERS the prior on local consistency. (5) Do NOT cite it for throughput (Appendix F, naive LRU on 1.43B toys, r ~ -0.3) and do NOT read any of this as reopening hot-expert offload on this RAM-resident host - the bottleneck kills of intake-1193#record/1200 stand. (6) Do NOT cite the Table-3 "Fixed" column, "20-line oracle", "full-precision checkpoints", "~18-20 points at every m", or "shared-expert band". (7) Reviewer criticism remains UNREAD (challenge-gated) - treat the absence of objections as unknown.
adopt_patterns: the portable artifact is a MEASUREMENT DEFINITION plus an offline replay procedure - pure arithmetic over routing traces, no CUDA, no kernel (SRP is provably a frequency threshold; SCH is an incremental oracle-cache simulation [corrected by the 2026-09-07 dive: the reference calc_fsf is 201 lines, 41_sch_calc.py:12-212 - not 20]). No component to adopt (the companion repo is analysis notebooks, MIT, 5 stars). NOT already_integrated - zero occurrences, and our one adjacent artifact measures a DIFFERENT quantity. NOT not_applicable, resisted twice: our checkpoint is RAM-resident in 1.1 TB so the OFFLOADING application really is weak here (the bottleneck reasoning that killed intake-1193#record/1200 stands) - but the paper's SECOND use, as a MoE model-selection and kill-scope diagnostic, does not depend on offloading; and it bears on a CLOSED conclusion measured on a metric that cannot measure the property in question, the exact defect the verify-negatives discipline exists to catch. NOT superseded. Ceiling: computing SRP/SCH on OUR corpus requires a per-token routing tap we do not have - compute-gated and filed, not done.

## intake-1330#record — Beyond a Million Tokens: Benchmarking and Enhancing Long-Term Memory in LLMs (BEAM / LIGHT)

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.27246
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty high, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, memory_augmented, context_management, search_retrieval, rag_alternatives, context_extension)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1316#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. BEAM is 100 synthetic but narrative-coherent single-user/single-assistant conversations at four scales (README: 128K x20, 500K x35, 1M x35, 10M x10 - the released parquet and Table 1 label the smallest scale 100K) with 2,000 human-validated probing questions, 20 per conversation, exactly 2 per memory ability (Section 2.4). Construction is plan-first and recursive (seed -> narratives -> sub-plans of bullet points with time anchors -> batched user turns -> role-played assistant turns with question/follow-up detection capped at delta_1 = delta_2 = 2); 10M conversations use ten interlocking plans (Section 2.2).
   - *unaffected*: Composition, scales, 20-per-conversation, 2-per-ability, delta_1=delta_2=2 and the ten-interlocking-plans construction all verified against paper v2 and the pinned tree. The 128K-vs-100K drift is now confirmed in FOUR places (paper body 128K, Table 1 label 100K, README table 128K, repo directory chats/100K/, project site "128K Tokens"). Cite by scale VALUE and table as the entry already instructs.
   - anchor `arXiv:2510.27246v2 Section 4.1, footnote 3 and the arm-asymmetry sentence` @ arXiv:2510.27246v2 (html): "For long-context experiments, Qwen2.5-32B-AWQ is evaluated with a 128K context length, while for the RAG baseline and our proposed method a 32K context length is used. At the 10M-token, since none of the four models support this length, they are evaluated on the largest recent dialogue segment fi..."
1. The TAXONOMY is ten memory abilities, seven inherited and three introduced (Instruction Following, Event Ordering, Contradiction Resolution; Section 2.2, Table 2): Abstention, Contradiction Resolution, Event Ordering, Information Extraction, Instruction Following, Information Update (= Knowledge Update in Table 1/README), Multi-hop Reasoning (= Multi-Session Reasoning in the README), Preference Following, Summarization, Temporal Reasoning.
   - *unaffected*: Seven-inherited + three-new confirmed verbatim. Adds a usable detail: the CODE key names (multi_session_reasoning, knowledge_update) are the canonical strings and are exactly what Scroll's Table 6 rows use, which is corroborating evidence that external consumers ran BEAM's own rubric files.
2. The JUDGE PROTOCOL is nugget-based and ONE-SHOT: Section 2.1 formalises the probing question as the (|T|+1)-th turn with a single response y-hat = pi(x; T); annotators decompose the reference into atomic nuggets scored 0 / 0.5 / 1 by an LLM judge (Listing 20); Event Ordering uses Kendall tau-b over LLM-aligned sequences (Section 2.4). THE JUDGE MODEL IS NEVER NAMED IN THE PAPER; the released code hard-codes gpt-4.1-mini (src/llm.py:62 -> run_evaluation.py:273 @ b2da22ea) - the SAME family that generated the probing questions and candidate answers (Section 2.3).
   - *narrowed*: CONFIRMED but must be stated more precisely and more damningly. (a) "The judge model is never named in the paper" is TRUE, but the paper DOES contain the string GPT-4.1-mini twice — as the question generator — so anyone grepping will hit it and must not conclude the judge is named. (b) "the SAME family that generated the probing questions" UNDERSTATES it: Section 2.3 names GPT-4.1-mini as the generator of the questions AND candidate answers, and src/llm.py:62 makes GPT-4.1-mini the judge — the same model, not the same family. (c) NEW, previously unrecorded: the judge prompt is internally inconsistent — it mandates a responsiveness check against "the QUESTION" but has no question placeholder, and all ten evaluate_* functions accept probing_question and never insert it. The one guard against blanket refusal is inert.

3. NO ADMISSIBLE-INTERACTION-PROTOCOL RULE EXISTS: full-text search of v2, the README and the eval entrypoints for one-shot / single-turn / iterative / agentic / multi-turn / tool-call / leaderboard constraints finds only the Section 2.1 formalism; no leaderboard, no submission rules, no prohibition on multi-turn or agentic retrieval, no Limitations section. One-shot is a CONVENTION OF THE REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION (LIGHT is structurally single-shot), NOT a published rule. [Settles the Scroll question: Cognee's "outside the spirit of the benchmark" is a self-imposed community norm, not a BEAM constraint.]
   - *unaffected*: CONFIRMED and STRENGTHENED. The entry says one-shot is "a convention of the reference implementation". Sharper: it is a structural property — the released driver makes exactly one model.invoke per probing question against a fixed prefix, with no loop and no tool surface. Also resolves the entry's open agentmemorybenchmark.ai item: that site contains no BEAM data and is run by Hindsight/Vectorize, a scored competitor, so it is not an authority on BEAM protocol either.

4. LIGHT equips an LLM with three memories: episodic (per-turn Qwen2.5-32B-AWQ key-value + summary extraction, bge-small-en-v1.5 into FAISS IndexFlatIP, top-k retrieval), working (most recent z turn-pairs verbatim), scratchpad (per-turn salient-fact extraction, iteratively merged, compressed by GPT-4.1-nano to 15K once over 30K, question-filtered at inference by SemanticChunker + a yes/no relevance call) (Sections 3.1-3.2). Headline: 3.5%-12.69% average improvement over the strongest baseline.

NOT FOUND IN SOURCE: no Limitations; no reproducibility statement; no CIs, seeds, repetitions or variance; no latency/cost/token accounting for LIGHT (its per-turn indexing + per-turn scratchpad extraction + compression cascade is unpriced); no judge-vs-human agreement; no inter-annotator agreement on probe validity (the only kappa, 0.7044 / 0.7391 / 0.7849, covers conversation-quality Likert ratings on 20 conversations); no leaderboard.
