### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 dive: verdict UNCHANGED (adopt_component, the benchmark not LIGHT) and every key_claim CONFIRMED against arXiv v2 (sha256 cdb068bfcce4, no v3) and the pinned tree b2da22ea. What this dive CHANGED, and what must not be re-derived later: (1) RUNNABILITY IS SETTLED AND CHEAP — the reader is already a configurable OpenAI-compatible base URL (--reader_model_url), so llama-server is a drop-in for the system under test with zero edits; the complete hard-coded OpenAI surface is six sites (src/llm.py:61-64 and :68, run_evaluation.py:9 and :273, compute_metrics.py:15, answer_generation.py:289-293, long_term_memory_methods.py:33), and repointing src/llm.py:61-64 at a served model fixes the judge AND the LIGHT arm in one edit; the vanilla and pair_chunk-RAG arms then need no OpenAI key at all. The README''s step-3 path src/model_inference/answer_generation.sh is 404 — the real file is src/answer_probing_questions/answer_generation.sh and it is a pure env-var wrapper (that 404 is a README typo, not a missing capability). (2) THE 73.1-vs-0.266 GAP IS SETTLED BY ARITHMETIC — Scroll 73.1, Exabase 68.0, Hindsight 64.1 and Mem0 48.6 are each EXACTLY the unweighted mean of their ten BEAM ability columns x100 (731.2/680.4/640.8/486.1 divided by 10), so they use BEAM''s data, rubrics and aggregation; but they cannot have used BEAM''s driver, which makes exactly ONE model.invoke per question with a judge hard-wired to gpt-4.1-mini. They are on BEAM''s AXIS, not BEAM''s SCALE. MemPalace #125 proves the drift empirically: official judge prompt copied verbatim, but GPT-5.4-mini as judge plus a post-hoc >=0.75/>=0.25 binarisation BEAM never performs. (3) THE ABSTENTION CONFOUND IS NOW A MEASURED FACT, NOT AN INFERENCE — all 60 abstention rubrics I read (20 at 10M, 40 at 100K) are single-nugget and are the same refusal template "Based on the provided chat, there is no information related to X"; there are no answerable distractors; a topic-restating blanket refusal scores 1.0 by construction. BEAM''s own Table 1 100K row shows RAG beating LIGHT on abstention for all four backbones, and the mechanism is the arm-asymmetric prompt ("Do NOT use your internal knowledge" is given to the retrieval arms and not to the vanilla arm). (4) NEW DEFECT NOT PREVIOUSLY RECORDED ANYWHERE: unified_llm_judge_base_prompt mandates a responsiveness check against "the QUESTION" but has no question placeholder, and ALL TEN evaluate_* functions accept probing_question and never insert it — the judge never sees the question, in any ability. (5) THE JUDGE IS THE SAME MODEL, NOT THE SAME FAMILY, as the question-and-reference-answer generator; the paper does contain the string GPT-4.1-mini twice, but only as the generator. (6) LOCAL CORRECTIONS: ADAPTER_SUITES is at dataset_adapters.py:50, NOT in long_context_adapters.py, and registration is a five-point edit; a served-judge scoring mechanism already exists (scoring_method "llm_judge", judge_port 8082); and the repo ALREADY has an episodic-memory suite, tulving_episodic (arXiv 2501.13121) with a deterministic scorer and data on disk — so "the only memory benchmark in reach" is false and must be narrowed to "the only conversational, multi-session, LLM-judged one, and the only one above ~1M tokens". (7) All 48 Table-1 cells independently republished on the authors'' project site, retiring the entry''s ltx_tabular parse caveat. (8) Listing 20''s BODY does not render in the arXiv HTML — cite src/prompts.py:11547 for it, never the paper HTML. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): dive-surfaced source DECLINED by the operator - mem0.ai/blog/ai-memory-benchmarks-in-2026 (provenance of the 48.6 figure only). MemPalace issue #125 and arXiv 2501.13121 (= existing intake-408#record, re-dive) SELECTED for Stage-2b. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-408#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: narrows (claims []) - No BEAM key_claim (indices 0-5) is touched — this source says nothing about BEAM itself. The effect lands on the intake-1330#record DIVE''s justification-level corrections and its M-12 actionable, which it both CORROBORATES with a mechanism and NARROWS on three points. CORROBORATED. The 1330 dive''s claim_correction 10 ("''the only non-saturated memory benchmark in reach'' is false; tulving_episodic is the better instrument on the abstention/hallucination axis because its scorer is deterministic and its empty-answer group is a genuine control") is now proven at the mechanism level, not merely asserted: bin-0 questions are the SAME 36 templates as the answerable ones (paper Table 11), they are scored by the SAME f1_list, they are one of FIVE equally weighted bins (adapter:281), and the scorer decides emptiness by list length with no judge in the loop (adapter:156, :161). A blanket refusal therefore caps at Simple Recall 0.20 here, where it scores a clean 1.0 on BEAM''s abstention ability. The 1330 rider "pair BEAM with tulving_episodic for the abstention/hallucination axis" is upheld and should be strengthened to a sequencing instruction: Tulving first (registered, data on disk, no judge), BEAM second. NARROWED, THREE WAYS. (1) "Deterministic scorer" is an advantage with a measured cost — it replaces the paper''s LLM semantic matcher (Appendix B.3.1/B.4) and collapses free-text retrieval types (Event contents 0.0726, Full event details 0.0190, zero perfect scores across 126 questions), so it is a better instrument on the abstention and lexical-recall axes and a WORSE one on content recall. BEAM''s judged scoring, defects included, covers a type ours cannot. (2) The implied "clean instrument, ready to use" reading is too generous: score_tulving_run.py:120 folds latest and chronological rows into the Simple Recall bins, and chronological_tau (:51) computes tau over partial matches against the paper''s exact-match rule — two zero-compute fixes that must land before any Tulving number is citable, exactly parallel to the judge fix BEAM needs. (3) The 1330 dive''s framing "We have shipped an instrument nobody has characterised" is REFUTED on the substance while true on the letter: "tulving" does return ~0 hits in intake_index.yaml, but run 20260619_141212 scored the full 456-question short book on 2026-06-19, produced a dedicated failure-mode report, and is compiled into wiki/benchmark-methodology.md, memory-augmented.md, context-extension.md, routing-intelligence.md and INDEX.md. The gap was in the INTAKE INDEX, not in the repo''s knowledge. ONE ADDITION THE 1330 DIVE COULD NOT HAVE HAD: the authors ship a 548-row, 12-arm per-question memory-on/off reference (result_lenient_all_book_200.csv) already on our disk, in which chapter-chunk RAG beats full 100K in-context for all four published models. M-12 gains an external baseline curve that neither BEAM nor any other candidate instrument offers. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1337#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: corroborates (claims [2, 3, 7]) - CORROBORATES, on three claims, and extends two of them past what that dive could establish. key_claims[2] (the judge is unnamed in the paper, is gpt-4.1-mini in code, and its responsiveness guard is inert because the probing question is never passed): corroborated from an unexpected direction — an independent engineer, working only from the released prompt, evidently read the same defect and shipped the repair, adding a "<question>" placeholder to EVALUATION INPUTS and re-anchoring the RESPONSIVENESS REQUIREMENT (beam_100k_bench.py:207-209, :217-220, substituted at :329-333). The fix intake-1330#record''s ledger filed as new work for canonical-judge-suite-revamp.md is already written and diffable; transcribe it. The dive''s "judge swap changes the scale" thesis is also corroborated concretely: GPT-5.4-mini here, gpt-4.1-mini in BEAM, DeepSeek V4 Flash in Mnemosyne, Llama-4-Maverick in Hindsight — four judges, four axes. key_claims[3] (BEAM publishes no admissible-protocol rule and one-shot is a structural property of the released driver): corroborated by a third party independently reimplementing a strictly one-shot driver — one retrieval, one synthesis call, one judge call per rubric item, no loop, no tools, no cross-question accumulation — without any rule telling it to. The convention is now observed twice in independent implementations. key_claims[7] / contradicting_evidence (abstention is a confounded ability): corroborated and materially upgraded. intake-1330#record argued from rubric structure and from BEAM''s Table 1 (RAG beats LIGHT on abstention for all four backbones, a comparison that varies system AND backbone). This artifact gives a controlled five-arm ladder on ONE system, ONE judge and ONE dataset in which the worst arm overall (AAAK, 26.2%, temporal 1%) scores the HIGHEST abstention (85%), with the mechanism stated by the author. A fourth party (Mnemosyne v3.0.0) then reports 100.0%. This is now the best available citation for the confound and should replace the Table 1 citation in the M-12 rider. ONE NARROWING, NOT A REFUTATION, to intake-1330#record''s adversarial_pass[0], which described this artifact as demonstrating drift "even when the rubric prompt is copied verbatim". The CONCLUSION strengthens but the stated reason is wrong in two particulars: the rubric prompt was NOT copied verbatim (it was patched, favourably), and the principal source of drift is neither the judge nor the clamping but the FOLD — a rubric-item micro-average of verdicts binarised at >= 0.5, which that dive did not have and which is worth 6.7pp on its own. Restate as "even with the official rubric FILES and a near-official judge prompt". Also narrow "the only independent end-to-end BEAM run I found": it is the FIRST (2026-04-07) and the only one with a published harness, but AxDSan/mnemosyne (2026-05-18) is a second. NOT A REFUTATION OF ANYTHING in intake-1330#record; no claim there requires amendment, only the two wording corrections above. The dependency also runs both ways: my reading of the judge-prompt patch AS a repair rests on intake-1330#record''s finding that all ten evaluate_* functions discard probing_question (see depends_on).

### Verdict

DIVE-VERIFIED — every key_claim and every Table 1/8/9/13 cell cited by this entry is now safe to cite, with Table 1 double-sourced against the authors'' project site. Carry all of the following together or none of it. (a) BEAM publishes NO admissible-interaction-protocol rule, and one-shot is not a convention but a structural property of the released driver (one model.invoke per question, no loop, no tools) — so a multi-turn agentic system and a one-shot memory pipeline quoted on the same BEAM_10M axis are NOT comparable, and the asymmetry is a community norm, not a benchmark rule. (b) EVERY externally quoted BEAM_10M headline is the unweighted mean of the ten ability columns x100 — verified by exact arithmetic on Scroll 73.1, Exabase 68.0, Hindsight 64.1, Mem0 48.6 — but NONE of them used BEAM''s driver or BEAM''s judge, so they share BEAM''s axis and not BEAM''s scale; never place 73.1 and 0.266 on one number line without saying so. (c) The judge is gpt-4.1-mini, from src/llm.py:62 only, and it is the SAME MODEL that wrote the questions and the reference answers; there is no judge-vs-human agreement anywhere in paper or repo; and the judge prompt''s responsiveness guard is inert because the question is never passed to it. Swap the judge before any local number is citable, and measure agreement on a sample. (d) ABSTENTION MUST BE EXCLUDED from any memory-quality average: 60/60 rubrics read are single-nugget refusal templates with no answerable controls, and BEAM''s own Table 1 shows the arm that sees less of the conversation scoring higher. Report instruction_following and preference_following separately (they are style/format compliance — one 100K rubric is literally "code blocks with syntax highlighting") and event_ordering separately (tau_norm, a different scale). The discriminating core is information_extraction, multi_session_reasoning, knowledge_update, temporal_reasoning and summarization; contradiction_resolution is 0.000-0.053 for every published system and contributes no signal. (e) Prefer the 100K/128K split: 40 questions/ability (2.5pp per flipped question) against 10M''s 20 (5pp). (f) Any first-party A/B must prompt-match the arms — BEAM does not, and its retrieval arms carry a closed-book instruction the vanilla arm lacks. (g) DO NOT carry: "ADAPTER_SUITES lives in long_context_adapters.py" (it is dataset_adapters.py:50); "we have no episodic-memory instrument" (tulving_episodic is registered with data on disk); "the judge swap is new work" (scoring_method llm_judge with judge_port 8082 already exists); "all 44 listings are readable in the paper" (the HTML build drops the bodies — cite src/prompts.py). (h) LIGHT remains NOT adopted: working memory is net-negative at three of four scales by its own Table 8, the per-turn indexing/compression cascade is unpriced against 7,757-turn conversations, and K=15 and sparse SPLADE both beat the published defaults.
The COMPONENT is the benchmark: MIT code, CC BY-SA 4.0 data on HuggingFace as parquet, a documented runner (answer generation -> run_evaluation -> report_results), no proprietary dependency except an OpenAI judge we would swap for a served model. It fills a hole our own completed roster names by omission (five long-context datasets, zero conversational-memory ones) and is the only non-saturated memory benchmark in reach (our indexed leaders intake-326#record/611 differ by 0.4pp on LongMemEval and nothing is measurable). Not already_integrated (0 hits). Not new_opportunity (nothing here is a technique to build). Not superseded (the only published 10M-token coherent-conversation memory benchmark; Scroll treats it as the headline target). Not not_applicable (self-hostable, open-licensed, 128K/500K runnable on our fleet). The LIGHT half is NOT adopted - working memory net-negative at three of four scales by its own Table 8, the indexing/compression cascade unpriced, the architecture already covered; at most a comparison arm.

## intake-1331#record — firstmate - the durable outcome store and the lost-wake outcome backstop (bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh, bin/fm-wake-drain.sh, bin/fm-classify-lib.sh, tests/fm-wake-drain-outcome-backstop.test.sh)

- **URL**: https://github.com/kunchenguid/firstmate/blob/d4eb22801f7509c1c7942ea047db78bcb5389ea0/bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, tool_implementation, knowledge_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1325#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. THE BACKSTOP IS NOT THE SEQUENCE/PROCESSED-MARKER MACHINERY - the entry that surfaced it conflates two SEPARABLE mechanisms living in different files. (A) THE OUTCOME STORE, bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh: an append-only JSONL with a gap-free monotonic seq, a READ CURSOR, and a SEPARATE PROCESSED MARKER. (B) THE LOST-WAKE OUTCOME BACKSTOP, print_status_outcome_backstop_section in bin/fm-wake-drain.sh:270-361: a main-actor-only, WAKE-QUEUE-INDEPENDENT reconciliation between WORLD STATE (each task's $STATE/<task>.status append-only log) and HANDLED STATE (a bounded per-task outcome index derived from the store). The backstop never reads seq, the cursor, or the processed marker; it compares BYTE POSITIONS in the status log. The portable idea is RECONCILE ARTIFACTS, NOT TRACK MESSAGES.
   - *unaffected*: The two-mechanism separation is visible directly in the file boundaries and the variable sets; no read of the doc alone would have produced it, which is why the bearing entry conflated them.
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-wake-drain.sh:270-273` @ d4eb2280: "print_status_outcome_backstop_section() { # <task-and-endpoint-snapshot> ... [ "$ACTOR" = main ] || return 0"
1. WHAT COUNTS AS A LOST WAKE, MECHANICALLY: the wake queue ($STATE/.wake-queue, TSV epoch\tseq\tkind\tkey\tpayload, monotonic seq from $STATE/.wake-queue.seq under a lock, fm-wake-lib.sh:1597-1624) has rows DELETED on per-actor acknowledgement. A wake is lost when a row was claimed and acked but no outcome row covering that task was ever appended - e.g. the branch claimed the row, acked it, then settled on a provider error with no fm_branch_report. DETECTION DOES NOT TRACK THE ROW AT ALL: on every main drain the newest non-blank status line per task is classified captain-facing (status_is_captain_relevant, fm-classify-lib.sh:134) and suppressed ONLY IF the per-task index shows same file identity AND an outcome-captured byte endpoint >= the event's end offset (fm-wake-drain.sh:324-327). Uncovered -> printed once under "STATUS OUTCOME BACKSTOP (newest captain-facing task event has no covering branch outcome):". RE-DELIVERY IS PRINTING INTO THE NEXT DRAIN'S STDOUT - there is no re-queue, no new wake, no turn opened.
   - *unaffected*: Queue-independence is not merely asserted - it is the subject of a dedicated regression that consumes the queue row first and then asserts recovery.
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-wake-drain.sh:324-327` @ d4eb2280: "if [ -n "$BRANCH_OUTCOME_INDEX_ENDPOINT" ] && [ "$BRANCH_OUTCOME_INDEX_IDENT" = "$ident" ] && [ "$BRANCH_OUTCOME_INDEX_ENDPOINT" -ge "$event_endpoint" ]; then continue; fi"
   - anchor `firstmate docs/pi-supervision-branch.md:85-86` @ d4eb2280: "Every main-actor wake drain checks each task's newest non-blank status event against the latest supervision-branch outcome that causally covers that task's status log. When that event is terminal or otherwise captain-facing and remains uncovered, the drain prints it once in `STATUS OUTCOME BACKST..."
2. THE BACKSTOP IS ONE-SHOT PER EVENT, NOT A STANDING OBLIGATION - this is its sharpest limit and the entry does not say it. A fourth TSV column in $STATE/.status-presentation-cursor is a per-task BYTE-OFFSET RECEIPT (fm-classify-lib.sh:985-1010 reader, :1258-1290 writer). Once an event is presented, receipt >= event_endpoint and the loop `continue`s forever after (fm-wake-drain.sh:300-304). A captain-facing event main SEES AND IGNORES never re-appears. The standing "delivered but unprocessed, re-presented until explicitly acknowledged" property is the OTHER mechanism: the processed marker in bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh, driven by the Pi extension. Conflating them would port the wrong half.
   - *unaffected*: Load-bearing for the port decision: the backstop is a notification, not an obligation. Verified in code and by the idempotence test asserting an EMPTY second drain.
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-wake-drain.sh:300-304` @ d4eb2280: "receipt=$(status_outcome_backstop_cursor_offset "$STATE/$task.status") || { rc=1; break; } / [ "$receipt" -lt "$endpoint" ] || continue ... [ "$receipt" -lt "$event_endpoint" ] || continue"
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-wake-drain.sh:530-532` @ d4eb2280: "Prepare every section before presentation, but do not commit its receipt until the prepared bytes reach stdout. If the consumer closes or fails, leave the receipt behind so the next drain can recover the presentation."
   - anchor `local /workspace/scripts/coordination/session_bus.py:1131-1134` @ workspace HEAD 2026-09-07: "acked -> bare ack(s) only; clears reach-only messages, but an action_required message KEEPS APPEARING (receipt != action)"
   - anchor `local /workspace/coordination/session-bus/BUS_PROTOCOL.md:216-221` @ workspace HEAD 2026-09-07: "A bare ack is receipt, not action: it clears a reach-only message but an `action_required` one stays listed as acked-awaiting-action."
3. THE OUTCOME-STORE CONTRACT (bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh:5-58 header, enforced in code): store written BEFORE delivery ("store-first durability: nothing about a handled event depends on conversation memory", :56-58); EVERY read and append re-validates the whole log as a gap-free 1..N sequence with an exact key-set schema and fails closed (last_seq(), :177-208); the cursor advances only forwards and never past the validated tail; the PROCESSED MARKER advances ONLY through an explicit sequence-bound acknowledgement naming a currently-unprocessed CAPTAIN row at or below the read cursor - a routine, unread, or already-processed target is REFUSED (:530-573); it never moves past the cursor or backwards, so "an unrelated or empty model answer cannot move it"; an absent marker reads as 0 (every delivered row unprocessed - the safe direction); every mutation runs under one lock. THIS half is plain bash+jq over JSONL and is harness-independent; only its CALLER is Pi.
   - *unaffected*: This is the half our defects actually need and the half the bearing entry did not distinguish. Its tests are in tests/fm-branch-supervision.test.sh, which was NOT fetched - the store contract is verified here against CODE, not against its own suite.
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh:25-36` @ d4eb2280: "Processed marker: $STATE/.branch-outcomes-processed holds the highest seq whose captain rows main has ACKNOWLEDGED as processed, separately from the read cursor ... It only advances through an explicit sequence-bound acknowledgement naming a currently unprocessed captain row at or below the read ..."
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh:56-58` @ d4eb2280: "The store is written BEFORE the outcome is delivered to main (store-first durability): nothing about a handled event depends on conversation memory."
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh:10-12` @ d4eb2280: "Every read and append validates the complete log as a gap-free sequence; malformed, duplicate, or reordered rows fail closed."
4. BOUNDED COST AND FAIL-CLOSED DISCIPLINE ARE FIRST-CLASS, AND TEST-ENFORCED. The drain reads a versioned single-line per-task cache (<=512 B, tag fm-branch-outcome-index-v1) instead of scanning lifetime history; inspects at most the final 64 KiB of each status log (fm-classify-lib.sh:880, limit=65536) and treats a latest line crossing that window as UNCLASSIFIABLE AND SILENT; caps each printed item at 219 chars and total task output at 4000 bytes with an explicit "N more omitted (byte cap)" line; commits the receipt ONLY AFTER the prepared bytes reach stdout (fm-wake-drain.sh:512-542), so a failed consumer leaves the event recoverable; and emits distinct SKIPPED lines for a malformed store, a busy lock, and an unreadable index rather than presenting anything. Self-heal: a missing/invalid ready marker triggers `processed-init --held-lock`, itself guarded by a bounded live-ancestry walk of the lock owner (:389-418).
   - *narrowed*: All constants and fail-closed paths confirmed in code, but the 64 KiB claim's cited test is confounded (a `working:` verb is rejected for an independent reason), so that one sub-property rests on code reading alone.
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-classify-lib.sh:872-878` @ d4eb2280: "A pathological latest line that crosses the fixed bound is intentionally unclassifiable and omitted: bounded memory and never presenting a possibly routine line as captain-facing take precedence on that edge."
   - anchor `firstmate bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh:280-283` @ d4eb2280: "Legacy rows have only whole-second epochs, so equal timestamps cannot prove whether the status preceded the outcome. Leave that span uncovered: migration may rarely duplicate an old handled event, but it will not hide a plausibly later captain-facing one."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + Stage-2b on firstmate bin/fm-branch-outcome.sh, bin/fm-wake-drain.sh, bin/fm-classify-lib.sh and tests/fm-wake-drain-outcome-backstop.test.sh @ d4eb2280. (1) intake-1325#record key_claims[1] IS CONFIRMED verbatim on every sub-assertion and moves from unverified to dive-verified; its dive's claim_corrections row "LOAD-BEARING ... no pass has checked" and reader_should_conclude's "do not carry the lost-wake outcome backstop as verified" are now SUPERSEDED and must be annotated as such in place, not left standing. (2) A CONFLATION IS CORRECTED: the entry treats the backstop and the seq/read-cursor/processed-marker machinery as one thing. They are two mechanisms in two files with different state and different guarantees - the backstop is a queue-independent, ONE-SHOT, byte-receipted reconciliation between world state and handled state; the processed marker is the standing obligation. The filed "port the backstop" actionable is ambiguous as written and must name which. (3) THE HALF OUR DEFECTS NEED IS THE OTHER HALF: RC-4/F-33(a) need a standing obligation, which the backstop deliberately does not provide. (4) NEW LOCAL FACT THAT NARROWS THE ACTIONABLE SHARPLY: the read/processed split already exists on our bus, derived (session_bus.py:1121-1244, BUS_PROTOCOL.md:216-221) - the missing pieces are a monotonic sequence, a DURABLE watermark, gap/duplicate detection, and reconciliation against worker-written artifacts rather than bus rows. Frame the work as COMPLETING an existing mechanism, not building a new one. (5) TWO ID DISAMBIGUATIONS THE DISPATCH ITSELF NEEDED: F-33 and C49 each name two different defects; F-33..F-36 is a previously unrecorded four-way collision. (6) ONE FAULT FOUND IN THE SOURCE: the 64 KiB oversize test is confounded and does not prove the property the doc cites it for. (7) ONE INDEPENDENT CORROBORATION of the principle (IETF draft-abak-agent-control-delivery-evidence-00), which lowers novelty to medium and raises adoption confidence. (8) A HOUSE BIAS MUST BE DECIDED, NOT ASSUMED: store-before-deliver inverts session_bus_coordinator.py:1626's deliberate save-after-relay ordering. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): IETF draft-abak-agent-control-delivery-evidence-00, "Evidence Requirements for Agent Control Deliver; firstmate .pi/extensions/fm-branch-supervision.ts + tests/fm-pi-branch-extension.test.sh @ d4eb2280.

### Verdict

DIVE-VERIFIED. intake-1325#record key_claims[1] is CONFIRMED - stop carrying it as unverified and annotate its dive's "do not treat the backstop as dive-verified" as SUPERSEDED. Then carry five things. (1) THE ENTRY CONFLATES TWO MECHANISMS. The lost-wake outcome backstop (fm-wake-drain.sh:270-361) is a queue-independent ONE-SHOT reconciliation that prints an uncovered captain-facing status event once and never again; the read-cursor/processed-marker machinery (fm-branch-outcome.sh) is the standing obligation. Any row saying "port the backstop" must name which, because they solve different problems. (2) THE PORTABLE IDEA IS THE RECONCILIATION SOURCE, NOT THE CODE: reconcile against artifacts the WORKER wrote, not against the message plane. Every mechanism on our bus is scoped to bus rows and therefore structurally cannot find work whose notification never became a row - which is the exact loss class. (3) HALF OF IT IS ALREADY OURS: routed_view's pending/acked/actioned with "receipt != action" (session_bus.py:1121-1244), TERMINAL_DISPOSITIONS (:998), _print_owed_actions on every drain including the empty one (:2020-2043, :2181-2192), BUS_PROTOCOL.md:216-221. Cost the work as COMPLETING this - add a durable sequence and a `processed_seq` in the owner-write-only cursors/<agent>.json (INVARIANTS 1/9 satisfied by construction, INVARIANT 7 argues for it) - not as building a primitive. (4) IT DOES NOT FIX RC-4. The backstop is durability, not liveness; RC-4's ruled fix is a tick (R-16), and A-6 stays open. It DOES fit F-33(a) "lost track of the mains" precisely, and it fixes the ACCOUNTING half of F-33(b) while fixing none of its effector half. It does nothing for C49(a). (5) BEFORE MAKING ANY RE-PRESENTATION PERSISTENT, ADOPT C39'S REMEDIES FIRST - a spent-receipt lookup and "annotate, never suppress" (session-bus-thin-dispatcher.md:1937-1946) - because C49's own thesis ("an escalation that fires on a well-run night trains everyone to ignore it") and FW-1's cry-wolf rule both bite here; firstmate avoids this only by being one-shot and byte-capped. NOT TO CARRY: the bash/jq/perl artifacts (decline stands and is now evidenced); the 64 KiB silence property as test-proven (its cited test is confounded); the backstop as anything that creates an obligation; store-before-deliver as a free change (it inverts session_bus_coordinator.py:1626). Do not wire into the belief kernel - no empirical claims, credibility null.
adopt_patterns, not adopt_component - and the DECLINE in intake-1325#record's dive ledger ("their plane is bash+TypeScript, ours is Python+JSONL; the code is not portable") survives contact with the code and is REINFORCED: fm-branch-outcome.sh is 640 lines of Bash-3.2-compatible shell whose correctness rests on jq schema predicates, `mv -f` rename atomicity, mktemp-in-the-same-directory, a symlink-or-directory lock with live-pid ancestry walking, and perl for byte-span reads. Re-expressing that in Python is a rewrite, not a port. What IS adoptable is three separable DESIGN commitments: (1) reconcile against worker-written world-state artifacts, not only against messages; (2) durable processed watermark distinct from the read cursor, advanced only by a sequence-naming acknowledgement that refuses an out-of-band target; (3) bounded cost and fail-closed skip messages so the recovery path cannot itself become the outage. Not new_opportunity (the owning problem is already filed), not already_integrated (half of it is - see prior_coverage_found), not superseded, not not_applicable.

- updated `handoffs/active/session-bus-thin-dispatcher.md`

## intake-1332#record — Harness-Bench: Measuring Harness Effects across Models in Realistic Agent Workflows

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27922
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, tool_implementation, cost_aware_routing, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1320#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Agent capability is a property of the model-harness CONFIGURATION, not of the base model: over 5,194 trajectories the aggregate score spans 23.8 points between the best and worst configurable harness on the identical task set and backend pool (Abstract; Section 4.2; Table 2).
   - *unaffected*: Headline verified verbatim and arithmetically (76.2-52.4=23.8; 6x8x106+106=5,194). This is the strongest claim in the paper and it is now the primary-verified basis for four index entries that have been citing it secondhand since 2026-07. Carry it. Note only that the 23.8pp is an aggregate-SCORE gap; the completion-only gap is 21.6pp, and the score gap is partly amplified by the multiplicative Process factor.
   - anchor `Section 4.2, Observed configuration-level variation` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "Among configurable harnesses, NanoBot obtains the highest aggregate score (76.2), while OpenClaw obtains the lowest score (52.4), giving a 23.8-point gap under the same task set and model-backend pool."
   - anchor `Abstract, final results sentence` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "These results suggest that agent capability should be reported at the model--harness configuration level rather than attributed to the base model alone."
   - anchor `src/harnessbench/usage_proxy.py:178-292 (_forward) and runner.py:444-499 (_collect_proxy_usage_summary) - the total-token instrument` @ github.com/Qihoo360/harness-bench @ 1025086a: "log_entry.update(_normalize_usage(payload)) ... summary["total_tokens"] += int(row.get("total_tokens", 0) or 0)"
   - anchor `Appendix B.1, evaluated harness list (the full set - note the absentees)` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "OpenClaw [OpenClaw, 2026], ZeroClaw [ZeroClaw Labs, 2026], Hermes [Nous Research, 2026], Moltis [Moltis, 2026], NullClaw [NullClaw, 2026], and NanoBot [HKUDS, 2026]. Each harness is evaluated using its native execution behavior while following the same task environment, budget, and evaluation pro..."
1. The evaluation protocol fixes the external task conditions - prompt, fixtures, initial sandbox state, budget, timeout, evaluator - while leaving prompting format, action format, tool interface, state policy and retry/recovery NATIVE to each harness (Section 3.1; Table 1; Section 4.1).
   - *narrowed*: The NATIVE-EXECUTION half is fully confirmed and is the paper's best contribution - config rewriting plus a transparent proxy, with a published one-method adapter ABC. The SHARED-BUDGET half does not survive the code: no token budget or step cap exists at all, and the per-harness timeout_sec overrides the per-task timeout (runner.py:545), the inverse of Table 1. Cite "preserving native execution behaviour" and the adapter protocol; never cite "under shared budgets" without this caveat.
   - anchor `Section 3.1, second paragraph` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "We do not force all systems into a common internal policy or runtime. Instead, each harness runs with its native execution behavior under the same task resources and evaluation protocol. The resulting measurements should therefore be interpreted as configuration-level diagnostics of model-harness..."
   - anchor `Table 1, controlled-vs-varying factor rows` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "Budget, timeout, evaluator | Fixed for each task ... Prompting and action format | Native to each harness ... Retry and recovery behavior | Native to each harness ... Permissions and tools | Minimal required set enabled"
   - anchor `src/harnessbench/runner.py:545 (inside AdapterRunContext construction)` @ github.com/Qihoo360/harness-bench @ 1025086a446653702b80cfb48babbeec35db6b2c: "timeout_sec=int(model_cfg.get("timeout_sec", task.timeout_sec or app.default_timeout_sec)),"
   - anchor `src/harnessbench/adapters/base.py:8-13 (the entire adapter protocol)` @ github.com/Qihoo360/harness-bench @ 1025086a: "class BaseAdapter(ABC): name = "base" @abstractmethod def run(self, ctx: AdapterRunContext) -> AdapterRunResult: raise NotImplementedError"
   - anchor `src/harnessbench/adapters/openclaw.py:30-41 (_merge_user_config, the backbone-swap mechanism)` @ github.com/Qihoo360/harness-bench @ 1025086a: "upstream = str(provider_cfg.get("baseUrl") or "").strip() ... routes[prefix] = {"framework": "openclaw", "provider": str(provider_name), "upstream": upstream} provider_cfg["baseUrl"] = f"{proxy_base_url}{prefix}""
2. The benchmark asset is 106 sandboxed, offline, oracle-checkable tasks across 8 workflow categories, each with a manifest, fixtures, evaluator, timeout and optional hooks, admitted only on four manual criteria - Realism, Solvability, Oracle-checkability, Integrity (Section 3.2; Figure 2).
   - *unaffected*: Artifact-verified at the pinned SHA - 106/106/106. Load-bearing: this is the rare case where the released asset independently confirms a paper's count. The task-admission criteria and the manifest schema are safe to reuse.
   - anchor `Section 3.2, admission criteria` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "We retain tasks only when they satisfy four criteria: Realism, reflecting a plausible user workflow; Solvability, meaning the task can be completed using the provided sandbox resources; Oracle-checkability, meaning success can be verified by deterministic checks or a specified rubric; and Integri..."
3. Harness dependence is inversely related to backbone strength: stronger backends achieve higher mean score AND lower cross-harness variance, so weaker backbones are more sensitive to the surrounding execution substrate (Section 4.3; Figure 3 left). Category-level, variance is largest in Data/BI/Finance Analytics, Workspace/Tool Use and Software Engineering, smallest in Office & Business Communication (Appendix C; Figure 4).
   - *narrowed*: DIRECTION confirmed verbatim, MAGNITUDE unpublished. Both the backbone gradient and the category ordering exist only as figures with no value quoted in text, over n=1 cells on 4 non-deterministic hosted backends, with the paper itself disclaiming that its variance is not repeated-run variance. Cite the direction as a qualitative expectation; never quote or infer a number.
   - anchor `Section 4.3, Harness Dependence` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "stronger model backends tend to achieve higher mean scores while exhibiting lower cross-harness variance ... In contrast, weaker or less robust backends show larger variance across harnesses ... This variance reflects cross-harness variation over the fixed task suite, not repeated-run stochastic ..."
   - anchor `Appendix C, Category-Level Harness Dependence` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "The largest cross-harness variance appears in Data, BI & Finance Analytics, Workspace/Tool Use, and Software Engineering tasks ... By contrast, Office & Business Communication exhibits the lowest variance."
4. Failures are diagnosed as "execution drift" - reasoning decoupling from the machine-checkable conditions of success - with five recurring symptoms whose rates among FAILED trajectories are Contract/format 36.4%, Tool/recovery 24.6%, Evidence/grounding 14.6%, Artifact commitment 11.1%, State/continuation 9.3% (Sections 5.1-5.2; Table 3).
   - *unaffected*: Rates and the execution-alignment definition verified verbatim, and the non-exclusivity is stated by the paper, so the 96.0 sum is not a defect. Residual: the denominator is never counted and the symptoms are partly extracted by the same LLM judge that produced the Process score. Usable as a failure-triage LABEL SET; not as calibrated rates.
   - anchor `Section 5.2, Execution Alignment definition` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "We use execution alignment to describe the degree to which a harness preserves correspondence among the agent's reasoning, the observed workspace state, the actions taken through tools, and the conditions checked by the evaluator."
   - anchor `Table 3, Section 5.1` @ arXiv:2605.27922v1: "Contract / format 36.4% ... Tool / recovery 24.6% ... Evidence / grounding 14.6% ... Artifact commitment 11.1% ... State / continuation 9.3%"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 Stage-2b combined ingest+dive. THE DISPATCH PREMISE IS REFUTED BY THE PRIMARY SOURCE: Harness-Bench evaluates OpenClaw, ZeroClaw, Hermes, Moltis, NullClaw, NanoBot plus Codex - "OpenCode" and "Claude Code" occur ZERO times in v1 and no opencode adapter exists in the tree. "OpenClaw" is not "OpenCode". Consequently intake-1320#record's adversarial row 3 is WRONG on two counts and must be corrected where it is applied: (a) JIT's Hermes/OpenCode ordering cannot be replicated against this paper - OpenCode is absent and there is NO per-(harness x backbone) table at all, only 8-backend averages, with the eight model names appearing solely in Table 5; (b) the "Claude Code strongest for open Qwen models on three of four runs" attribution is unsupported - no such statement, harness, or per-model result exists here (the nearest indexed thing is intake-916#record's Terminal-Bench-2.1 winner-flip figure, a different source). THE CENTRAL QUESTION IS SETTLED FAVOURABLY AND FROM CODE: the documented adapter protocol JIT lacks exists here - a one-method BaseAdapter ABC, twelve concrete adapters plus a generic_cli escape hatch, and a backbone-swap mechanism that rewrites the harness's OWN provider baseUrl to a local forwarding proxy so the native loop is untouched. Token accounting is the OPPOSITE of JIT's defect: a 127.0.0.1 MITM proxy logs the usage block of every response the harness elicits, so harness-internal planner/summarizer/sub-agent/retry tokens are all counted without harness self-report, and the judge is correctly excluded because the proxy context closes before the rubric LLM runs. Two accounting defects of its own: silent zero-fill on any non-JSON/non-usage-SSE response, and a session-log fallback (Hermes's own SQLite) that mixes two instruments into one reported column. THE SHARED BUDGET DOES NOT SURVIVE THE CODE: no token budget or step cap exists anywhere; runner.py:545 lets the per-harness timeout override the per-task timeout, and the only shipped config spreads harnesses over 180s-3600s - the inverse of Table 1. FOR HS-4: NOT the instrument. It measures Hermes but not OpenCode, oh-my-pi, dsh, earendil-pi or any ACP-speaker; publishes no per-backbone number; uses 8 hosted APIs and no self-hosted cell; pins no harness version; and does not measure the cooperation surface that HS-4 actually turns on. Its one real HS-4 contribution is an efficiency datapoint on Hermes: 139.7K tokens / 22.6 turns per task, 2.03x tokens and 3.10x turns of the best harness for 5.0 points less. NEW ADVERSARIAL FINDING: arXiv:2606.12344 Claw-SWE-Bench ranks OpenClaw FIRST on both GLM-5.1 (73.4%) and Qwen 3.6-flash (66.0%) where this paper ranks it LAST of six - a cross-benchmark ordering reversal over four overlapping harnesses, neither paper pinning a version. NEW INTERNAL FINDING: Security = 100.0 for all seven systems over all 5,194 trajectories, so the binary security gate never fired and the multiplicative Security factor is inert across the whole study. POSITIVE ARITHMETIC FINDING: the check that broke JIT's Table 3 PASSES here - Score exceeds Completion x mean(process) by a monotone positive-correlation gap (OpenClaw +7.5pp down to Codex +0.3pp). RELEASE VERIFIED: exactly 106 task dirs / 106 task.yaml / 106 oracle_grade.py at HEAD 1025086a, but NO LICENSE file, the model-selection per-harness configs are absent, and the tree ships 12 adapters for a paper reporting 7 systems. FIVE PRIOR PROSE MENTIONS LOCATED: intake_index.yaml:52522 (intake-916#record), :53257 and :53292 (intake-921#record), :54031 (intake-926#record), :80403 (intake-1140#record) - all four secondhand figure sets are now primary-verified CORRECT, so none needs amending. No arxiv_id/url collision: this is a genuinely new entry. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv:2606.12344 - Claw-SWE-Bench: A Benchmark for Evaluating OpenClaw-style Agent Harnesses on Codi; https://github.com/reacher-z/HarnessBench.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY: (1) the adapter protocol as the answer to "how do you drive a harness on a non-native backbone" - a one-method BaseAdapter, one adapter per harness, a generic_cli escape hatch, and a backbone swap performed by REWRITING THE HARNESS'S OWN provider baseUrl to a benchmark-controlled endpoint rather than wrapping its model call; this is the documented protocol intake-1320#record found missing. (2) usage_proxy.py as the reference total-token instrument - count at the proxy, not from the harness's self-report, so internal planner/summarizer/sub-agent/retry tokens are included by construction; and note its two defects (silent zero-fill on non-usage responses, session-log fallback mixing instruments). (3) The four task-admission criteria (Realism, Solvability, Oracle-checkability, Integrity) and the Security x Completion x Process form for suite construction. (4) The Table 3 five-symptom failure taxonomy as a LABEL SET. (5) The single HS-4-relevant number: Hermes at 139.7K tokens / 22.6 turns per task, 2.03x and 3.10x the best harness for 5.0 points less. NEVER CARRY: any harness ORDERING from this paper (Claw-SWE-Bench reverses it on four overlapping harnesses; no version is pinned on either side); any claim that Harness-Bench measures Claude Code or OpenCode (it measures neither - "OpenClaw" is a different system); any per-backbone or per-Qwen result (none exists; the backend names appear only in Table 5); any magnitude for the backbone-strength-versus-variance gradient (figure-only, n=1, disclaimed as not repeated-run variance); "under shared budgets" without the caveat that the code has no token budget and lets the harness timeout override the task timeout; and any security or permission conclusion (the gate returned 100.0 for all seven systems on all 5,194 trajectories and never fired). DO NOT let this paper's numbers enter the HS-4 decision packet as evidence - file it as an instrument-and-caution pointer. DO NOT resolve "HarnessBench" by name: github.com/reacher-z/HarnessBench is a different project.
The transferable content is the METHOD, not the numbers. Adopt: (a) the adapter protocol - a one-method BaseAdapter plus one adapter per harness that rewrites the harness's OWN provider config to a benchmark-controlled endpoint, which is exactly the documented protocol JIT-Agent Table 3 lacks; (b) usage_proxy.py - a local forwarding proxy that counts every token the harness spends, including internal planner/summarizer/sub-agent calls, without harness cooperation and without trusting the harness's self-report; (c) the four admission criteria (Realism, Solvability, Oracle-checkability, Integrity) and the multiplicative Security x Completion x Process score for our own suite construction. Do NOT adopt the numbers for HS-4: the measured harness set does not contain OpenCode, oh-my-pi, dsh or earendil-pi, no harness version is pinned anywhere, all 8 backends are hosted APIs (no self-hosted llama.cpp cell), and no per-(harness x backbone) table exists. Not adopt_component: running it here is possible (the adapters rewrite baseUrl, so our local /v1 is reachable) but is compute-gated, requires installing six third-party harnesses, and the repo carries NO LICENSE file.

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-selection-and-integration.md`

## intake-1333#record — The Harness Effect: How Orchestration Design Sets the Token Economics of Enterprise Agentic AI

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06906
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, cost_aware_routing, context_management, benchmark_methodology, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1325#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. CONTROLLED SINGLE-VARIABLE SWAP. 22 locked, capability-audited enterprise prompts x six foundation models (Claude Sonnet 4.6, Gemini 3.1, Gemini Flash 3.5, Qwen 3.6, GLM 5.1, Palmyra X6), same judges and same pinned OpenRouter price table in both arms; only the orchestration layer changes (a conventional production loop frozen 2026-06-07 vs the Writer Agent Harness). Blended result: cost/task -41% ($0.21->$0.12), median wall-clock -44% (48s->27s), tokens/task -38% (14.2k->8.8k), task-completion 0.78->0.81 REPORTED AS PARITY, not improvement (Table 3, Sections 5.1, 6.1, 5.5).
   - *unaffected*: Every Table 3 cell and the parity framing verified verbatim against v1. The one thing to carry forward is the paper's own reading, not the press's: the defensible headline is -38% tokens at parity, and +0.03 quality is NOT a result.
   - anchor `Section 5.5 (Statistical posture)` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "At n=22, quality deltas are directional, not statistically significant, and we treat them as such throughout: the headline +0.03 is reported as parity, not improvement."
1. EFFICIENCY IS MODEL-INVARIANT, QUALITY IS NOT. Every one of six models gets cheaper, -33% (Gemini 3.1) to -61% (Flash 3.5), no exception (Table 4); mean quality gain per model correlates with baseline strength at r=0.99, n=6 ("harness leverage", Section 6.4: Palmyra +0.079, Sonnet +0.073, Qwen -0.031). Of 48 capability x model cells, 30 improve, 11 flat, 7 regress, and all 7 regressions are on the three smaller models concentrated in orchestration-heavy capabilities (Section 6.3). The one net-new capability, sub-agent delegation, has a CAPABILITY FLOOR: 0.86/0.85 on the two strongest models but 0.42-0.45 on the fast tier (Table 5).
   - *unaffected*: Table 4, Section 6.4 and Table 5 verified cell by cell. Narrowing to remember when citing: r=0.99 is over six points and the authors call it suggestive; the sub-agent floor row has no baseline arm at all.
   - anchor `Section 6.2, Table 4 and prose` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "under the baseline, moving from the most expensive model (Palmyra X6 at $0.25) to the cheapest (Qwen 3.6 at $0.16) saves 36%; keeping any model and adopting the harness saves 33% to 61%."
   - anchor `Section 6.5` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "an orchestration feature carries a capability floor, below which exposing it produces failures rather than function."
2. A FORMAL TOKEN-ECONOMICS MODEL AT THE ORCHESTRATION LAYER. Per-task cost C = sum over turns of (p_in*T_in + p_out*T_out) (Eq. 1); the input side decomposes into harness-constructed terms T_in = S(system) + H(history) + G(tool schemas) + R(retrieval) + U(user turn) (Eq. 2); naive full-transcript replay makes total input tokens grow as O(k^2) in turn count (Eq. 3); and under provider prefix caching the EFFECTIVE input price is p_in_eff = p_in*(1 - h*(1-kappa)) with kappa about 0.1, where h is the cache-read fraction and is "neither a model property nor a provider favor: it is a function of prompt byte-stability across turns, which is set entirely by how the orchestration layer assembles context" (Eq. 4, Section 3.1). Plus Definition 1 (token maxing as a measurable trajectory) and two efficiency metrics, quality-per-dollar and completions-per-million-tokens (Eq. 5).
   - *unaffected*: Eqs. 1-5 and Definition 1 verified. This is the part of the paper with the longest half-life for us: it is arithmetic, not a vendor result, and it survives the paper's provenance problems entirely.
   - anchor `Section 3.1, after Eq. 4` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1, sha256 43b050f6466d: "Crucially, h is neither a model property nor a provider favor: it is a function of prompt byte-stability across turns, which is set entirely by how the orchestration layer assembles context."
3. THE PAPER DOES NOT MEASURE THE ORCHESTRATION SHARE OF TOKENS. The S/H/G/R/U split of Eq. 2 is ANALYTIC ONLY; every reported result is an aggregate per-task total (tokens, cost, latency, quality). No table, figure or appendix reports what fraction of tokens went to system prompt, replayed history, tool schemas, retrieval or coordination, in either arm. The single cache measurement in the paper — 7,876 of 7,886 prompt tokens (99.9%) served as cache reads — is one identical-prefix call "in the harness repository" (Figure 2 caption), NOT a statistic over the 22-task evaluation.
   - *unaffected*: Re-checked exhaustively rather than accepted: there is no orchestration-share number anywhere in the results, appendices or figure captions. This is the answer to the dispatch's central question and it is a negative.
   - anchor `Figure 2 caption, Section 4.3` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "Measured on an identical-prefix call: 99.9% of prompt tokens (7,876 of 7,886) served as cache reads, pricing the dominant input term at approx 0.1x list (Eq. 4)."
   - anchor `Section 4.4 (What the comparison shows)` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "We stress the epistemic status: Table 1 compares public designs and documentation, not measured runs; configuration-level measurement of the token dimension across harnesses, in the style of Harness-Bench [28], is natural future work."
   - anchor `Section 4.3, mechanism (4) Zero-token waiting` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "Waiting is a continuation, not a loop: when a run needs a human answer, an approval, or a long background job, it suspends durably at zero token cost and resumes on an ingress event - no polling turns."
   - anchor `Section 4.4 (Multi-agent conversation frameworks)` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "Shared-transcript multi-agency is a token multiplier by construction: each participating agent re-reads the growing conversation and each carries its own role preamble."
4. ACCOUNTING SCOPE IS UNDER-SPECIFIED AND THE GAPS ARE ASYMMETRIC BETWEEN ARMS. Token intensity is "prompt and completion tokens ... recorded per turn by the trace shim" for "every turn of every run" (Sections 4.1, 5.4), but the harness (and NOT the frozen baseline) also runs compaction summarization "on a cheaper helper model, off the paying loop" and side work — telemetry, reasoning-trace titles, deliverable materialization — that "runs off the paying loop on helper models" (Section 4.3 mechanisms 2 and 3), and spawns sub-agents whose exploration "the parent loop never pays for" (Sections 4.3, 6.5). The paper NEVER states whether helper-model or sub-agent tokens are inside the reported per-task total. Internal tension on the task set: Section 5.1 "Both arms execute the same 22 tasks" vs Section 5.2 "and — under the harness only — Sub-agents (delegated work, net-new)" and Appendix C "the sub-agent capability is excluded from means because it has no baseline arm".
   - *narrowed*: Narrowed in the paper's FAVOUR on one point and against it on another. In favour: judge tokens are a symmetric instrument and their exclusion is not a defect, so the intake-1320#record analogy must not be stretched to cover them. Against: the helper-model and sub-agent exposures are ASYMMETRIC (harness arm only), which is the sharp form of the objection and stronger than a generic accounting complaint; plus the previously unnoticed task-composition tension between Sections 5.1/5.5 and Section 5.2/Appendix C.
   - anchor `Section 5.4 (Metrics)` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "Prompt and completion tokens are recorded per turn by the trace shim; token intensity tau is the per-task total."
   - anchor `Section 4.3, mechanism (3) Context offload` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "side work - telemetry, reasoning-trace titles, deliverable materialization - runs off the paying loop on helper models."
   - anchor `Appendix C` @ arXiv:2607.06906v1: "the sub-agent capability is excluded from means because it has no baseline arm."
   - anchor `/home/node/.claude/projects/-workspace/*.jsonl (61 files), computed 2026-09-07` @ local read-only computation, no files written: "45,902 usage-bearing assistant messages; input-side 21,844,037,961 tokens (cache_read 21,506,294,714 / cache_creation 337,282,686 / uncached input 460,561), output 62,314,305; h = 0.9845; input:output = 350.5:1"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 STAGE-2b combined ingest + dive; the entry is born dive-verified, every key_claim checked against arXiv:2607.06906v1 read in full by the HTML route (sha256 43b050f6466d). SIX THINGS THAT CANNOT BE RE-DERIVED LATER. (1) THE DISPATCH QUESTION IS ANSWERED NEGATIVELY: the paper does NOT measure the orchestration share of tokens; Eq. 2 is an analytic decomposition that is never instantiated, and all results are aggregate per-task totals. The only share-shaped fact derivable is a FLOOR - at least 38% of a conventional replay-everything loop's per-task tokens bought no quality on this workload. (2) THE ACCOUNTING DEFECT OF intake-1320#record RECURS IN A WEAKER-EVIDENCE FORM: tau is defined without scope, and three token sinks that exist ONLY in the harness arm (helper-model compaction summaries, helper-model side work, sub-agent exploration) are described as "off the paying loop" and never accounted; unlike intake-1320#record this cannot be settled from code because nothing is released. Judge tokens are explicitly NOT part of the objection - they are a symmetric instrument. Treat -38%/-41% as an upper bound. (3) A NEW, UNDISCLOSED INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY: Sections 5.1/5.5 claim both arms ran the same 22 prompts while Section 5.2 makes Sub-agents harness-only and Appendix C says that capability has no baseline arm. (4) ON TOPOLOGY the paper is architectural only: it names shared-transcript in-thread multi-agency "a token multiplier by construction" and specifies a capped-summary/metadata-sidecar delegation firewall and zero-token durable waits, but measures no topology comparison, contains no supervision branch or turnless-append mechanism, and Table 1's ratings of Claude Code and Hermes are design-time readings of public documentation, not runs. (5) WHAT WE CAN ACTUALLY REUSE, verified locally in this pass: Eq. 4 is computable on our EXISTING Claude Code journals today - 61 transcripts under /home/node/.claude/projects/-workspace/ carry 45,902 usage-bearing messages, giving cache-read fraction h = 0.9845 and an input:output ratio of 350.5:1 (3.5x more input-dominated than the ~100:1 the paper cites), so the input term is even more of the whole bill here than there. The Eq.-2 split is NOT computable without a purpose label added at write time, and the paper supplies no method for recovering it. (6) NO COMPANION ARTIFACT EXISTS: Appendix D is a prospective release checklist with no URL, no repo, no data - do not cite it as available. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv:2605.27922 Harness-Bench (ref [28]; 0 index entries, currently only a Tier-2b citation inside ; arXiv:2605.26112 "From model scaling to system scaling: Scaling the harness in agentic AI" (ref [27]; https://manus.im/blog/Context-Engineering-for-AI-Agents-Lessons-from-Building-Manus (ref [30]; 0 ind; arXiv:2604.18071 "Architectural Design Decisions in AI Agent Harnesses" (the OTHER source intake-132#record.

### Verdict

DIVE-VERIFIED. USE THIS PAPER AS AN ACCOUNTING MODEL, NEVER AS A MEASUREMENT OF OURS. Carry five facts. (1) It does NOT answer "what fraction of tokens is orchestration" - nobody has published that number, and this paper explicitly leaves cross-harness token measurement to future work; the only defensible share-shaped statement is the FLOOR: on one enterprise workload at least 38% of a naive loop's tokens bought no quality. (2) Its -38%/-41% is an UPPER BOUND, not a like-for-like delta: three token sinks (helper-model summarization, helper-model side work, sub-agent exploration) exist only in the harness arm and are described as "off the paying loop" with no statement of whether they are counted; and the sub-agent capability has no baseline arm while the paper claims both arms ran the same 22 prompts. (3) The DURABLE part is arithmetic and survives the vendor provenance intact: p_in_eff = p_in(1 - h(1-kappa)) with kappa about 0.1, the S/H/G/R/U decomposition, O(k^2)-to-O(k) history replay, and CPM/quality-per-dollar as the KPI that makes token maxing visible. (4) OUR FILED ACTIONABLE MUST BE SPLIT IN TWO: the pricing half is computable on existing journals today with zero compute (61 Claude Code transcripts, 45,902 usage records, h = 0.9845, input:output 350.5:1 - our plane is 3.5x more input-dominated than the ~100:1 this paper cites), while the coordination-SHARE half needs a per-turn purpose label added at WRITE time - a 32-author team measuring exactly this produced no share number without one, which is the whole belief-kernel write-side argument restated in another domain. (5) On topology it is an argument, not evidence: in-thread shared-transcript coordination is a token multiplier by construction, firewalled delegation with a capped summary and a metadata sidecar is the cheaper shape, and durable zero-token suspension beats polling - all unmeasured here. NOT to carry: -38%, -41%, 14.2k->8.8k, $0.21->$0.12, the 99.9% cache figure (one identical-prefix call in their repo), the $1.08M/year (arithmetic on their blended rates), or Table 1's ratings of Claude Code and Hermes as measurements. Do not wire into the belief kernel - vendor self-evaluation, n=22, single pair, no release, no replication. One caution that transfers even without numbers: richer orchestration REGRESSED quality on the three smaller models in orchestration-heavy capabilities and sub-agent delegation collapsed to 0.42-0.45 on the fast tier, so orchestration structure is a load a weak model can fail - relevant before handing coordination duties to our smaller local models.
Not not_applicable: it is squarely in domain and points at open local tasks. Not adopt_component: there is no component — no code, no data, no released traces; Appendix D is a prospective checklist and the harness is a closed multi-tenant SaaS runtime. Not new_opportunity: the mechanisms it inventories are already programs here (DCP, context-folding, repl-turn-efficiency, the session-bus wake work). Not already_integrated: we have no per-task token accounting surface at all, which is the one column the paper says everybody fails. adopt_patterns is exact — take the ACCOUNTING (Eq. 2 taxonomy, Eq. 4 with kappa=0.1, Definition 1, CPM/quality-per-dollar, "the meter lives in the same layer that spends the tokens") and the six mechanism families as a design checklist; take NONE of the numbers.

## intake-1334#record — Less Is More: Fast and Accurate Reasoning with Cross-Head Unified Sparse Attention

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07101
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: kv_cache, context_management, inference_serving, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1315#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Existing sparse-attention masks fail specifically on REASONING because selection error compounds over generation length, not context length: on an AIME-24 trace with a 4K token budget and generation up to 32K tokens on Qwen3-8B, TidalDecode holds only ~75% attention recall and StreamingLLM falls BELOW 65%, while the same budgets preserve accuracy on needle-in-a-haystack retrieval (Section 2.2, Figure 1).
   - *unaffected*: Verbatim in Section 2.2 with the setup verbatim in the Figure 1 caption. The narrowings were already in the Stage-1 claim text: n=1 problem, recall not accuracy, and the StreamingLLM sink/window split is NOT FOUND IN SOURCE.
   - anchor `Section 2.2 "Failure of Existing Sparse Attention in Reasoning", second paragraph` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2 sha256 f8dce886fdc7: "As shown in Figure 1(a), both StreamingLLM and TidalDecode exhibit substantial attention recall degradation on the AIME-24 benchmark, with recall deteriorating steadily as generation length increases. TidalDecode achieves only ~75% recall, while StreamingLLM falls below 65%."
   - anchor `Figure 1 caption (the ONLY statement of the setup)` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "Figure 1(a) compares cumulative average attention recall among StreamingLLM (Xiao et al., 2023), TidalDecode (Yang et al., 2024), and LessIsMore on an AIME-24 reasoning task, using a token budget of 4K and generation length up to 32K tokens on Qwen3-8B."
   - anchor `Section 2.2, third paragraph - the governing variable` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "The key distinction is generation length. Reasoning tasks involve orders of magnitude more decoding steps than retrieval tasks, causing even small selection errors to compound over time. Once critical tokens are omitted, subsequent reasoning steps cannot recover, leading to cascading logical erro..."
1. Token importance in reasoning is a GLOBAL, cross-head property, not a head-local one: the ground-truth top-4K token sets overlap heavily both within a KV group and across all 32 heads of Qwen3-8B at layer 4, and the overlap persists across layers and decoding steps (Section 2.3, Figures 2, 9, 10).
   - *unaffected*: Section 2.3 verbatim, with persistence across steps shown in Figures 9 and 10. Keep the caveat that it is Layer 4 of one model presented as heat maps with no overlap coefficient reported.
2. Reasoning attention has a stable PROPORTIONAL recency structure - the ratio of recent tokens to total critical tokens stays approximately constant across decoding steps - so a fixed-size sliding window (StreamingLLM's design) is the wrong shape; a fixed FRACTION r of the budget is the right one (Section 2.4, Section 3.3).
   - *unaffected*: Section 2.4 and A.6 verbatim including the explicit rejection of a fixed window size. NOT QUANTIFIED anywhere - "approximately constant" is read off heat maps.
   - anchor `Section 2.4, final paragraph - the explicit rejection of StreamingLLM's window shape` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "Prior approaches, such as StreamingLLM, recognize the importance of recent tokens by maintaining a fixed sliding window (Xiao et al., 2023). However, a static window size does not adapt to different token budgets and fails to reflect the stable proportional structure observed in reasoning attention."
3. LessIsMore = TidalDecode + (i) union-then-globally-rank the per-head top-k proposals into ONE index set shared by all heads and reused across layers, (ii) reserve r=0.25 of the budget for the most recent tokens, (iii) 4 attention sinks. It is training-free, needs only one re-selection layer, and matches or exceeds full attention from a 4K budget on AIME-24/25, MATH500 and GPQA-Diamond across Qwen3-4/8/14B and DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B (Section 3, Tables 5 and 6).
   - *narrowed*: "Matches or exceeds full attention" holds from a 4K budget, not from 2K: Table 5's 2K column loses in 5 of 8 model x benchmark cells and the conclusion's "full accuracy on AIME-24 with a 2K token budget" is 73.00 vs 74.48. All AIME deltas are inside one SE at n=30; the accuracy claim is established on MATH500 and GPQA-Diamond only. Also add, from the code, the budget decomposition the paper never gives (K=4096, r=0.25, sink=4 -> 4 + 3072 + 1020) and, from Section A.5, that the re-selection layer is a per-model hyperparameter worth up to 20 pp, tuned on a retrieval task.
   - anchor `Section 4.1 "Baselines", final paragraph - the ONLY configuration statement, and note StreamingLLM is not configured` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "In all experiments, LessIsMore uses a static recency-window ratio of r=0.25, with an ablation study on the effect of r reported in Section A.1.2. We additionally reserve four tokens as attention sinks in all experiments."
   - anchor `src/tidal_build/modify_qwen3_lim.py, qwen_tidal_attention_forward, token-selection branch (the budget decomposition, absent from the paper)` @ repo fa3d23a45fde680ac220394e7c569f5e98ca144e: "middle_budget = int(token_budget * tidal_ratio); most_recent_amount = token_budget - middle_budget; if most_recent_amount < attention_sink: attention_sink = 0 else: most_recent_amount -= attention_sink; assert middle_budget + attention_sink + most_recent_amount == token_budget -> at K=4096, r=0.2..."
   - anchor `Section 4.2 - average full-attention generation lengths per benchmark (the number that sets the eviction regime)` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "For challenging AIME-24 and -25 tasks, experiments span token budgets of 2K, 4K, 6K, and 8K where the Full Attention model solves problems with an average reasoning length of 15K and 17K tokens, respectively. ... A similar trend holds for GPQA (Full Attention 8K vs. 8K-18K under baselines) and MA..."
   - anchor `/mnt/raid0/llm/llama.cpp @ 0db32c06e3e5, src/llama-kv-compress.h:15-35 and src/llama-kv-compress.cpp:326,334 - our scorer has NO recency reservation` @ production-consolidated-v9, read-only 2026-09-07: "struct llama_kv_compress_params { float compression_ratio = 0.50f; int n_future = 128; int n_sink = 4; bool use_covariance = true; bool compact_positions = false; std::vector<float> layer_weights; }; ... std::vector<float> sorted(scores.begin() + params.n_sink, scores.end()); ... for (int p = par..."
   - anchor `/mnt/raid0/llm/llama.cpp @ 0db32c06e3e5, tools/server/server-context.cpp:5361-5376 and tools/server/server-task.h:177 - the exposed server knobs` @ production-consolidated-v9, read-only 2026-09-07: "ea_params.compression_ratio = 1.0f - task.slot_action.keep_ratio; ea_params.n_sink = task.slot_action.keep_first; ea_params.n_future = task.slot_action.n_future; ea_params.use_covariance = ...; ea_params.layer_weights = ...; // server-task.h:177 int keep_first = 8; // sink tokens protected from e..."
4. Both halves of the design are load-bearing and each fails alone: cross-head selection with 0% recent window raises recall over TidalDecode yet still FAILS to reach the correct answer, and 100% recent (i.e. pure sliding window) has the LOWEST recall of any arm; only 25/50/75% recent combined with cross-head selection solve the task, with 25% highest-recall (Section A.1.2, Figure 8).
   - *unaffected*: Section A.1.2 verbatim. Load-bearing for us: it is a direct experiment on the configuration our deployed Expected Attention scorer implements (unified cross-head score ranking, 0% recency reserved). Keep the caveat that "solve the task" is binary on one problem and no recall values are printed.
   - anchor `Section A.1.2 "Effect of Recent Window Ratio" (Figure 8; AIME-24, 4K budget, 32K generation, Qwen3-8B)` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "Only configurations that combine a recent window with Cross-Head Selection (25%, 50%, 75%) successfully solve the task. Using only recent tokens (r=100%) yields the lowest recall by discarding essential long-range context. TidalDecode improves recall yet still fails to reach the correct answer, a..."
5. The efficiency win is real but modest and context-length-dependent: 1.09x-1.58x end-to-end TBT vs full attention at 16K-64K context (Figure 6a), 1.3x-1.72x sparse-attention kernel latency vs TidalDecode, and - critically for a memory-bound host - LessIsMore does NOT reduce KV memory at all (8.38 MB, same as full attention and every other selection method), whereas StreamingLLM's eviction gives 1.04 MB at identical FLOPs and identical 20.1 us kernel latency (Table 4).
   - *narrowed*: The kernel numbers are confirmed to the digit, but the end-to-end speedup has THREE inconsistent values for the same quantity (1.09-1.58x / 1.100-1.427x / 1.11-1.51x) across Figure 6a, Table 1 and Table 3, on two different GPUs, and the abstract takes the largest; the v1 headline for the same axis was 1.1x average. The memory finding is the one to carry: LessIsMore's on-device memory (8.38 MB) is IDENTICAL to full attention, and StreamingLLM at 1.04 MB matches LessIsMore's FLOPs, G2S transfer and 20.1 us latency exactly.
   - anchor `Table 4 (2K budget, 16K context, DeepSeek-R1-Distilled-LLaMA-8B, FlashInfer) - FLOPs / G2S transfer / on-device memory / latency` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "LessIsMore 1.05M | 1.04MB | 8.38MB | 20.1us ; TidalDecode 1.05M | 2.34MB | 8.38MB | 32.1us ; Quest/SeerAttn-R 1.05M | 2.34MB | 8.38MB | 32.1us ; StreamingLLM 1.05M | 1.04MB | 1.04MB | 20.1us ; Full Attention 8.40M | 8.38MB | 8.38MB | 76.4us"
   - anchor `Section A.2.1, on the StreamingLLM row of Table 4` @ arXiv:2508.07101v2: "StreamingLLM achieves optimal FLOP and memory-transfer numbers but performs poorly on reasoning accuracy due to its static attention structure, making it less suitable for long-form reasoning tasks."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + dive: entry born dive-verified. Nothing about the source needed correcting after first reading because the entry was authored against the primary source, but SIX things a reader would otherwise get wrong are now pinned. (1) VERSION: v2 (2026-04-27) is current; v1 (2025-08-09) has a different title AND a different headline (1.1x average, not "up to 1.6x"). The companion repo and the HuggingFace page still show the v1 title. (2) THE STREAMINGLLM CONFIGURATION IS NOT IN THE SOURCE - not the sink size, not the window, not anywhere, and there is no StreamingLLM code in the release; only the 4K total budget is stated. Any 4-sink/4092-window reading is INFERRED from the canonical StreamingLLM convention, not reported. (3) THE GOVERNING VARIABLE IS GENERATION LENGTH, not context length or window size - this is the transferable finding and it reframes PS-1. (4) THE PREFIX-WIDE-SINK DISTINCTION IS NEARLY VACUOUS ON REASONING because the prompt is ~10^2 tokens, so this measurement DOES bear on Prefix Sliding's configuration - which narrows the bearing entry's reading. (5) TABLE 4 SETTLES THE EFFICIENCY QUESTION IN OUR FAVOUR: at equal budget the sink+window mask matches the best selection method on FLOPs, on global-to-shared transfer and on kernel latency (20.1 us both) and uses 8x less KV memory, so the only open question about our --kv-streaming-* path is accuracy. (6) THE ACCURACY EVIDENCE IS MUCH WEAKER THAN THE ABSTRACT: every AIME comparison is inside one SE at n=30 with no error bars printed, the 2K budget loses in 5 of 8 cells, and three mutually inconsistent numbers are given for the one end-to-end speedup. NEW LOCAL FINDING: our deployed Expected Attention scorer (src/llama-kv-compress.h/.cpp, server keep_first=8) protects only initial sink tokens and reserves NOTHING for recency - the exact arm Section A.1.2 shows failing on reasoning despite improved recall. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv 2410.05076 - TidalDecode: Fast and Accurate LLM Decoding with Position Persistent Sparse Atten; arXiv 2406.10774 - Quest: Query-Aware Sparsity for Efficient Long-Context LLM Inference; arXiv 2506.08889 - SeerAttention-R: Sparse Attention Adaptation for Long Reasoning (ICLR 2026 poster.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY: (1) sink+window masks degrade on reasoning as a function of GENERATION LENGTH relative to W, not context length - a mask that looks fine at 5K generated tokens has simply not been tested at W=4096; design every PS-1 arm to generate >> W or it measures nothing. (2) The one hard number is StreamingLLM attention recall <65% at a 4K budget, Qwen3-8B, AIME-24, 32K generation - ONE problem, RECALL not accuracy, and the sink/window split is NOT STATED anywhere in the paper or the code. (3) Table 4 is the efficiency ruling and it favours what we already ship: at equal budget the sink+window mask matches the best selection method on FLOPs, on global-to-shared transfer and on kernel latency (20.1 us both) while using 8x less KV memory; the ONLY open question about --kv-streaming-* is accuracy. (4) Section A.1.2 is a testable prediction against production code: unified cross-head score-ranked selection with 0% recency reserved improves recall AND STILL FAILS the reasoning task - and that is exactly the configuration of llama_kv_compress (n_sink only, server keep_first=8, no recency field). (5) A prefix-wide sink is nearly indistinguishable from a 4-token sink on AIME-class workloads because the prompt is ~10^2 tokens; it differentiates only where the PROMPT is long, which is the decode-light regime where the mask saves least. (6) The paper is an ICML 2026 poster (icml.cc/virtual/2026/poster/61079) - it has passed peer review. DO NOT CARRY: "LessIsMore achieves full accuracy at a 2K budget" (loses in 5 of 8 Table-5 cells; the honest floor is 4K); any AIME-24/25 delta as a result (all inside one SE at n=30, no error bars anywhere in the paper); "1.6x end-to-end speedup" as a measured value (three inconsistent sets - 1.09-1.58x, 1.100-1.427x, 1.11-1.51x - on two different GPUs, and v1 said 1.1x average for the same axis); any KV MEMORY saving (LessIsMore holds the full cache: 8.38 MB, identical to full attention); any StreamingLLM ACCURACY number on reasoning (there is none - the only StreamingLLM accuracy in the paper is a 10K retrieval task on an MHA model); the released repo as a performance artifact (it materialises dense attention and masks it in Python); and the method as portable to v9 (selection-based, needs a gather-attention op on CPU and HIP plus a per-model tuned re-selection layer worth up to 20 pp).
Two patterns transfer and the mechanism does not. TRANSFER 1 (code, zero kernel): add a recency reservation to llama_kv_compress. Our deployed Expected Attention scorer ranks every non-sink position purely on predicted future attention and protects only the first keep_first tokens; Section A.1.2 is a direct experiment saying that a purely score-ranked set with 0% reserved recency raises attention recall yet still fails the reasoning task, and that reserving 25% of the kept budget for the most recent positions is what makes it work. That is roughly 20 lines in src/llama-kv-compress.cpp on an experimental branch and a new keep_recent knob in slot_action, and it directly sharpens the OPEN S8 sweep (keep_ratio x layer_weights -> keep_ratio x layer_weights x recency_ratio). TRANSFER 2 (experimental design, zero compute): the governing variable for sink+window degradation is GENERATION LENGTH relative to W, not context length - which retroactively explains why our 2026-07-20 floor sweep (-c 384, -n 768, windows 128/192/256) and Prefix Sliding's own MATH500 result (~5K generation, W=4096) both sit in the regime where almost nothing is ever evicted. DECLINE the mechanism itself: LessIsMore is SELECTION-based (full KV retained, per-step top-k over q.K^T at a tuned re-selection layer, then a gather-based sparse attention for all downstream layers). llama.cpp has no per-step token-selection attention path at all - both of its KV-reduction paths are eviction (positional --kv-streaming-* at context shift, and one-shot Expected Attention compaction at a slot boundary) - so this needs a new gather-attention op on both the CPU and HIP backends plus score extraction at a designated layer, on a FROZEN production kernel. And per Table 4 it would buy zero KV memory. Not not_applicable: it is open-source, self-hostable, and the patterns are actionable today. Not adopt_component: nothing here is droppable into v9.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/attention-matching-kv-compaction.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/streaming-llm-baseline.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/triattention-kv-selection.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`

### Claims (as verified)

0. A protocol-guided, source-grounded study of a FROZEN corpus of 70 agent-system projects (corpus frozen 23 March 2026; 67 open-source repos + 3 public-evidence comparison cases; 6 first-party lab products, 64 community) yields FIVE recurring design-decision dimensions and no more: subagent architecture, context management, tool systems, safety mechanisms, orchestration. (Abstract; §3.3; §3.9; Appendix A.2 Table 15; Appendix A.3 Table 16.)

Marginal distributions (all three tables sum to exactly 70; arithmetic re-checked by this pass): subagent architecture — None 21/30.0%, Basic Spawn 5/7.1%, Tool-based Delegation 12/17.1%, Pipeline 1/1.4%, Orchestrator-Worker 13/18.6%, Multi-level Recursive 9/12.9%, Swarm 4/5.7%, Event-driven 5/7.1% (Table 4). Context management — Context Window 3/4.3%, LLM Summarization 4/5.7%, File Persistence 16/22.9%, Vector/RAG 7/10.0%, Hierarchical 12/17.1%, Hybrid 19/27.1%, Enterprise 9/12.9% (Table 5). Tool systems — Registry modal at 24/34.3%, MCP-first 10/14.3%, Minimalist 8/11.4% (Table 6).

The observed spectrum runs from no persistence, to session-only in-memory state, to file-backed storage, and finally to more structured database- or vector-backed retrieval."

Three headline co-occurrences, reported as SUPPORT/LIFT from "internal cross-project summaries" that are NOT released: subagent complexity -> memory sophistication (support 0.73, lift 1.8); container isolation -> policy-structured security (support 0.89, lift 3.4; "100% of container-isolated projects implement policy engines versus 23% without"); MCP-first -> stronger discovery (support 0.62, lift 2.8); plus project scale -> complexity (0.68, 1.5). Tables 10 and 12, §5.2.

Every support/lift value and every backing score (memory/discovery/security) traces to "the internal cross-project summaries"; §3.4 confirms the encoded dataset is withheld; the score scales and the A/E/F/G/H labels are undefined. 4. Three NON-co-occurrences (the paper's explicit negative results): programming language does not determine architectural pattern; use case does not fix design complexity; capability growth does not automatically produce safety maturity. §5.3.

Disagreements were resolved through discussion and renewed source inspection, yielding 94% initial field-level agreement before final consensus coding."
