   - anchor `2604.18071v1 Appendix B Table 17, Subagent architecture row` @ arXiv:2604.18071v1: "Additional notes recorded creation style, nesting depth, and communication boundary."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 Stage-2b combined ingest+dive (born dive-verified). WHAT THIS PASS ESTABLISHES, so none of it is re-derived: (1) THE ANSWER TO THE DISPATCHED QUESTION IS A CLEAN, WELL-EVIDENCED NEGATIVE — the supervision-branch topology is not a design point in this taxonomy, and neither is the model-context-excluded durable entry, and neither is any hook/wake/injection mechanism; all verified as zero-occurrence against the RAW HTML, not a derived text. (2) THE NEGATIVE IS SHARPER THAN ABSENCE: the corpus CONTAINS pi-mono, hermes-agent and claude-code-src — the three harnesses that own the turnless-append/supervision primitives per intake-1325#record, intake-454#record and intake-473#record — and the five-dimension grid returned only structural spawn-pattern labels for all three. Appendix B shows why: "communication boundary" was coded and then never reported. (3) BUT THE NEGATIVE MUST NOT BE OVERREAD, and this is the correction that matters most: the silence is a property of THIS paper's granularity, not of the literature. arXiv:2609.00006 (3 months later, 11 systems, 7 subsystems, 29 patterns) covers hooks (42), injection (33), steering and notification, enumerates Claude Code's and Codex's lifecycle-hook vocabularies verbatim including PreCompact, and independently corroborates the intake-1325#record dive's PreCompact finding from a different harness. Anyone citing 2604.18071 as evidence that hook-based wake is exotic would be overclaiming. (4) VERDICT IS superseded, NOT worth_investigating: 2609.00006 out-competes it on granularity, recency, longitudinality and — decisively — on covering the exact mechanism class this one omits; secondary successor Rombaut "Inside the Scaffold". 2609.00006 does not cite this paper at all. (5) CREDIBILITY CAPPED AT 2 with four defects the rubric does not price: the encoded matrix is withheld so every support/lift is unfalsifiable; the "94% field-level agreement / disagreements resolved through discussion" describes an inter-rater procedure a single-author paper cannot have performed as written, and §7.3 concedes chance-corrected agreement was never computed; the memory/discovery/security scores and the A/E/F/G/H pattern letters carrying the whole co-occurrence argument are undefined; and the Claude Code row is coded from an unpinned LEAKED snapshot. (6) ONE INTERNAL CONTRADICTION FOUND: openclaw is Pattern-4-coded in Table 11 and Pattern-2-assigned in §6.3. (7) WHAT SURVIVES AND IS WORTH KEEPING: the marginal tables are arithmetically sound (all four re-summed to exactly 70); the three non-co-occurrences in §5.3 are honest negative results independent of the withheld magnitudes; and Table 16 is a genuinely reusable link-pinned inventory of 70 harnesses. (8) LOCATOR DEFECT IN THE BEARING ENTRY'S LEDGER, verified: intake-1325#record's derived-actionable #2 assigns OBS-11/FW-2 to loop-owned-fleet-implementation.md, where both appear 0 times; they live at session-bus-thin-dispatcher.md:2882 and :2905. A colliding OBS-11/OBS-12 series in non-inference-backlog.md:251-252 is the likely cause. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv:2609.00006 "Harness Engineering: Anatomy, Architecture, and Evolution of Coding Agents — A Sou; Rombaut, "Inside the Scaffold" — source-code taxonomy of 13 open-source coding-agent scaffolds, 12 d; Preprints 202604.0428 "Agent Harness for LLM Agents: A Survey" — github.com/Gloriaameng/Awesome-Agen; arXiv:2606.25447 "The Interplay of Harness Design and Post-Training in LLM Agents" (2 index hits, no; arXiv:2604.11378 "From Agent Loops to Structured Graphs: A Scheduler-Theoretic Framework for LLM Age.

### Verdict

CARRY THE NEGATIVE, DO NOT OVERREAD IT. Three things to carry: (1) THE SUPERVISION-BRANCH TOPOLOGY IS NOT A RECOGNISED DESIGN POINT. The widest published harness census — 70 projects, frozen 23 Mar 2026 — has no category for a second short-lived model conversation that triages wake-ups for a long-lived main, and no category for reaching a running loop from outside at all. Its subagent axis is eight ways of SPAWNING SEPARATE AGENTS; a second conversation inside one logical agent is not on the spectrum. This is usable as A-6 evidence for the AFFIRMATIVE: our prompt-driven, single-threaded, message-only coordinator shape was a default nobody had categorised, not a tradeoff we consciously lost. (2) THE MODEL-CONTEXT-EXCLUDED DURABLE ENTRY IS NEITHER "UNUSUAL" NOR "COMMON" HERE — IT IS UNMEASURED. Context management is coded on storage backend, compression, persistence scope and token awareness; a durable entry deliberately withheld from the prompt codes as ordinary "File Persistence", indistinguishable from a log. Absence of the measurement is not absence of the mechanism. (3) THE SHARP FORM OF (1): the corpus CONTAINS pi-mono, hermes-agent and claude-code-src — the three harnesses that own the primitive — and the instrument saw nothing, because Appendix B coded "communication boundary" and never reported it. WHAT MUST NOT BE CARRIED: do NOT cite this paper as evidence that hook-based wake is exotic or unrecognised — that silence is this taxonomy's coarseness, and arXiv:2609.00006 shows hooks are a mainstream, actively CONVERGING extension substrate (Codex copies Claude Code's hook vocabulary verbatim, including PreCompact), which independently corroborates the intake-1325#record dive from a different harness. Do NOT cite any support, lift, confidence, memory/discovery/security score or A-E-F-G-H pattern label from this paper: the encoded matrix is withheld, the scales are undefined, and the reliability figure (94%) describes multi-rater discussion in a single-author paper. Do NOT treat the claude-code-src row as an authority on Claude Code — it is an unpinned leaked snapshot. Do NOT wire this into the belief kernel: there is no measurement here, only prevalence. WHAT IS SAFE TO CITE: the five-dimension vocabulary as a SELECTION CHECKLIST for the open HS-4 gate; the three non-co-occurrences in §5.3 (language does not determine architecture; use case does not fix complexity; capability growth does not imply safety maturity) — the last being directly applicable to how we read any harness candidate's feature list; and Table 16 as a 70-row inventory. GO READ 2609.00006 NEXT — it is the source that actually answers what this one was surfaced to answer.
In-domain, openly available, and not out of scope — but out-competed on every axis this repo cares about by arXiv:2609.00006 "Harness Engineering: Anatomy, Architecture, and Evolution of Coding Agents — A Source-Code Study of Eleven Systems" (Barbaste, Darrigol, Vu, Wiltberger), which is (i) FINER (7 subsystems x 29 patterns vs 5 dimensions), (ii) LONGITUDINAL (90 days of source diffs, not one frozen snapshot), (iii) mechanism-level where this paper is category-level, and decisively (iv) it COVERS THE EXACT MECHANISM CLASS 2604.18071 omits — 42 occurrences of "hook", 33 of "inject", plus steering and notification, including verbatim lifecycle-hook vocabularies (PreToolUse, PreCompact, SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, SubagentStart) and Pi's append-only JSONL session tree. Secondary successor for taxonomy breadth: Rombaut, "Inside the Scaffold" (13 scaffolds, 12 dimensions). What 2604.18071 still uniquely supplies and should be cited FOR, and only for: the widest published corpus census (70 vs 11/13/23) and the three explicit non-co-occurrences in §5.3.

## intake-1336#record — Cacheable by Design? Training Mixture-of-Experts Routers for Locality Against the Edge Memory-Bandwidth Wall: A Pre-Registered Negative Result with a Systems Measurement Study

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18261
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty high, relevance high; categories: moe_optimization, inference_serving, hardware_optimization, local_inference, benchmark_methodology, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1328#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. llama-moe-trace is a ~120-line addition to llama.cpp's eval-callback that records the router's top-k selection (the ggml tensor named ffn_moe_topk) for every layer and token with the model weights untouched, emitting (layer, n_tokens, k, ids...) binary records; ffn_moe_topk is a NON-CONTIGUOUS view (the top-k slice of a 128-wide argsort), so a flat ggml_backend_tensor_get reads whole argsort rows and produces "perfectly uniform garbage - every expert appearing exactly k times, with sub-chance reuse", fixed by a stride-honoring row-by-row copy.
   - *unaffected*: Tool description exact, and independently strengthened: the non-contiguous-view hazard it documents is verifiably present in our own frozen v9 tree (ggml_argsort_top_k is a ggml_view_4d over a full argsort), so the warning is actionable here, not incidental.
   - anchor `Section 4, Measuring Routing Structure` @ arXiv:2608.18261v1 / paper.tex @ 03366ef1: "No serving engine exposes this, so we built llama-moe-trace, a ~120-line addition to llama.cpp's eval-callback that records the router's top-k selection (ffn_moe_topk) for every layer and token, with weights untouched."
   - anchor `llama-moe-trace/moe-trace.cpp:29-56 (moe_trace_cb)` @ 03366ef1cb986a7ac6067c840f8ac58d8e5c8595: "const bool match = strncmp(t->name, "ffn_moe_topk-", 13) == 0; ... for (int32_t i = 0; i < n_tokens; i++) { ggml_backend_tensor_get(t, ids.data() + (size_t) i * k, (size_t) i * t->nb[1], k * sizeof(int32_t)); }"

1. Routing profile of Qwen3-30B-A3B (48 layers, 128 experts, top-8; Q4_K_M GGUF) over 8,000 tokens each of prose, code, math and medical text: P(expert reused at next token) = 0.444 vs chance 0.223, i.e. 2.0x; 95% of traffic flows through 52.5% of experts; cross-domain expert-usage similarity puts code at 0.11-0.16 against the other three vs 0.33-0.42 among prose/math/medical.
   - *unaffected*: Recomputed end-to-end from the released traces: 0.4439 / 0.2227 / 1.99x and 0.5247 against the paper's 0.444 / 0.223 / 2.0x and 52.5%. Add the definitional fact the paper leaves in the code: "chance" is the marginal-skew-aware independence baseline sum_e p_e^2 / sum_e p_e, not k/N - so the 2.0x is the temporal residue ON TOP of a marginal distribution already 3.6x more concentrated than uniform.

2. Replaying those traces through a simulated per-layer expert cache AT LAYER 24, CODE DOMAIN and a 13.4% fast-memory budget: LRU serves 65.9% of requests, LFU 60.1%, static pinning 59.2%, and a clairvoyant Belady oracle 79.1% - LRU beats static pinning "because the exploitable signal is temporal".

3. The pre-registered gate is JOINT and per-RQ, not a bare perplexity bar: RQ1 requires >=30% reduction in LRU miss/token at 25% capacity AND validation perplexity within +1% of the balance-loss baseline; RQ2 requires >=90% static-pin hit@50% within-domain AND perplexity within +2%. Every configuration fails - RQ1 at lambda in {0.02, 0.03, 0.05 (two seeds), 0.20} (miss reduction +15/+24/+59/+60/+72%, PPL cost +0.3/+1.8/+2.1/+3.1/+119%) and RQ2 on quality (static hit@50% 0.991 PASSES, PPL +3.1% FAILS).

The entry must not compress it to "the <=1% perplexity gate": +1% is RQ1's quality half only, RQ2's is +2%, and lambda=0.02 failed on the MISS half (+15% < 30%) while passing quality (+0.3%). Also record that lambda 0.02 and 0.03 are post-hoc refinements of a pre-registered sweep of {0.01, 0.05, 0.2} (run_lambda_refine.sh) - they made the gate harder to clear, not easier, so this is a deviation to note rather than a flaw.

4. The locality tax does not shrink with scale: 137M +2.02% PPL for 59.3% miss reduction vs 340M +2.53% for 57.2%; and stacking training-free cache-aware tau-rerouting on top of a locality-trained model reaches 80% miss reduction at +2.4% PPL (137M) and 82% at +3.4% (340M), because "training co-adapts experts into mutually-substitutable neighbourhoods".

(ii) The author names the undertraining confound himself. (iii) The underlying runs/scale340-* and cacheaware.json artifacts are ABSENT from the release, so this is the one headline number I could not verify against primary data, and the appendix claim that "every number in this paper is regenerable" is false for it.

5. Path-Mapped Serving has a hard ceiling on this hardware: projected to the 235B, miss traffic falls to ~2.7 GB/token at LRU rates for ~0.9 tok/s (2-7x over baseline at full accuracy), but single-stream 10 tok/s needs ~95% hit rate, which requires ~52% of experts (~70 GB) resident - 4x the machine's fast memory. "Placement alone cannot reach interactive speed here."

On StickyMoE (arXiv:2607.08780, Kayyam 2026) the paper does NOT claim a contradiction: "We read the two as complementary but in tension: StickyMoE shows the mechanism can be quality-preserving on single-domain, sub-25M models; we show it is not free on a harder multi-domain 137M setting, and flag the corpus and scale dependence that would reconcile them."

The paper says "complementary but in tension" and its author renamed his own repository to avoid a StickyMoE name collision. Reattribute the contradiction claim away from the source: it was the bearing entry's reading, not the paper's position - and StickyMoE's own abstract ("less than 4% perplexity degradation") narrows the gap further.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-2b ingest+dive. Born dive-verified: all seven key_claims survive, five confirmed outright and two narrowed, none falsified - and Tables 1 and 2 were REGENERATED from the released primary traces rather than read (0.4439/0.2227/1.99x/52.47% vs the paper's 0.444/0.223/2.0x/52.5%; cache_code.csv yields 65.95/60.06/59.19/79.12% vs 65.9/60.1/59.2/79.1). What this dive CHANGES about the surrounding repo, not about this paper: (1) THE BEARING ENTRY'S "explicitly contradicting StickyMoE" IS WRONG and must be corrected in intake-1328#record's adversarial_pass row and its dive_corrections text - the paper says "complementary but in tension", the author renamed his repo to AVOID a StickyMoE collision, and StickyMoE's own abstract reports "less than 4% perplexity degradation", i.e. the same low-single-digit tax this paper measures. (2) The bearing entry's "<=1% perplexity gate" is half a gate: RQ1 is >=30% miss reduction AND +1% PPL, RQ2 is >=90% static-pin hit AND +2% PPL, and one arm failed on the miss half while passing quality. (3) The bearing entry's "zero-surgery router-telemetry tool" needs one qualifier: zero surgery to the MODEL, but a new file plus a CMake target and a BUILD, which the freeze forbids on production-consolidated-v9 - it is an experimental-branch item, additive-only, and I verified every API symbol it needs exists in v9 unchanged. (4) intake-1328#record actionable #5 must drop "int8": our checkpoints have 256 and 512 routed experts, which overflow int8; the tool already emits int32 and converts to int16, and the trace is 20-31 MB at this paper's scale, 0.7-1.0 GB at ljcleo's. (5) NEW AND INDEPENDENT OF THE BEARING ENTRY: from the released traces I measured that pooling four domains moves normalised entropy from 0.79-0.85 to 0.9453 and Gini from 0.62-0.71 to 0.3889 on the SAME model - so a single mixed-corpus cumulative histogram, which is exactly what our 2026-07-17 GLM artifact was, understates skew by roughly a factor of two at four domains. That is a SECOND defect in the closing measurement behind the near-uniform kill, independent of the missing-time-axis defect intake-1328#record found. (6) The paper's own two forward-looking headlines - the 340M scale rung and the 80%-at-+2.4% stacking result - are NOT reproducible from the release (runs/scale340-* and cacheaware.json are absent), and the 340M rung is not in the frozen pre-registration at all. (7) The "pre-registration" is a self-attestation: DESIGN.md, RESULTS.md and every run landed in one initial commit on 2026-08-10; there is no external registration or timestamp. (8) Qwen3-30B routing is NOT near-uniform per-layer (Gini 0.62-0.71), which contradicts intake-1193#record ProMoE's motivating premise for that model family. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv:2412.00099 - Mixture of Cache-Conditional Experts for Efficient Mobile Device Inference (Sklia; Oracle-MoE - Locality-preserving Routing in the Oracle Space for Memory-constrained LLM Inference (I; arXiv:2607.08780 - Sticky Routing / StickyMoE (Ali Kayyam, BrainChip Inc., 2026-06-12); arXiv:2607.00466 - ELDR: Expert-Locality-Aware Decode Routing for PD-Disaggregated MoE Serving (Choi.

### Verdict

ADOPT THE INSTRUMENT, SCOPE THE SYSTEMS RESULT OUT, AND DO NOT CARRY THE STICKYMOE FRAMING. (1) llama-moe-trace is the tap intake-1328#record filed as a build item, and it is ~80 lines of public llama.cpp API. Port it to an llama.cpp-experimental branch off current production - NEVER build in /mnt/raid0/llm/llama.cpp, which the freeze forbids - and note it is additive-only (one new file in examples/eval-callback/ plus a five-line CMake target), so promotion is trivial. Every symbol it needs exists unchanged in v9: cb_eval at common/common.cpp:1615-1616, the ffn_moe_topk name at src/llama-graph.cpp:1987. (2) COPY THE STRIDE-HONORING READ VERBATIM. ggml_argsort_top_k in our tree (ggml/src/ggml.c:5373) is a ggml_view_4d over a full argsort, so a flat ggml_backend_tensor_get returns garbage that LOOKS like a clean negative result - every expert appearing exactly k times with sub-chance reuse. Anyone writing this tap from scratch WILL hit that bug and may publish it as "routing is uniform". (3) CORRECT THE FILED ACTIONABLE: int8 is impossible for our fleet (256 and 512 routed experts overflow it); int16 is the right width, the tool already does this, and the traces are 20-31 MB at 32k tokens. (4) A v9-based tracer covers qwen35moe and qwen3next only - "qwen4exp" and "glm5next" appear nowhere in v9's src/, so Qwen3.8-Flash-Next and GLM-5.3-Flash need a newer tree. (5) THE 2026-07-17 NEAR-UNIFORM KILL NOW HAS TWO INDEPENDENT DEFECTS, not one: no time axis (intake-1328#record), and corpus mixing - pooling four domains on the same model moves Gini 0.62-0.71 -> 0.389 and entropy 0.79-0.85 -> 0.945. A mixed-calibration-corpus histogram cannot distinguish a uniform model from a strongly domain-specialised one. Rewrite the reopen trigger accordingly; the kill itself still stands on the bottleneck argument, which this paper does not touch. (6) DO NOT CARRY "explicitly contradicts StickyMoE" - the paper says "complementary but in tension" and StickyMoE's own abstract reports a <4% perplexity degradation, so the two agree on magnitude and differ on acceptability. Fix that sentence in intake-1328#record. (7) DO NOT CARRY "the <=1% perplexity gate" - the gate is joint and two-armed (+1% with >=30% miss reduction; +2% with >=90% static-pin hit). (8) DO NOT CITE Section 3 or Section 5 for anything about our host: 0.44 tok/s, 12 GB/token, the 2.4 GB/s SSD ceiling and the whole PMS projection are an 8 GB card behind a consumer NVMe, and Limitations (v) says the speed numbers are modelled, not deployed. The intake-1191#record/1193/1200 bottleneck kills are untouched and expert offload stays closed on this RAM-resident host. (9) DO NOT CITE Table 6 (scale) or the 80%-at-+2.4% stacking number as reproducible - runs/scale340-* and cacheaware.json are absent from the release, and the scale rung is post-hoc relative to the frozen pre-registration. (10) TREAT "pre-registered" AS SELF-ATTESTED: DESIGN.md and all results landed in a single initial commit; the internal evidence is honest but there is no external timestamp. (11) The cache row (65.9% LRU) is ONE LAYER of 48 and ONE domain - never quote it as a whole-model hit rate.
The component is llama-moe-trace/moe-trace.cpp (4,577 bytes, MIT) plus routing-lab/06_convert_gguf_trace.py. It is not a paper to cite - it is a tool to build on an experimental branch. I verified against the FROZEN v9 tree, read-only, that every symbol it needs is present: params.cb_eval / cb_eval_user_data plumbed at common/common.cpp:1615-1616; ggml_backend_sched_eval_callback typedef at ggml/include/ggml-backend.h:314; LLAMA_EXAMPLE_COMMON at common/arg.h:22; llama_memory_clear at include/llama.h:717; llama_get_memory at include/llama.h:560; common_init_from_params returning common_init_result_ptr at common/common.h:925 (matches the tool's llama_init->model()); examples/eval-callback/CMakeLists.txt has exactly the target shape the tool's README asks to be copied. The traced tensor name is emitted at src/llama-graph.cpp:1987 (cb(selected_experts, "ffn_moe_topk", il)). The FINDINGS split three ways: the routing-locality measurement is adoptable as method, the training result is a training-side negative we cannot act on (no pretraining here), and the PMS bandwidth ceiling is an 8 GB / SSD-tier result that does not apply to a RAM-resident host.

## intake-1337#record — BEAM 100K benchmark results - first end-to-end answer quality evaluation (MemPalace issue #125, with companion PR #168)

- **URL**: https://github.com/MemPalace/mempalace/issues/125
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, memory_augmented, search_retrieval, rag_alternatives, knowledge_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1330#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. THE RUN: BEAM 100K, all 20 conversations and all 400 probing questions, 1,051 rubric items per arm, five retrieval arms (Raw ChromaDB k=10; Hybrid keyword re-rank k=10; Hybrid+Claude-Haiku rerank k=10; AAAK k=50; AAAK+spec k=50), ~41-47 min per arm on Apple Silicon. GPT-5.4-mini at temperature 0.0 is BOTH the answer synthesiser AND the judge (disclosed by the author). Best arm is Raw ChromaDB at 49.0% overall; every MemPalace-specific mode scores lower; AAAK compression collapses to 26.2%.
   - *unaffected*: Every configuration fact in the issue prose is confirmed by the code, and the 1,051 figure is independently reproduced from BEAM's own rubric files, which also proves no question subsetting occurred. The one thing the prose omits is that the judge and synthesiser sharing a model is not merely disclosed but structural — a single (client, model) pair is threaded to both call sites, so they cannot diverge without a code change.

1. THE FOLD IS NOT BEAM'S FOLD. The headline is total_passed/total_checks where a rubric item counts as passed iff the judge verdict is >= 0.5 — i.e. a rubric-item-weighted MICRO-average of BINARISED verdicts, with 0.5 partial credit promoted to a full pass. BEAM's own report_results.py instead means the three-valued nugget scores within a question, means questions within an ability, and takes the UNWEIGHTED mean of the ten ability columns. On BEAM's fold the same five arms would headline 55.7 / 51.4 / 50.5 / 29.5 / 30.1, not 49.0 / 43.0 / 43.6 / 26.2 / 27.9.

Both halves are in code, and the arithmetic reproduces all five published headlines from the official rubric-item weights (two of them exact to the decimal). Carry the magnitude with the finding: on this dataset the fold choice alone is worth 6.7pp on the best arm, because event_ordering + summarization + contradiction_resolution hold 568 of 1,051 rubric items (54.0%) against 30% of the macro weight and are the three lowest-scoring abilities.

The script's BEAM_JUDGE_PROMPT (labelled "verbatim from BEAM paper") adds a "- QUESTION (what the user asked): <question>" line to EVALUATION INPUTS, retitles the section "RESPONSIVENESS REQUIREMENT (anchored to the QUESTION)", rewords its first line to "on-topic with respect to the QUESTION", and truncates the reason-field description; judge_rubric() substitutes the probing question. ### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + Stage-2b: this source is BORN dive-verified. All five key_claims CONFIRMED against the issue body (sha256 af4a82e1fba1), all 9 comments, PR #168 metadata, and the three benchmark files at head 475d3fbd241f03757e2eb7a7ed430040677ccf87, cross-checked against BEAM at b2da22ea. What this dive ESTABLISHES that exists in NO source, including the artifact''s own prose and intake-1330#record''s dive: (1) THE HEADLINE IS A DIFFERENT FOLD, PROVEN ARITHMETICALLY. 49.0% is total_passed/total_checks over 1,051 rubric items, where a rubric item passes iff the judge verdict is >= 0.5 (beam_100k_bench.py:625-627, printed at :673-674, stated in the PR as "515/1051 rubric checks passed (49.0%)"). BEAM''s fold is the unweighted mean of ten ability columns of three-valued nugget means (report_results.py:37-66). I reproduced all five published headlines from the official rubric-item weights (48.83 / 43.04 / 43.59 / 26.31 / 28.10 vs reported 49.0 / 43.0 / 43.6 / 26.2 / 27.9) and the macro alternative (55.70 / 51.40 / 50.50 / 29.50 / 30.10). THE FOLD ALONE IS WORTH 6.7pp on the best arm, because event_ordering (213) + summarization (195) + contradiction_resolution (160) hold 54.0% of all rubric items but 30% of the macro weight and are the three worst abilities. Two deviations in opposite directions — binarisation inflates, rubric-item weighting deflates — so 49.0% is not a monotone transform of any BEAM number. (2) THE "EXACT OFFICIAL JUDGE PROMPT" IS PATCHED, AND THE PATCH IS THE FIX intake-1330#record PRESCRIBED. Diff against prompts.py:11547 shows exactly three substantive changes: a "- QUESTION (what the user asked): <question>" line added to EVALUATION INPUTS, the RESPONSIVENESS REQUIREMENT retitled and re-anchored to the QUESTION, and the reason-field description shortened. judge_rubric:329-333 substitutes the question. An independent engineer wrote and shipped, in April 2026, the exact canonical-judge-suite-revamp.md patch this wave proposed — do not re-derive it, transcribe it. (3) THE CLAMP IS NOT BEAM''S. The issue calls ">= 0.75 -> 1.0, >= 0.25 -> 0.5" "standard BEAM methodology". BEAM does float(response[''score'']) with no clamping; grep over compute_metrics.py + report_results.py at b2da22ea returns 0 hits for 0.75 and one for 0.25 (a BLEU weight). On schema-compliant output the clamp is a no-op; its real function is to silently rescue off-schema verdicts. (4) THE READER SAW HALF THE CONVERSATION. _beam_utils gates on `role == "user"` at parquet->JSON conversion, so every assistant turn is discarded before indexing; one document per message, no chunking, top-10, closed-book synthesis prompt, max_tokens=512. (5) THE RUN USED THE UNMODIFIED OFFICIAL RUBRIC SET AND NO SUBSET — I computed the per-ability rubric-item counts over all 20 chats/100K files at b2da22ea (40/160/213/92/59/42/101/74/195/75 = 1,051 over 400 questions) and they match PR #168''s Total column cell-for-cell. (6) THE ABSTENTION CONFOUND IS NOW DEMONSTRATED ON A CONTROLLED FIVE-ARM LADDER: the arm with the worst overall (AAAK 26.2%, temporal 1%) has the BEST abstention (85%). Better evidence than BEAM''s Table 1, which varies system and backbone together. (7) A SECOND INDEPENDENT END-TO-END BEAM RUN EXISTS (AxDSan/mnemosyne, 2026-05-18, Llama 3.3 70B + DeepSeek V4 Flash judge, Abstention 100.0%, and it states judge non-comparability outright). #125 is the FIRST and the only one with a published harness, but not the only one — narrow intake-1330#record''s adversarial_pass[0] wording accordingly. (8) TRANSPORT IS CHEAPER THAN BEAM''S: no hard-coded base_url anywhere, so OPENAI_BASE_URL alone repoints both synthesis and judging at llama-server. Verify llama-server honours response_format json_object before relying on it — a parse failure scores 0.0 silently (judge_rubric:346-347). (9) PROVENANCE: PR #168 is OPEN and UNMERGED as of 2026-09-07 despite the maintainer inviting it on 2026-04-07; the repository was renamed from milla-jovovich/mempalace to MemPalace/mempalace; and the issue dates BEAM to 2024 when it is arXiv:2510.27246 (Oct 2025, ICLR 2026). Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): https://github.com/AxDSan/mnemosyne/blob/main/docs/beam-benchmark.md (Mnemosyne BEAM Benchmark v3.0.; https://github.com/rohithzr/karta (the author's own Rust memory system, and the standing reason he b; arXiv 2605.25869 (Mitigating Provenance-Role Collapse in Long-Term Agents via Typed Memory Represent.

### Verdict

DIVE-VERIFIED — every claim in this entry is safe to cite, and every number in it is verified against the published code rather than the published prose. Carry all of the following together or none of it. (a) NEVER QUOTE 49.0% AS A BEAM SCORE. It is total_passed/1051 with rubric items binarised at >= 0.5; BEAM''s own fold on the same five runs gives 55.7 / 51.4 / 50.5 / 29.5 / 30.1. If the arm is needed as an external reference point, quote it as "a deliberately thin vanilla-RAG arm, 55.7% on BEAM''s fold / 49.0% on the author''s", and say it is a floor. (b) THE AGGREGATION-IDENTITY CHECK IS TWO-SIDED AND IS NOW A STANDING INSTRUMENT. intake-1330#record proved four external BEAM_10M headlines reproduce BEAM''s unweighted ten-ability mean exactly; this artifact is the case where the official rubric FILES and a near-official judge prompt still produce a headline 4-7pp off, because the fold changed. Reproduce a quoted number from its own per-category table before comparing it to anything. (c) THE JUDGE-PROMPT PATCH EXISTS — transcribe beam_100k_bench.py:204-274 and :329-333 rather than writing it. It adds the QUESTION to EVALUATION INPUTS and re-anchors the responsiveness guard, which is exactly the repair for the inert guard intake-1330#record found. Note it makes the judge STRICTER, so this run''s refusal behaviour is a lower bound on official BEAM''s. (d) THE ABSTENTION CONFOUND HAS ITS BEST CITATION HERE, not in BEAM''s Table 1: on one system, one judge, one dataset and five arms, the worst arm overall (26.2%) scores the HIGHEST abstention (85%). Exclude abstention from any M-12 headline. (e) THE DO-NOT-COPY LIST IS SEVEN ITEMS AND IS NOT OPTIONAL: the fold and the >= 0.5 binarisation; the assistant-turn drop at _beam_utils (`role == "user"`); max_tokens=512 on synthesis against 4.88-nugget summarization rubrics; judge == synthesiser; the SSL bypass in _unverified_ssl_context; one-document-per-message with fixed top-10 for the arm under test; and the issue''s own prose, which misdescribes its method in three places. (f) DO NOT CARRY: "the run copied BEAM''s judge prompt verbatim" (it patched it); "the clamp is standard BEAM methodology" (BEAM does no clamping); "this is the only independent end-to-end BEAM run" (Mnemosyne v3.0.0, 2026-05-18, is a second one — #125 is the first, and the only one with a published harness); "PR #168 is in the MemPalace repo" (open and unmerged at rohithzr/mempalace:bench/beam-100k); "BEAM, Tavakoli et al. 2024" (it is arXiv:2510.27246, Oct 2025, ICLR 2026). (g) DO NOT FOLD ANY OF THIS INTO intake-326#record. That entry is the MemPalace system claim; this is a benchmark-methodology record about a fold, a judge and an ingest filter, and it happens to be hosted in the same repo''s issue tracker. Its findings apply to every BEAM number, ours included.
Adopt the HARNESS SHAPE and the DEFECT LIST; adopt none of the numbers. The reusable pattern is a complete, readable, ~1,000-line single-shot BEAM driver that is env-var-configurable to an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, keeps a per-question JSONL audit log carrying both the per-rubric verdicts and the correct BEAM-shaped per-question mean, and treats abstention/summarization/event-ordering as diagnostics rather than a lump. The numbers are not adoptable in either direction: 49.0% is neither BEAM's fold nor a monotone transform of it (binarisation inflates, rubric-item weighting deflates, and the two do not cancel), the judge is a different model from BEAM's and grades its own synthesis output, and the reader was fed only half the conversation. Not `adopt_component` — there is nothing to import, the code is a MemPalace-shaped script sitting in an unmerged PR. Not `new_opportunity` — the opportunity (BEAM as an M-12 instrument) is already owned by intake-1330#record's ledger; this artifact refines HOW, not WHETHER.

## intake-1338#record — ReMoE: Boosting Expert Reuse through Router Fine-Tuning in Memory-Constrained MoE LLM Inference

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27081
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance medium; categories: moe_optimization, inference_serving, local_inference, training_distillation, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1328#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. IDENTITY / DISAMBIGUATION: this is NOT the 2024 "ReMoE: Fully Differentiable Mixture-of-Experts with ReLU Routing" (arXiv 2412.14711). This 2605.27081 is a post-training, GATE-ONLY fine-tuning framework: all non-router weights (embeddings, attention, expert FFNs) are frozen and only theta_gate is updated, with objective L = L_CE + lambda_KL*L_Trust + alpha_t*L_Loc, where L_Loc = lambda_Reuse*L_Reuse + lambda_Smooth*L_Smooth + lambda_Lag*L_Lag + lambda_WS*L_WS and the standard MoE load-balancing loss is DISABLED (aux_alpha_train=0.0). The architecture, inference graph, kernels and runtime cache policy are unchanged; a deployed ReMoE model is a checkpoint swap, nothing more.
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism, objective and frozen-parameter scope confirmed verbatim against Sec 4 and Appendix E.1. Identity versus the 2024 ReLU-routing ReMoE (2412.14711) confirmed on title, authors, date and category. Carry it as "a checkpoint swap": there is no runtime component to port.
   - anchor `Section 4, opening` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "We freeze all non-router weights—including embeddings, attention blocks, and expert FFNs—and fine-tune only the gate parameters theta_gate. As a result, ReMoE is lightweight to train and preserves the baseline inference graph, incurring zero runtime overhead at deployment."
   - anchor `Section 4.2, Balancing locality and semantic drift` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "During router fine-tuning, we disable the standard MoE load-balancing loss (L_aux = 0) because it explicitly encourages expert dispersion, which conflicts with cache locality under memory constraints."
1. The optimisation target is EOR (Expert Overlap Ratio), the mean step-to-step Top-K overlap IR_t = |E_t ∩ E_{t-1}|/K. Proposition A.4 proves N_fetch(t) <= K(1 - IR_t) under three assumptions - C >= K, serve-and-admit atomicity, and CACHE ISOLATION - and Appendix A.4 enumerates the failure modes, explicitly including a cache that shares its memory pool with KV cache / activations / other layers, and inter-step prefetch insertions.
   - *narrowed*: Proposition A.4 and its proof are exact, but the assumption set is load-bearing and the paper's own headline experiment violates it. Assumption A.3 (cache isolation) and failure mode A.4(2) exclude a cache sharing a memory pool with KV cache / activations / other layers; the Jetson --mmap run on a 16 GB unified-memory device is precisely that. Cite the bound only for a DEDICATED per-layer expert cache, never for a page-cache or unified-memory setting.
   - anchor `Appendix A.4, failure mode 2` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "Suppose expert weights share the same memory pool with other objects that can evict experts between steps (e.g., KV-cache expansion, activation buffers, or a different layer/request). Then even if E_{t-1} was fully loaded during step t-1, some of these experts might be evicted before step t begin..."
2. Reported locality gains, DeepSeek-V2-Lite (15.7B total / 2.4B active, 27 layers, 26 MoE, 64 routed + 2 shared, Top-K=6): EOR 27.3% -> 34.5% (+7.2 pp, +26.4% RELATIVE); entropy 0.9998 -> 0.9971; load-balance CV 0.0409 -> 0.1608 (+293.1%); unique experts per sequence 64.000 -> 63.997. Qwen1.5-MoE-A2.7B, same recipe untuned: EOR 0.1695 -> 0.2156 (+27.2% relative, +4.6 pp), CV 0.0174 -> 0.1109.
   - *narrowed*: Every number exact. Two framing corrections. (i) "+26%" is RELATIVE off a small base: the absolute move is +7.2 pp on DeepSeek (27.3 -> 34.5) and +4.6 pp on Qwen1.5 (16.95 -> 21.56). Quote the pp, not the percent, or the effect reads three times larger than it is. (ii) DERIVED and needed for any transfer argument: chance overlap at K=6 of 64 is 9.375%, so baseline is already 2.91x chance and ReMoE reaches 3.68x - the method moves a model that was ALREADY substantially local, which is not the regime of a 512-expert/top-10 model where chance is 1.95%.
   - anchor `Table 2` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "Baseline | 27.3% | 0.9998 | 0.0409 ; CE-only | 22.9% | 0.9998 | 0.0392 ; ReMoE | 34.5% | 0.9971 | 0.1608 ; Rel. delta | +26.4% | -0.27% | +293.1%"
3. Trace-driven offline cache simulator (128 ShareGPT prompts, greedy B=1, max_new_tokens=64, per-layer cache, request-level resets): uHR at C=6/LRU 0.3187 -> 0.3687; #uMiss 0.8707M -> 0.8068M. Across the whole grid the #uMiss reduction is only -3.98% (C=4) to -10.24% (C=12, LFU); the uHR gain is +11.24% to +15.69% relative. Belady MIN also improves (C=6: 684,671 -> 635,069 oracle misses), so the gain is not merely LRU alignment. Step-level TPOT proxy at bandwidth_GBps=4.0: -4.2% to -8.8% median.
   - *narrowed*: Simulator numbers exact, Belady included, so the gain is genuinely a better trace and not LRU alignment. NARROWED on which statistic to quote: the uHR gain (+11.24% to +15.69% relative) is the flattering one; the quantity that maps to I/O is #uMiss, and that falls by only 3.98% to 10.24%. Also record the assumptions the numbers ride on: 128 ShareGPT prompts, max_new_tokens=64, greedy, B=1, request-level cache resets (every prompt starts cold), and a flat bandwidth_GBps=4.0 for the Table-10 TPOT proxy.
   - anchor `Table 9(b), Total unique misses (millions)` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "4 LRU 1.0150 0.9746 -0.0404 -3.98% ; 6 LRU 0.8707 0.8068 -0.0639 -7.34% ; 8 LRU 0.8141 0.7486 -0.0655 -8.05% ; 12 LRU 0.7005 0.6345 -0.0660 -9.41% ; 12 LFU 0.6904 0.6197 -0.0707 -10.24% ; 12 FIFO 0.7116 0.6479 -0.0637 -8.95%"
4. Real-system results. vLLM (RTX 3090 24 GB, PCIe Gen3 x16, max-num-seqs=1, moe-expert-cache-size=6, prefix caching and chunked prefill DISABLED): throughput 3.58 -> 3.88 tok/s (+8.4%), TPOT 254.31 -> 242.99 ms (-4.5%), TTFT 769.23 -> 758.27 ms, uHR 39.4% -> 43.4%. Jetson Orin NX 16GB + llama.cpp (-np 1 -n 128 --mmap, model on an aigo NVMe SSD DP35 256GB over PCIe Gen3 x4): TPOT -44.8% / -43.6% / -49.8% on ShareGPT / GSM8K / HumanEval = 1.81x / 1.77x / 1.99x decode, and TTFT -24.9% / -21.6% / -27.2%. Two further platforms: Iluvatar BI-V150 x2 with expert-kit (cache size 800) 2.47x prefill / 1.54x decode / 1.67x end-to-end; Kunpeng-920 + Ascend 910B3 with llama.cpp on Qwen1.5-MoE 4.3 -> 4.8 tok/s (+11.6%).
   - *narrowed*: All platform numbers exact, but the headline must be re-attributed. The 1.77-1.99x is NOT an expert-cache result: llama.cpp was unmodified stock b8185 (2afcdb9777b1) with --mmap on a 16 GB Jetson holding a 15.7B model whose released GGUFs are 16.7-31.4 GB, so it measures reduced OS page-fault traffic on a machine where the model does not fit. The paper's only clean cache measurement is the vLLM row: +8.4% throughput / -4.5% TPOT, taken under an UNMERGED PR (vllm#37190, open as of 2026-09-07) at an absolute operating point of 3.58 -> 3.88 tok/s. Quote +8.4% as the transferable number and 1.77-1.99x never. Minor: prose says 43.3% where Table 4 says 43.4%; the 21-27% TTFT reductions have no stated mechanism.
   - anchor `Table 5 caption` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "Edge evaluation on Jetson Orin NX 16GB + llama.cpp (-np 1, -n 128, --mmap). All latencies are in ms."
   - anchor `References, entry "ggml-org (2026)"` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "ggml-org. llama.cpp: llm inference in c/c++. Note: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp Version v8185. Cited by: §5.1."
   - anchor `Section 5.4, Analysis` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "The vLLM result provides conservative server-side validation: the PCIe host-device path partially hides expert-cache miss cost, limiting the observable gain."
   - anchor `Table 4` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "Baseline 3.58 | 769.23 | 254.31 | 39.4% ; CE-only 2.95 | 780.12 | 286.82 | 21.1% ; ReMoE 3.88 | 758.27 | 242.99 | 43.4% ; vs. Baseline +8.4% | -1.4% | -4.5% | +3.9 pp"
   - anchor `ggml-org/llama.cpp common/arg.cpp @ 2afcdb9777b1bac79fa4bfe284b9bf23085b0469 (tag b8185), lines 2201-2205 and 2279` @ 2afcdb9777b1bac79fa4bfe284b9bf23085b0469, released 2026-03-02T09:08:30Z: "{"--mmap"}, {"--no-mmap"}, "whether to memory-map model. (if mmap disabled, slower load but may reduce pageouts if not using mlock) (default: enabled)" ... {"-ncmoe", "--n-cpu-moe"}, "N" — zero occurrences of expert cache / expert_cache in the file"
   - anchor `/mnt/raid0/llm/llama.cpp/common/arg.cpp:2530-2545 (production-consolidated-v9 @ 0db32c06e3e550065b78311a6031ef3dd2c4f27c)` @ frozen production tree, read read-only 2026-09-07: "{"-cmoe", "--cpu-moe"}, "keep all Mixture of Experts (MoE) weights in the CPU" ... {"-ncmoe", "--n-cpu-moe"}, "N", "keep the Mixture of Experts (MoE) weights of the first N layers in the CPU" — a load-time tensor_buft_override, and a repo-wide case-insensitive search for "expert cache"/"expert_ca..."
   - anchor `wiki/moe-optimization.md:59-70` @ repo working tree 2026-09-07: "The monotone decline in N is the finding: every additional layer of CPU-resident experts costs throughput proportionally, which identifies the CPU-MoE tail as the cost rather than any GPU-side inefficiency. A hybrid MoE placement is a capacity compromise that you pay for linearly."
   - anchor `github.com/BUAA-OSCAR/ReMoE git tree @701420b62013a6ff2b2ca4827ce29555bafccfe8 (recursive, truncated=false)` @ 701420b62013a6ff2b2ca4827ce29555bafccfe8, "initial", 2026-06-30T07:50:59Z: "blob 2846 README.md — the complete contents of the repository"
5. Quality claim and its evidence base. lm-eval on DeepSeek-V2-Lite (Table 6, NO error bars): GSM8K strict -0.76 pp, GSM8K flex -0.68 pp, HumanEval pass@1 +2.44 pp, MMLU +0.09 pp, IFEval prompt-strict -1.11 pp, IFEval prompt-loose 0.00. Qwen1.5-MoE (Table 14, WITH stderr): all four deltas within one stderr. CRITICAL GAP: no baseline-vs-ReMoE PERPLEXITY is reported anywhere for the headline DeepSeek model - Table 7 contains only ablations measured against Full ReMoE (3.2280) and has no Baseline row. The only baseline-vs-ReMoE PPL in the paper is Qwen1.5-MoE Table 13, where ReMoE IMPROVES PPL by 12.7% (2.7104 -> 2.3659), which is what in-domain adaptation to the fine-tuning corpus looks like, not a locality-neutral quality measure.
   - *narrowed*: Every cell exact; the CLAIM built on them is what narrows. "Maintaining downstream task performance" rests on lm-eval accuracies with NO error bars (Table 6) where the largest movements are inside one stderr, and no baseline-vs-ReMoE perplexity exists for the headline model - Table 7 has no Baseline row. The one baseline-vs-ReMoE PPL (Qwen1.5, -12.71%) is in-domain adaptation to the held-out OpenHermes split and must NOT be cited as evidence of quality preservation. DERIVED: the w/o-Trust ablation is +1.081% PPL for +0.0424 EOR, i.e. one notch of extra dose fails a <=1% gate.
   - anchor `Table 7 (Ablation results)` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "Ours (Full) 3.2280 | 71.78 | 89.65 | 0.3453 | 0.9971 | 0.1608 ; w/o Trust 3.2629 | 71.58 | 89.54 | 0.3877 | 0.9950 | 0.2110 — the table contains NO Baseline row; all deltas are "w.r.t. the full ReMoE objective""
   - anchor `Table 13 (Qwen1.5-MoE-A2.7B)` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "Baseline 2.7104 | 0.1695 | 0.99996 | 0.0174 ; ReMoE 2.3659 | 0.2156 | 0.99861 | 0.1109 ; Rel. delta -12.7% | +27.2% | -0.14% | +537.4%"
6. Attribution and composability. CE-only router fine-tuning (identical data/optimizer/length, no locality terms, no Trust-KL) makes everything WORSE than the untuned baseline: EOR 0.2293 (below baseline 0.2730), vLLM throughput 2.95 tok/s and uHR 21.1% (both below baseline). Appendix F.2: a Skliar-style training-free cache-residency bias at beta=4 lifts uHR to 63.88% but collapses PPL to 3607.92; at matched beta=0.5, ReMoE+heuristic beats baseline+heuristic on all three of uHR (34.07% vs 32.07%), PPL (6.51 vs 6.97) and estimated TPS (2.1106 vs 2.0481), so trained locality and inference-time rerouting STACK.
   - *unaffected*: CE-only control and the F.2 composability/collapse results confirmed cell by cell. These are the strongest parts of the paper and the parts most worth carrying: generic router continued fine-tuning made locality WORSE than doing nothing (EOR 0.2293 vs baseline 0.2730, vLLM 2.95 tok/s vs 3.58), which independently rules out the obvious confound; and the beta=4 collapse to PPL 3607.92 reproduces the bounded-tolerance caveat that Skliar et al. 2025 and arXiv 2608.18261 both report.
   - anchor `Table 11 / Table 12 (Appendix F.2, C=4, LRU)` @ arXiv:2605.27081v1: "ReMoE learned router beta 0.0 | 23.74% | 6.35 ; Baseline + heuristic beta 1.0 | 41.66% | 10.60 ; Baseline + heuristic beta 4.0 | 63.88% | 3607.92 — and at matched beta 0.5: Baseline+heuristic 32.07% / 6.97 / 2.0481 vs ReMoE+heuristic 34.07% / 6.51 / 2.1106"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + dive. Born dive-verified: all seven key_claims about the paper CONFIRMED, five narrowed, none falsified. What the dive establishes that a summary would not. (1) LLAMA.CPP WAS NOT MODIFIED. The paper's only llama.cpp citation is a Note reading "Version v8185"; I pinned that tag to commit 2afcdb9777b1bac79fa4bfe284b9bf23085b0469 (released 2026-03-02) and read its common/arg.cpp raw - ZERO occurrences of expert cache/expert_cache, and --mmap is the default. The companion repo at 701420b62013a6ff2b2ca4827ce29555bafccfe8 contains EXACTLY ONE FILE, README.md, describing an Ollama checkpoint. So the Jetson "expert cache" is the Linux page cache on a 16 GB unified-memory device holding a 15.7B model - the configuration the paper's OWN Appendix A.4 failure mode 2 excludes from the EOR bound. The 1.77-1.99x is an mmap paging result. This directly corrects intake-1328#record's claim_corrections[5], which says "under llama.cpp with a co-designed router". (2) THE TRANSFERABLE NUMBER IS +8.4%, not 1.77-1.99x - the vLLM row is the paper's only clean per-layer-expert-cache measurement, it is taken under an UNMERGED PR (vllm-project/vllm#37190, verified OPEN 2026-09-07, created 2026-03-16, +1645 lines), and its absolute operating point is 3.58 -> 3.88 tok/s, 3-4x slower than our existing CPU-resident qwen4exp at 10.09 tok/s. (3) NOTHING TRANSFERS TO THIS HOST, at two levels. Mechanism: production llama.cpp @0db32c06e has ZERO occurrences of expert_cache across src/common/ggml/tools; -cmoe/-ncmoe (common/arg.cpp:2530-2545) are load-time static placement overrides, so there is no cache whose hit rate a router could raise - corroborated by our own -ncmoe sweep (wiki/moe-optimization.md:59-70: decode falls monotonically and proportionally with CPU-resident layers, "a capacity compromise that you pay for linearly"). Magnitude, DERIVED from first-party measurements: ReMoE's best simulated miss reduction (7.34% at C=6, 9.41% at C=12) applied to the intake-1318#record expert path (20.96 ms of a 99.1 ms token, +27% Amdahl ceiling) bounds the end-to-end gain at +1.58% to +2.03%, and that is generous because our expert path has no fills at all. (4) THE QUALITY QUESTION IS UNTESTED, not answered: no baseline-vs-ReMoE perplexity exists for DeepSeek-V2-Lite anywhere (Table 7 has NO Baseline row); Table 6 has no error bars; the one baseline PPL pair (Qwen1.5, -12.71%) is in-domain adaptation to the held-out OpenHermes split with L_CE in the objective. Meanwhile ReMoE's own w/o-Trust ablation is +1.081% PPL for +0.0424 EOR - one notch of extra dose FAILS a <=1% gate. (5) NOT IN CONFLICT WITH arXiv 2608.18261: read directly, that paper CITES ReMoE and categorises it apart from its refutation target ("post-hoc router fine-tuning ... adapts a pretrained router for reuse rather than co-adapting experts and routing from step one"); its negative names StickyMoE in three places, it never evaluates a post-hoc gate-only fine-tune, and §9 concedes both its rungs (137M/340M) are below compute-optimal. The reconciliation is DOSE: 2608.18261 measures +2.0% PPL for 59% miss reduction; ReMoE achieves 4-10% miss reduction, a 6-15x smaller dose on the same monotone curve. They positively AGREE in two places - the stacking of trained locality with training-free rerouting, and the bounded tolerance of aggressive rerouting. (6) THE ARTIFACT DOES NOT MATCH THE MEASUREMENTS: the released GGUF is DeepSeek-V2-Lite-CHAT-ReMoE (f16 31.4 GB / Q8 16.7 GB, 55 downloads) while every paper number is on the BASE checkpoint; no training code, cache simulator or harness is released. (7) NO QUANTIZATION IS STATED anywhere for any experiment - NOT FOUND IN SOURCE. (8) Reviewer text UNREAD: OpenReview forum ylAhgNb2ak (id recovered from the README BibTeX) 403 ChallengeRequiredError on api and api2; Semantic Scholar 429 twice. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): Skliar et al. 2025, "Mixture of Cache-Conditional Experts for Efficient Mobile Device Inference", TM.

### Verdict

Carry these seven statements and not the abstract. (1) DO NOT CITE 1.77-1.99x, OR "43.6-49.8% TPOT under llama.cpp", FOR ANYTHING. llama.cpp was stock release b8185 (2afcdb9777b1) with -np 1 -n 128 --mmap; that tree has no expert cache, so the Jetson result measures OS page-fault traffic on a 16 GB device holding a 15.7B model. If you need a number from this paper, it is +8.4% output throughput / -4.5% TPOT (vLLM, RTX 3090, moe-expert-cache-size=6), and it was taken under vllm#37190, still OPEN and unmerged on 2026-09-07. (2) CORRECT intake-1328#record IN PLACE: its claim_corrections[5] and adversarial_pass[1] say "1.77-1.99x decode under llama.cpp with a co-designed router". Nothing was co-designed. Rewrite it to the +8.4% figure with the unmerged-PR caveat; the sentence as written will otherwise be quoted as evidence that hit rate buys throughput. (3) NOTHING HERE IS BUILDABLE ON THIS HOST, and the reason is structural, not a judgement call: production-consolidated-v9 @0db32c06e has ZERO occurrences of expert_cache anywhere in src/, common/, ggml/src/ or tools/, and -cmoe/-ncmoe are load-time static placement overrides. There is no cache. Our own -ncmoe sweep says the same from the other side (wiki/moe-optimization.md:59-70). (4) EVEN IN THE COUNTERFACTUAL, THE CEILING IS +2.0%: ReMoE's best simulated miss reduction (7.34% at the Top-K-matched C=6, 9.41% at C=12) against the intake-1318#record expert path (20.96 ms of a 99.1 ms token, perfect-hit ceiling +27%) gives +1.58% to +2.03% end-to-end on qwen4exp — and that assumes a fill-bound expert path, which ours is not. (5) THE BOTTLENECK KILLS OF intake-1191#record/1193/1200 STAND, UNTOUCHED. This source changes the routing trace; it changes neither the 28.89 GB/s PCIe link, nor the 61.8 GB/s CPU expert path, nor the fact that streaming a token's 1.296 GB of experts over that link costs 44.9 ms against 20.96 ms on CPU. Do not read a training-side result as a licence to reopen an offload bottleneck. (6) IT IS NOT IN CONFLICT WITH arXiv 2608.18261, and do not report it as one: 2608.18261 CITES ReMoE, categorises it apart from its refutation target (StickyMoE), never evaluates a post-hoc gate-only fine-tune, and concedes both its rungs are below compute-optimal. The reconciliation is dose — 2608.18261 pays +2.0% PPL for 59% miss reduction; ReMoE takes 4-10% miss reduction for a tax too small for its own instruments to resolve. Where ReMoE DOES push the dose (w/o Trust: +1.081% PPL for +0.0424 EOR) it fails the <=1% gate, so the two papers agree about the curve and disagree about nothing. (7) DO NOT CITE "maintaining downstream task performance" as a measured quality bound: Table 6 has no error bars, Table 7 has NO Baseline row, no baseline-vs-ReMoE perplexity for the headline model exists anywhere, and the one baseline PPL pair (Qwen1.5, -12.71%) is in-domain adaptation to the held-out fine-tuning split. Nor cite the released checkpoint as reproducing the paper: it is the CHAT lineage, and no paper number is on Chat. What IS worth keeping: the EOR/IR_t definition as the cheap companion to intake-1328#record's SRP (it needs no simulator — just the Top-K index trace the 1328 ledger already specifies); the frozen-snapshot Trust-KL anchor as the right shape for any post-hoc router edit; and the CE-only control as the mandatory attribution baseline, since here generic router fine-tuning came back WORSE than doing nothing.
Not `not_applicable`: this is squarely in domain (fine-grained MoE serving under a memory constraint), open-source, self-hostable, and the checkpoints are on HF. Not `superseded`: no successor exists for post-hoc gate-only locality fine-tuning - StickyMoE, Oracle-MoE and 2608.18261 all co-train from scratch, and Skliar et al. is training-free, so it is a sibling not a successor. Not `new_opportunity` or `adopt_component`: there is nothing to deploy, because the mechanism's only consumer would be an expert cache we do not have and the ceiling if we did is +2.0%. `adopt_patterns` names exactly four transferable items: (a) the EOR / IR_t definition as the cheap, policy-free companion to intake-1328#record's SRP - it needs no simulator at all, just the per-token Top-K index trace the 1328 ledger already specifies; (b) the frozen-snapshot Trust-KL trust region as the correct design for ANY post-hoc edit to a shipped router, with the w/o-Trust ablation as the price signal (+1.081% PPL for +0.0424 EOR); (c) the CE-only control as the MANDATORY attribution baseline for any router fine-tune claim - without it the whole result is confounded with generic continued adaptation, and here CE-only came back WORSE than doing nothing; (d) the dose-response framing that reconciles this paper with the 2608.18261 pre-registered negative. Everything else - and specifically the 1.77-1.99x - is declined below.

- updated `handoffs/active/design-backlog-triage-2026-07-23.md`

- updated `handoffs/active/vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`

## intake-1339#record — The Interplay of Harness Design and Post-Training in LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25447
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, tool_implementation, benchmark_methodology, training_distillation, routing_intelligence)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1323#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Obs. 1 (zero-shot): harness informativeness monotonically improves the OVERALL zero-shot success rate of a FROZEN model, and the magnitude scales with model capacity - h-low -> h-high moves GPT-5 Mini 28.1 -> 68.3 (+40.2), Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct 7.4 -> 29.0 (+21.6), Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct 1.3 -> 13.9 (+12.6) on the 274-game ALFWorld test split (Section 4.2, Table 10).
   - *narrowed*: Numbers exact and the prose verbatim. NARROW the adverb: "monotonically" holds for the All column in all three models but fails at task-family granularity (GPT-5 Mini h-mid regresses on Pick, Look and Cool), and GPT-5 Mini is n=1 seed by the paper's own disclosure, so its +2.9 h-mid step carries no information. Cite the All-column figures with the scope attached.
   - anchor `Section 4.2, Observation 1` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "Harness informativeness monotonically improves zero-shot performance of LLM agents, and the magnitude of the gain scales with model capacity."
   - anchor `Appendix D, Table 10, All column` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "GPT-5 Mini (high): h-low 28.1, h-mid 31.0 (+2.9), h-high 68.3 (+40.2); Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct: 1.3+/-0.6, 3.3+/-1.1 (+2.0), 13.9+/-0.7 (+12.6); Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct: 7.4+/-0.9, 16.1+/-1.7 (+8.7), 29.0+/-1.8 (+21.6)"
1. Obs. 2 (in-distribution post-training): the monotonic harness gain largely carries over after RL, and harness choice can outweigh model capacity - Qwen2.5-3B+GRPO under h-high (69.7) beats Qwen2.5-7B+GRPO under h-low (55.6) by 14.1 points (Section 4.2, Fig. 3, Table 11).
   - *narrowed*: The 14.1-point cross-capacity comparison is exact (69.7 - 55.6). NARROW two things: the trend is "largely", not strictly, monotone after training (3B GiGPO h-mid 83.2 > h-high 82.1); and "GiGPO consistently outperforms GRPO across all configurations" is in-distribution only - it is false in Table 14 (3B h-high) and Table 15 (7B h-high).
   - anchor `Section 4.2, paragraph under Observation 2` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct post-trained with GRPO under h-high outperforms Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct post-trained with GRPO under h-low by 14.1 points, indicating that the choice of harness can outweigh the effect of model capacity even after post-training."
2. Obs. 3 (post-hoc gap): a harness applied ONLY at evaluation to a model post-trained under h-low recovers almost none of the benefit of training with it in place - up to -22.5 points (Qwen2.5-7B+GRPO, h-high post-hoc 55.4 vs training-time 77.9) and -20.7 under h-mid (Section 4.3, Fig. 4, Table 12).
   - *unaffected*: Every Table 12 cell and every parenthesised delta verified verbatim. The dive ADDS the sharper sideways reading, which strengthens rather than changes the claim: post-hoc h-mid/h-high on the 7B GRPO h-low model give 55.5 and 55.4 against that model's own 55.6, i.e. -0.1 and -0.2, where the SAME harnesses gave the SAME model +8.7 and +21.6 before post-training.
   - anchor `Section 4.3, Observation 3` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "Applying a harness only after training recovers little of the benefit of training with it in place. ... The gap is particularly large for Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct post-trained with GRPO, where training-time harness application outperforms post-hoc application by 20.7 points under h-mid and 22.5 points..."
   - anchor `Appendix D, Table 12, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct GRPO rows, All column` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "h-mid 55.5+/-1.1 (-20.7) | h-high 55.4+/-1.2 (-22.5)"
3. Obs. 4 (tool-environment shift): post-training under a low-effort harness is catastrophically brittle to a tool-schema change and can land BELOW the untrained base model - Qwen2.5-7B+GRPO under h-low scores 2.7 under schema v2.0, which is 10.8 points below the zero-shot base model's 13.5; with GiGPO the drop from in-distribution is -47.8 (h-low), -45.2 (h-mid) but only -17.3 (h-high, 86.9 -> 69.6) (Section 4.4, Fig. 5/6, Table 13).
   - *narrowed*: All numbers verified verbatim. NARROW the causal framing before this is cited: Table 13's own zero-shot rows show the base model is NOT harmed by the shift (7.4 -> 13.5 under the strong shift; 7 of 9 base cells equal or improve), so the collapse is created by post-training under a weak harness, not by the shift being intrinsically hard. AND flag the evidence quality: Table 13 is the ONLY results table with no standard deviations, contradicting Appendix C.
   - anchor `Section 4.4, Observation 4 and following paragraph` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "In particular, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct post-trained with GRPO under h-low achieves 2.7 under v2.0, which is 10.8 points below the base model without post-training (13.5, Table 13)."
   - anchor `Appendix D, Table 13, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct rows, All columns for v1.0 / v1.1 / v2.0` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "Zero-shot h-low 7.4 / 7.9 / 13.5; GRPO h-low 55.6 / 54.3 / 2.7; GiGPO h-low 81.0 / 74.9 / 33.2; GiGPO h-mid 83.0 / 83.0 / 37.8; GiGPO h-high 86.9 / 82.5 / 69.6"
4. Obs. 5 (task shift): harness informativeness also carries inter-task transfer - training on t-hard only and evaluating on all categories, Qwen2.5-7B moves 33.1 -> 50.6 (GRPO, +17.5) and 28.8 -> 73.4 (GiGPO, +44.6) from h-low to h-high, with the gain concentrated in OOD categories (Heat 0.9 -> 29.1 under GRPO; Cool 0.0 -> 51.4 under GiGPO) (Section 4.4, Fig. 7, Table 16).
   - *unaffected*: All task-shift figures verified verbatim in Tables 14/15/16. Dive adds the overfitting illustration (7B GiGPO h-low: Pick 2 = 95.1 with All = 28.8) and the disclosure that Table 16's entire 3B half is duplicated zero-shot data.
   - anchor `Section 4.4, paragraph under Observation 5` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "GRPO improves from 33.1 under h-low to 50.6 under h-high (+17.5), and GiGPO from 28.8 to 73.4 (+44.6, Table 16)."
5. [NOT FOUND IN SOURCE] Any code, data, or benchmark release. The paper's central contribution is an extension of ALFWorld into a harness-controllable, shift-evaluable benchmark, and nothing in the HTML announces a repository, project page or artifact. [NOT FOUND IN SOURCE] any cost figure for the harness side - only the training side is priced (approximately 1,800 H200 GPU-hours, Section 4.1 Remark and Limitations).
   - *unaffected*: The absence of any release is verified two independent ways (external-href enumeration and full-text release-language grep, both negative), and the ~1,800 H200 GPU-hour figure is verbatim in two places. This is a NEGATIVE that must be carried: the paper's central artifact is unpublished, so nothing in it is independently checkable.
   - anchor `Section 4.1, Remark (and repeated in Limitations)` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "This amounts to a total compute budget of approximately 1,800 H200 GPU-hours."
   - anchor `Section 3.2, closing paragraph` @ arXiv:2606.25447v1 html: "Informative harnesses like h-mid and h-high, however, do not come for free. They must either be hand-designed by experts with sufficient prior knowledge of the environment, or be discovered through costly exploratory interaction with it. This cost is rarely made explicit."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + dive on arXiv:2606.25447v1. (1) Every figure in this entry was read VERBATIM from the v1 HTML table bodies (sha256 22350e600a5a) rather than from prose - Tables 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16 - and every prose claim was checked back against those cells; no number in the entry is a paraphrase. (2) NO COMPANION REPO EXISTS. Verified two ways (external-href enumeration; full-text grep for release language). The paper''s central contribution is an unpublished benchmark extension, so there is no SHA and nothing is independently reproducible - a sharp contrast with the bearing entry, whose 407-file Apache-2.0 release let intake-1323#record''s dive reproduce Appendix F from raw counts. (3) THE PAPER''S OOD CLAIM IS REAL BUT ITS FRAMING IS WRONG, and this is the dive''s main correction: Table 13''s own zero-shot rows show the base model is NOT hurt by the "shift" - it improves in 7 of 9 cells, 7.4 -> 13.5 under the STRONG shift - so the collapse is damage done by post-training under a weak harness, not difficulty introduced by the shift. (4) EVIDENCE-QUALITY DEFECT: Table 13, which carries the abstract''s headline claim, is the ONLY results table with no standard deviations, directly contradicting Appendix C''s statement that they are reported "in all result tables". (5) Table 16''s entire Qwen2.5-3B half (six rows) is duplicated ZERO-SHOT data, disclosed only in a caption ("post-training fails to take effect"); the t-hard task-shift regime has no 3B training data at all. (6) TWO ADVERBS NARROWED: "monotonically" (Obs. 1) is All-column only and GPT-5 Mini regresses on three task families at h-mid at n=1 seed; "GiGPO consistently outperforms GRPO" is in-distribution only and fails in Tables 14 and 15. (7) NEW ARITHMETIC, analyst-generated over Table 11, and it is the corrective to the bearing entry''s implicit rule: the residual harness lever on top of post-training is +22.3 under GRPO but only +5.9 under GiGPO for the same 7B model - its size is set by the training algorithm''s credit assignment, so Harness-R1''s "+5.0 on top of Agent SFT" is not a constant. (8) NEW READING of Table 12 that the paper does not state: measured sideways rather than downward, a better harness handed to a post-trained model buys -0.1 and -0.2, against +8.7 and +21.6 for the same harnesses on the same model before training. (9) INDEPENDENT PRE-DATING CORROBORATION FOUND: arXiv:2605.08741 (OPHSD, 9 May 2026, absent from the index) reports the mirror phenomenon in a different domain - "reattaching the harness during inference yields no additional benefits and can even degrade performance". (10) A DIRECT SUCCESSOR FOUND AND ABSENT FROM THE INDEX: arXiv:2607.22688 Co-Harness (17 Jul 2026) jointly optimizes harness and weights, which is this paper''s own stated future work and the frame Harness-R1''s co-evolution row implies. (11) LOCAL CLAIMS ESTABLISHED, not inherited: our tool descriptions are one-line and our tool narrowing is per-request, never per-step, so we sit at the h-low/h-mid boundary on both knobs the paper varies and have never disclosed it on the HS-6 Harness Card. (12) The paper reproduces the very cost-disclosure asymmetry it indicts - it prices only the training side (~1,800 H200 GPU-hours) - so it does NOT settle the trade-off as an economic question, only as an ordering and interaction question. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv:2607.22688 - "Co-Harness: Co-Evolving Harnesses and Model Weights for LLM Agents" (Zhengyu Che; arXiv:2605.08741 - "Training with Harnesses: On-Policy Harness Self-Distillation for Complex Reasoni. 2026-10-20 Stage-3 (intake-2006#record) - the ledger row proposed fixing a quoted 2607.12227 phrase here; no such quotation exists in this entry. The only 2607.12227 statements are the two budget-matching sentences marked inline.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY these, all read verbatim from the v1 tables: the zero-shot capacity gradient +12.6 (Qwen2.5-3B) / +21.6 (Qwen2.5-7B) / +40.2 (GPT-5 Mini) for h-low -> h-high on a FROZEN model; the ORDERING RESULT as the paper''s one clean contribution - a harness applied only after post-training recovers essentially nothing (7B GRPO: 55.6 -> 55.5 with h-mid, 55.4 with h-high, against +8.7 and +21.6 for the same harnesses on the same model before training); the brittleness result - 7B GRPO under h-low falls to 2.7 under schema v2.0, one fifth of the untrained base model''s 13.5, while h-high holds 69.6; and the three concrete informativeness knobs (rich vs one-line tool descriptions; a per-step admissible-tool line; a per-step agent-state line) as a DISCLOSURE axis the HS-6 Harness Card lacks and our own surface fails. ALSO CARRY the reframing that the paper does not state: the base model is not harmed by the tool-schema shift at all, so the collapse is damage created by post-training, which means a frozen-weight stack is structurally immune to the failure that dominates this paper''s OOD half - every MCP tool rename or consolidation is exactly this shift and costs us approximately nothing. DO NOT CARRY: (a) any of these numbers as a predicted local gain - ALFWorld household planning with <=7B open models is not our workload, and under MEASUREMENT.md this is OBSERVATION-grade external evidence supporting a direction only; (b) "harness informativeness monotonically improves performance" without the All-column scope - GPT-5 Mini regresses on three of six task families at h-mid, at n=1 seed; (c) "GiGPO consistently outperforms GRPO" outside the in-distribution setting - it is false in Tables 14 and 15; (d) Table 13''s numbers as if they carried error bars - they are the only results in the paper without them, contradicting the paper''s own Appendix C; (e) Table 16''s Qwen2.5-3B rows as post-training results - all six are duplicated zero-shot data; (f) "post-training makes harness work marginal" as a general rule - the residual harness lever is +22.3 on top of GRPO and only +5.9 on top of GiGPO for the same model, so its size is set by the training algorithm; (g) this paper as a budget-matched comparison of the two levers - it prices only the training side (~1,800 H200 GPU-hours) and never the harness side, reproducing the exact omission it indicts in prior work; (h) anything from it as reproducible evidence - there is NO code, data or benchmark release, so every figure here is a read of a table, never a reproduction. AND FOR HS-4 SPECIFICALLY: weigh this as METHOD, never as a candidate score. It is a closed-world household planner with no coding environment and no model above 7B in any training arm; it cannot rank Hermes, OpenCode, dsh, oh-my-pi, earendil-works/pi or any ACP-speaker. Its contribution to HS-4 is one criterion (a harness must be frozen before anything is trained against it, so trainability is downstream of selection and can never be a reason to select) and one disclosure field for the HS-6 card.
Adopt the FRAMING and the three harness knobs it isolates, not the numbers. Two things are carriable at zero compute: (a) the harness-informativeness ladder as a DISCLOSURE axis for the HS-6 Harness Card - tool-description form, per-step admissible-tool line, per-step state auxiliary - which is a checkable property of our own tool_registry.yaml and tool_policy.py today; and (b) the ordering constraint (harness before training) as a standing HS-4/HS-5b selection criterion. The component is not adoptable: the benchmark extension is unreleased, and the post-training arm cost approximately 1,800 H200 GPU-hours on 4x H200 for 3B/7B models, which is decisively outside a single MI210. Not new_opportunity, because the actionable content is a criterion and a disclosure field, not a build. Not not_applicable, because it bears on an open decision row in an active handoff.

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-selection-and-integration.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-use-eval-contract.md`

## intake-1340#record — Sliding-window beats linear attention

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28444
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: kv_cache, ssm_hybrid, context_extension, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1315#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Training-free Sliding Window Attention with 4 attention sinks - SWA(w,4) - applied at inference to an unmodified pretrained checkpoint matches or beats post-trained/linearized attention retrofits of the SAME checkpoint on short-context knowledge and reasoning: SWA(64,4) wins the 6-benchmark average in 9 of 11 teacher models (Section 4.1, Table 2) and recovers 93.2% of teacher MMLU / 99.0% of the 6-benchmark average (Table 1).
   - *narrowed*: Confirmed and reproduced to three digits, but narrowed twice. (a) One of the 11 teachers (QwQ-32B) has an SWA row identical to its teacher row on all seven values - not a plausible measurement; excluding it gives 92.5 MMLU recovery, a tie with QRWKV6's 92.4, and 8 SWA wins out of 10 valid cases. (b) One of the 9 wins (Llama-2.0-7B) is a walkover: DiJiang prints "-" in the Average column. (c) Table 1 is not paired - SWA is averaged over 11 models, competitors over 2-5, and the QRWKV6 MMLU cell is not derivable from Table 2 at all. Never quote "93.2 vs 92.4" as a head-to-head.
   - anchor `Table 1 caption` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1: "Performance recovery (student score divided by teacher score) on MMLU-5shot and the average of 6 benchmarks (MMLU-5shot, ARC-C, ARC-E, Hellaswag, PIQA, Winogrande)."
   - anchor `Table 1, SWA row` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1: "Sliding Window Attention (64 window-size, 4 sinks) | 0 tokens | 0 stages | MMLU recovery 93.2 (3.5) | Average recovery 99.0 (0.5). Reproduced from Table 2 over all 11 teachers: 93.17 (3.53) and 98.98 (0.51)."
   - anchor `Table 2, QwQ-32B block (raw HTML markup)` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1 sha256 9124f10a58b4: "QwQ-32B Teacher 0 | 79.9 55.6 81.0 84.1 79.8 70.5 75.2 ;; SWA(64, 4) 0 | 79.9 55.6 81.0 84.1 79.8 70.5 75.2 - the SWA row is byte-identical to the teacher row on all seven values."
1. On long-context retrieval and reasoning the gap widens: at 4K context SWA beats LoLCATs and Liger-GLA at every window size (128/256/512) on all three S-NIAH tasks (Table 3) and on BABILong at window 256 (15 vs 3 at 4K, Table 4); the abstract states this as "2 to 10 times higher than linear attention".
   - *narrowed*: The ordering is exhaustively confirmed (all 36 S-NIAH cells plus BABILong) and is the paper's strongest result. Two narrowings. (a) The prose overstates the gap ~3x: the real 4K SWA range is 9.2-23.0 not 17.2-23, and LoLCATs(512) reaches 16.6 not "at most 5.8". (b) The comparison is between two badly degraded methods - at 4K, full attention scores 100/100/99.8 where the best SWA scores 19.0/23.0/23.0 and BABILong falls 60 -> 15. Carry "SWA loses less" and never "SWA is good at long context".
   - anchor `Table 3, 4K columns, base Llama-3.1-8B` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1: "SWA(512,4): 19.0 / 23.0 / 23.0 on S-NIAH-1/2/3; LoLCATs(+SWA)(512): 8.8 / 16.6 / 5.8; Liger-GLA(+SWA)(512): 0.0 / 0.0 / 0.0; Full Attention: 100 / 100 / 99.8."
   - anchor `Section 4.2.1 Results paragraph` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1 - the true 4K SWA range is 9.2-23.0 and LoLCATs(512) reaches 16.6: "At 4K context-length, SWA recovers 17.2-23% of the regular full attention accuracy, while LoLCATs and Liger-GLA reach at most 5.8% and 0.8% accuracy respectively."
   - anchor `Table 4, BABILong average, window 256, base Llama-3.1-8B` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1: "SWA(256, 4): 55 / 20 / 19 / 15 at 0K/1K/2K/4K; LoLCATs(+SWA): 56 / 22 / 10 / 3; Full Attention: 74 / 70 / 67 / 60."
2. The comparison the linear-attention literature omits is the comparison to sink-augmented SWA: "most linear attention papers only compare linear-attention to regular (sink-free) SWA" (Section 3), and sink-free SWA fails catastrophically once the sink tokens leave the window, so that baseline is not the right one.
   - *unaffected*: Verbatim in Sections 1 and 3; the mechanism is correctly attributed to Xiao et al. 2024 (StreamingLLM, our intake-1327#record already_integrated) and Barbero et al. 2025. Nothing here overturns intake-864#record's prior-art ruling or intake-1327#record's status; it is the same Lambda-mask family.
   - anchor `Section 3, opening paragraph` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1: "However, most linear attention papers only compare linear-attention to regular (sink-free) SWA. This makes the comparison inadequate since we know that sink-free SWA leads to catastrophic failure once the first (sink) tokens are out of the sliding window."
3. SWA is also cheaper at inference: decoding throughput stays flat with context length while full attention degrades past 1K, and KV memory saturates at the window size (Section 4.3, Figures 2/3/4). NO absolute speed or memory numbers appear anywhere in the paper - Figures only - and the measurement is a 4-layer toy transformer (embedding 1024, 16 heads, head-dim 64, batch 1, fp16) on one NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Max-Q, not any of the 11 evaluated LLMs.
   - *narrowed*: Prose and hardware confirmed verbatim, but this is the weakest claim in the paper and no number of any kind supports it. There is not one tabulated speed or memory value in the artifact - Figures 2/3/4 only - and the setup is a 4-layer toy transformer at head-dim 64, batch 1, on a Blackwell workstation card, so the KV-vs-state crossover is fixed at a geometry no production model has. The paper's own memory ordering puts SWA(512) ABOVE linear attention. Nothing in Section 4.3 is transferable to EPYC CPU decode, where the binding constraint is weight bandwidth, not KV size.
   - anchor `Section 4.3, Hardware/Software paragraph` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1: "Tests are done on a 4-layer Transformer (embedding-size=1024, 16 heads with dimension 64) with batch-size=1 in float16 precision. The hardware is an NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Max-Q Workstation Edition."
   - anchor `Section 4.3, Memory paragraph` @ arXiv:2608.28444v1 - the paper itself puts SWA(512) above linear attention in memory: "SWA at window-size=64 has the lowest memory cost, followed by Linear, Linear + SWA (LoLCATs), and finally SWA at window-size=512."

4. SCOPE, stated by the authors: the negative result is about RETROFITS only. Section 4 verbatim: "For simplicity and fairness, we do not compare to hybrid models containing some full-attention layers." Section 5 lists hybrids as future work, and the abstract concedes linear-attention models "likely require to be trained from scratch or extensive post-training in order to even match SWA".
   - *unaffected*: The scope limits are stated by the authors verbatim in three places (Section 4, Section 5, abstract) and are the most load-bearing sentences in the paper for us. They are what makes the title's generality false as applied to trained-from-scratch hybrids, and they are why the paper says nothing about our rho = 4 GDN production topology.
