Gated DeltaNet MMLU: 27.5 (Qwen3-8B, teacher 74.9), 24.3 (Phi-4-mini-reasoning, teacher 57.4), 25.9 (Phi-4-reasoning-plus, teacher 77.8) - at or below the 25% c..."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + dive on arXiv 2608.28444v1 (born dive-verified; there was no prior entry). What a later reader must not re-derive: (1) SCOPE IS THE HEADLINE. Section 4 excludes hybrids with full-attention layers by construction, Section 5 lists them as future work, and the abstract concedes trained-from-scratch linear models are out of scope - so the paper does NOT bear on the DeltaNet-vs-SWA choice our Qwen3.x GDN hybrids made (production 10 full-attention of 40, rho = 4). (2) The only Gated DeltaNet evidence in the paper (Appendix A.2 Table 5) is the authors' own 0.1B-token cleaned-Alpaca conversion, which sits at MMLU chance (27.5 / 24.3 / 25.9 vs a 25% floor) after 2.5x more tokens than LoLCATs uses to reach 83.2% in the same paper - it measures the recipe, not the architecture. (3) The main ordering result IS solid: I checked all 36 S-NIAH cells and all 11 Table-2 averages by hand. (4) But the headline table is unpaired - SWA over 11 teachers, LoLCATs over 4 (MMLU) or 5 (Average) with its best model silently dropped from the MMLU cell, QRWKV6's MMLU cell not derivable from Table 2 at all. (5) The QwQ-32B SWA row is byte-identical to its teacher row on all seven values; removing it makes 93.2 -> 92.5, a tie with QRWKV6. (6) Section 4.2.1 overstates the long-context gap ~3x (true SWA 4K range 9.2-23.0, LoLCATs(512) reaches 16.6 not 5.8). (7) BOTH families collapse at long context - full attention 100/100/99.8 vs best SWA 19.0/23.0/23.0 at 4K - and the accuracy experiments never exceed 4K. (8) There is NOT ONE speed or memory NUMBER in the paper; Section 4.3 is figures on a 4-layer toy transformer, and the paper itself puts SWA(512) above linear attention in memory. (9) No code, weights or eval artifacts were released. (10) NEW LOCAL FINDING, first-order for the sweep: the paper masks at every position including prefill, while frozen v9's --kv-streaming-* is decode-time eviction inside the context-shift branch only, and a prompt of n_ctx or more is rejected outright (server-context.cpp:3227-3235); there is no runtime n_swa override (common/arg.cpp:1491/:1501/:1512 expose only sink, window and --swa-full). Our long-INPUT retrieval arm therefore measures "full-attention prefill + streaming decode", not SWA. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv 2512.10411 - SWAA: Sliding Window Attention Adaptation for Efficient and Quality Preserving Lo; Cabannes, Beck, Lomeli, Szilvasy, Douze, Copet, Mazare, Synnaeve, Jegou - "Short window attention en.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY: (1) among BOUNDED-COST RETROFITS on a frozen checkpoint, SWA-with-sinks is the right shape - a zero-token inference-time mask beats every published linearization of the same teacher on the 6-benchmark average in 9 of 11 cases and in all 36 S-NIAH cells, so any bounded-cost method must be credited against a SINK-AUGMENTED SWA floor, never against sink-free SWA; (2) this is the strongest published warrant for the premise of streaming-llm-baseline.md and it predicts a WORKLOAD SPLIT in our pending 4-axis sweep (short-context passes trivially, long-context retrieval fails the <=10% floor); (3) the paper is SILENT on our production GDN hybrids by its own construction - record that once, in wiki/ssm-hybrid.md, so it is never cited at our rho = 4 topology; (4) our instrument is not the paper's - --kv-streaming-* is decode-time eviction and a prompt >= n_ctx is rejected, so a long-INPUT arm measures full-attention prefill plus streaming decode. DO NOT CARRY: the title as a general claim about linear attention (it is about CONVERSION - the abstract concedes from-scratch is a different question); "SWA 93.2 vs QRWKV6 92.4" as a head-to-head (unpaired model sets; the QwQ row is a duplicate and removing it makes it a tie); "SWA recovers 17.2-23% at 4K" or "LoLCATs reaches at most 5.8%" (9.2-23.0 and 16.6 respectively); any reading in which SWA is GOOD at long context (it recovers 15-23% of full attention at 4K); "SWA is faster / uses less memory" as a number (there are none, the model is a 4-layer toy, and the paper puts SWA(512) above linear attention in memory); anything from Appendix A.2 about Gated DeltaNet, GLA or QRWKV6 as architectures (all nine arms are at MMLU chance); and any long-context conclusion beyond 4K.
The technique is already integrated - our frozen v9 exposes sink+window as --kv-streaming-sink / --kv-streaming-window (common/arg.cpp:1491, :1501) and StreamingLLM is intake-1327#record already_integrated - so there is no component to adopt. What transfers is (a) the comparison protocol: paired per-model recovery ratios against the identical teacher, and the rule that a bounded-cost method is only credited against a sink-augmented SWA floor, which is exactly the gate streaming-llm-baseline.md exists to run; and (b) a strong negative prior on where bounded windows break (long-context retrieval), which should reshape that sweep's success criteria before compute is spent. Not new_opportunity (nothing new to build), not worth_investigating (the paper needs nothing investigated - our sweep does), not not_applicable (it amends an active handoff).

- updated `handoffs/active/streaming-llm-baseline.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`

## intake-1341#record — Measuring Harness-Induced Belief Divergence in Multi-Step LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04528
- **Verification**: dive-overturned
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, tool_implementation, knowledge_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1320#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Central thesis: an agent harness can change the agent's multi-step beliefs even when the task, environment and base LLM are held fixed; therefore "harness design is an experimental variable in agent evaluation, not an implementation detail" (Abstract, Section 1).
   - *narrowed*: The thesis is stated verbatim and is independently believed in this repository on much better evidence (intake-1140#record, 2605.23950, 2605.27922, benchmark-methodology.md:2114). What is corrected is that THIS paper does not establish it: the reference arm and the mediated arms differ in injected task metadata as well as in harness, so the demonstration confounds the named variable. Carry the thesis on our existing sources; never cite 2607.04528 as evidence for it.
   - anchor `Abstract, sentences 2 and last` @ arXiv:2607.04528v1: "We show that this harness can change the agent's multi-step beliefs even when the task, environment, and base LLM are fixed. ... The results suggest that harness design is an experimental variable in agent evaluation, not an implementation detail."
   - anchor `harnesses/h0_raw.py:44-47 versus harnesses/h1_structured.py:136-141 and h2_risk_gated.py:95-99` @ github.com/Hik289/Harness-induce-bias @ 902d17d4f83247b62d9128ab4d171e11f9864a39: "return Observation(raw_text=raw_text, structured={}, # H0 不暴露结构化 ... VS ... "task_constraints": {"target_state": task.get("target_state", ""), "safe_actions": ..., "risky_actions": ..., "expected_failure_modes": task.get("expected_failure_modes", [])},"
   - anchor `core/rollout.py:90-92 and :101` @ github.com/Hik289/Harness-induce-bias @ 902d17d4f832: ""current_observation": {"raw_text": obs.raw_text, "structured": obs.structured, "harness_meta": obs.meta}, ... f"{json.dumps(user_payload, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)}\n```\n\n""
1. Formal apparatus: a harness is a six-tuple H=(O_H, A_H, V_H, G_H, R_H, L_H) — observation map, action interface, verifier, risk gate, repair policy, logging policy (Definition 1). A cross-harness belief divergence D_belief is defined over K-step structured belief rollouts with fixed weights w=(0.30,0.15,0.25,0.25,0.05) for (cat, fail, set, num, act), and admits the EXACT decomposition D_belief = 0.30*D_arrival + 0.70*D_growth (Definitions 4-6, Appendix B eq. 56-58). Theorem 1 gives three sufficient conditions (C1 context sensitivity, C2 first-order propagation, C3 non-negative expected growth increment) under which expected growth divergence is non-decreasing in horizon.
   - *unaffected*: LOAD-BEARING AND CLEAN. The six-tuple, the five component distances, the weight vector and the exact 0.30/0.70 decomposition are definitional and survive every defect found. I re-derived the identity independently across Tables 1, 3 and 7 and it holds to three decimals in all 70 cells; Table 3's D_belief row also reproduces the mean of Table 2's five comparisons at all seven horizons. Theorem 1 is honestly scoped (it explicitly does not claim censoring alone implies monotonicity). This is the only part of the paper to carry forward.
   - anchor `Definition 1 (Harness), Section 3.1` @ arXiv:2607.04528v1: "A harness is a six-tuple of measurable maps H=(O_H, A_H, V_H, G_H, R_H, L_H) ... O_H the observation map; A_H the action interface; V_H the verifier, where ⊥ denotes "not checked"; G_H the risk gate, where G_H(a)=∅ indicates action a is blocked pre-execution; R_H the repair policy, applied post-f..."
   - anchor `Appendix B, eq. 56-58 and the sentence following` @ arXiv:2607.04528v1: "The implementation uses the fixed weight vector w=(0.30,0.15,0.25,0.25,0.05)^T ... The arrival and growth weights are w_arr=0.30 and w_gro=0.70 ... It follows directly that D_belief = 0.30 D_arrival + 0.70 D_growth. The recomputation procedure verifies this identity for every reported row."
2. Controlled and long-horizon empirics on HIBench-Code (6 harnesses x 8 tasks x 4 horizons x 3 seeds): the arrival readout is already at 0.994-1.000 at K=1 and stays there, so scalar D_belief has a large fixed interface floor and appears stable; the component readouts keep moving — D_num rises 0.044 (K=1) -> 0.136 (K=16), and D_fail is strongly non-monotone, 0.500 -> 0.575 -> 0.325 (transient K=5 convergence) -> 0.575 (Tables 1-3).
   - *narrowed*: Cell values are as printed. Two mechanisms behind them are implementation artifacts, not findings: (a) the near-saturated arrival floor follows from H0 carrying an EMPTY constraint set, which triggers Lemma 1's disjoint-set floor by construction; (b) D_growth's horizon story is undercut because the reference arm receives a constant observation at every step and is explicitly told so in its own prompt. No uncertainty is reported for any cell.
   - anchor `harnesses/h0_raw.py:36-42 (the step>0 observation)` @ github.com/Hik289/Harness-induce-bias @ 902d17d4f832: "f"[step {step}] 上一步你选择了 action: {last_act}\n" f"环境反馈 (raw, 未结构化):\n{raw}\n" f"(注: Day-1 rollout 不执行真实动作, 这里 obs 与 step 0 相同; 这是 imagined rollout, 你需要 imagine 选择 action 后的 belief 更新)""
3. Public-benchmark stress tests and behavioural propagation: on a SWE-bench Verified slice, raw-vs-risk-gated failure-mode divergence rises 0.400 -> 0.800 from K=3 to K=5 (Table 4); the harness effect persists across grouped slices (612 SWE-bench runs, 540 Terminal-Bench runs, Tables 8-9); across 840 step-level pairs the action-divergence rate rises from 0.28 in the lowest D_growth quartile to 0.595 in the highest, and UnsafeRetryRate under risk gating is 0.700 (42 of 60 blocked high-risk steps re-propose a same-class risky action within three steps) (Section 10). The Abstract further asserts these interventions "often preserve terminal success while changing the beliefs that drive later decisions".
   - *overturned*: The public-benchmark stress tests do not execute the public benchmarks — the adapters say so in their own docstrings — and the single largest effect (SWE-bench raw-vs-risk-gated D_fail 0.400 -> 0.800) is a failure-label divergence in which the mediated arm was shown a hand-written per-repository failure-label table and the reference arm was not. The Abstract's "often preserve terminal success" is NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE as a measurement and is unmeasurable in this design. Two of the four Table 4 cells are additionally below their own algebraic floor. This is the load-bearing falsification.

   - anchor `Table 4 caption and the paragraph above it, Section 7` @ arXiv:2607.04528v1: "Table 4 shows that D_fail increases from K=3 to K=5 for both tested comparisons. The increase is largest for the risk-gated comparison, where failure-mode divergence rises from 0.400 to 0.800."
   - anchor `Abstract (the only occurrence of "terminal success" in the paper)` @ arXiv:2607.04528v1: "On controlled coding tasks and public-benchmark stress tests, blocked actions, compressed repairs, selective verification, and cost-aware evidence pruning often preserve terminal success while changing the beliefs that drive later decisions."
4. BIWM (Belief-Invariant World-Modeling), a no-training instrumentation protocol with six components — canonicalisation, blocked-action logging, repair-unrolled traces, verification masks, shadow execution, cross-harness alignment — separates an EXPOSURE effect (wrapper components 1-5 raise measured divergence by making hidden channels visible; Delta_exp up to +0.226) from a ROBUSTNESS effect (component 6 alignment lowers it by reducing single-harness trajectory lock-in; Delta_rob up to -0.036) (Sections 8, 12, 14; Tables 7, 11, 13, 14).
   - *narrowed*: The BIWM component DESIGN is real, released and genuinely useful as a disclosure checklist, and the exposure-vs-robustness distinction is a good idea carefully stated. The QUANTITATIVE split is unreadable (Table 11 vs Table 7), and "shadow execution" cannot mean what it says because nothing executes — consistent with it being the weakest component in Table 14 (+0.019 / -0.002). Cite the six channels as a taxonomy; cite no Table 7/11/13/14 number.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1+dive: THE SOURCE WAS SURFACED TO SETTLE A QUESTION AND RETURNS A NULL. It cannot settle whether swapping the harness under a fixed backbone changes what the agent believes, for reasons found in the release, not in the paper. SIX findings, all exhaustively checkable at pinned SHA 902d17d4f832 (68-entry tree, truncated:false). (1) THE CONFOUND, decisive: every reported number is raw-reference-vs-mediated, and the two arms differ in more than the harness — harnesses/h0_raw.py:46 sends `structured={}` while all five mediated harnesses send `task_constraints` including `expected_failure_modes`, which core/rollout.py:90-92,101 serialises into the prompt. D_set (the arrival floor) and D_fail (the growth story) are exactly the two channels this feeds. On SWE-bench the leaked field is a hard-coded per-repository lookup table (swebench_adapter.py:31-38,154). (2) NOTHING EXECUTES: swebench_adapter.py:3-4 "Imagined rollout only — does not execute the real repos / docker images"; terminal_bench_adapter.py:7-8 the same for docker and run-tests.sh; h0_raw.py:38-42 tells the model in its own observation that the obs is identical to step 0 and it must imagine. Definition 3's Psi_{E,H} is not implemented. (3) TERMINAL SUCCESS IS NEVER MEASURED: "terminal success" occurs once in 28 pages, in the Abstract; "success rate", "resolve rate", "solved" occur zero times. The half of the Abstract a reader would carry is asserted, not measured, and is unmeasurable here. (4) THE METRIC DOES NOT SHIP: exactly one path in the release matches "metric" (the spec markdown), while EIGHT scripts import a missing `metrics` package — including scripts/phase1_main.py:40, the README's own paper-scale reproduction entry point; g4_recompute.py:51-52 also imports missing metrics.calibration and metrics.auroc. No data/ ships either, so the HIBench-Code fixtures and Appendix F's "raw rollout JSONL logs" are absent. NOT ONE TABLE CAN BE RECOMPUTED. tests/ ships 7 tests against a claimed 77/77. (5) THREE INTERNAL ARITHMETIC CONTRADICTIONS, calculator-checkable from the printed paper: Table 4's two K=5 rows sit BELOW their own algebraic floor (implied D_act -0.210 and -0.172; rounding bounds the error at ~0.001) while the K=3 rows are consistent; Table 11's Baseline column contradicts Table 7's None rows for the same declared quantity, with two of five rows numerically identical to Table 7's D_growth column instead — and those two rows are the entire Section-12 exposure headline; Tables 8-9 run ~0.25 above Tables 4-5 on the same benchmarks at the same horizons, unremarked. (6) RESOLVED FROM OUTSIDE THE PAPER: the base LLM is never named in 28 pages; the release default is gpt-5.4-mini against api.openai.com/v1 (core/llm_client.py:23-24, README.md:83). Carry that as a REPO DEFAULT, never as a paper statement. ALSO: the release README's weight table contradicts both the paper and the repo's own METRICS_SPEC.md:130-136. WHAT SURVIVES: the six-tuple H=(O,A,V,G,R,L), the D_arrival/D_growth decomposition (identity re-derived across 70 cells), and BIWM's six channels as a disclosure checklist. LOCAL FACTS ESTABLISHED, independent of the paper's validity: our unified trace store cannot pair two runs across harnesses at all — no harness identity (EventSource.HERMES_SESSION at store.py:36 is a dead constant with no producer), no seed, no step ordinal — and Vidya's ClaimTuple structurally refuses per-step agent belief states (claim_tuple.py:98-125 requires a metric with a direction; the spec already refuses the analogous dependency-evidence class at vidya-pilot-spec.md:399-407). Vidya "belief" != agent belief; the collision is nominal. Our frozen llama.cpp at 0db32c06e accepts all three client parameters (max_completion_tokens alias, response_format json_object WITH schema, seed), so a from-scratch instrument would be strictly better than the paper's parse-and-retry. VERDICT CHANGED worth_investigating -> adopt_patterns; credibility 2. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv:2603.19328 - Sah et al., The verifier tax: horizon dependent safety success tradeoffs in tool ; arXiv:2608.23953 - The Empire, Long Divided, Must Unite: Architectural Convergence in Three LLM Agen; arXiv:2606.11211 - Hiremath & Hiremath, Calibration drift under reasoning: how chain-of-thought budg; arXiv:2607.05458 - Learning to Control LLM Agent Harnesses with Offline Reinforcement Learning. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1344#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: corroborates (claims [0]) - CORROBORATES claim 0 (the premise that a harness is a first-order experimental variable at fixed model, task and environment) from a genuinely independent direction, and does so on EXECUTED outcome evidence rather than on 2607.04528's confounded raw-vs-mediated arms - which is exactly what the 1341 dive said should carry the thesis instead. Claims 1-4 of intake-1341#record (the six-tuple H, D_belief and its arrival/growth decomposition, the HIBench-Code and SWE-bench/Terminal-Bench belief tables, BIWM) are INDEPENDENT of this source: it contains no belief construct at all - "belief", "world model" and "elicit" occur zero times in v1 - so it neither supports nor threatens any of them. TWO NARROWINGS OWED BACK TO THE BEARING DIVE, neither of which changes a key_claim there. (1) The 1341 dive characterises this paper as one of the sources on which our harness thesis rests "on much better evidence" and as "strictly stronger" than 2607.04528 for HS-4. Both remain true - this paper is better designed, better disclosed in prose, and independently corroborated in direction - but "strictly stronger" should not be read as "unconfounded": its 7.80x is an author-built harness ladder measured against three deliberately-clustered models at n=2 per cell, and it releases no code, so on the artifact axis it is actually WEAKER than 2607.04528, which at least ships a repo. (2) The 1341 dive states we "are running on an unverified source" at harness-selection-and-integration.md:164. That is now resolved and the verdict is worse than feared in one specific way and better in another: the taxonomy is real and correctly named, but our stated DELTA against intake-921#record's six-dimension list is factually wrong and the resulting card omits two of the seven layers. VINDICATES the 1341 dive's single highest-priority surfaced source - it was right that this needed ingesting, and right about why. CONFIRMS ITS CENTRAL NEGATIVE: 1341's finding that no local or literature source measures elicited agent belief survives contact with the strongest harness-variance paper in the field, which measures pass@1 and nothing else. 2026-10-20 Stage-3 (intake-2006#record) - the 53276 and 53294 line pointers resolved into intake-908#record and are replaced; 2607.12227 figures are the v1/v2 reading, see inline markers.

### Verdict

dive-overturned. CARRY exactly three things: (a) the harness six-tuple H=(O_H observation, A_H action interface, V_H verifier, G_H risk gate, R_H repair policy, L_H logging) as the row vocabulary for the HS-6 Harness Card at harness-selection-and-integration.md:164; (b) the D_arrival/D_growth decomposition as a general reporting discipline — a weighted composite whose interface-facing terms saturate will look stable while the terms that matter keep moving, which is a real and transferable lesson for any of our own composite scores; (c) BIWM's six channels as an observability CHECKLIST for candidate harnesses. NEVER CARRY any number from this paper: not the 0.400 -> 0.800 SWE-bench failure-mode rise, not the 0.28 -> 0.595 action-divergence gradient, not UnsafeRetryRate 0.700, not any Table 1-14 cell, not any Delta_exp or Delta_rob. Every one is a raw-vs-mediated comparison in which the mediated arm was shown the task fixture's `expected_failure_modes` and the reference arm was shown an empty structured field, and on SWE-bench that field comes from a hard-coded per-repository table. Do NOT read the SWE-bench or Terminal-Bench rows as benchmark results — the adapters state in their own docstrings that they never execute repos, docker or run-tests.sh. Do NOT repeat the Abstract's "often preserve terminal success": task success is never measured in 28 pages and cannot be, because nothing runs. Do NOT plan a reproduction: the `metrics` package that eight scripts import is not in the release, the HIBench-Code fixtures are not in the release, and the README's own paper-scale command fails on import at a fresh checkout. Do NOT cite this paper in HS-4 — our own harness evidence (intake-1140#record, 2605.23950, 2605.27922, benchmark-methodology.md:2114) is strictly stronger. Do NOT attribute the base model to the paper: gpt-5.4-mini is a repo default (core/llm_client.py:24), never stated in the text. Do NOT confuse Vidya "belief" (graded confidence in research claims) with this paper's "belief" (elicited agent world-model state) — the ClaimTuple refuses the latter by construction and that refusal is correct. DO carry forward, as facts about US that this dive established and that stand whatever happens to the paper: our trace store has no harness identity, no seed and no step ordinal, so no two runs can be paired across harnesses today; and our frozen llama.cpp accepts response_format with a schema, so any belief-elicitation instrument we ever build should use grammar-constrained decoding rather than parse-and-retry.
First-pass reading only, before verification: the formal apparatus is clean and self-consistent, the arrival/growth decomposition is a real methodological contribution, the release is MIT-licensed with a documented reproduction path, and the paper targets exactly the question HS-4 leaves open. The dive in the same pass changes this — see verdict_after_dive.

- updated `handoffs/active/unified-trace-memory-service.md`

## intake-1342#record — LOCA-bench: Benchmarking Language Agents Under Controllable and Extreme Context Growth

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07962
- **Verification**: dive-verified

- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1316#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. LOCA-bench varies agent context by scaling the ENVIRONMENT DESCRIPTION LENGTH - the GPT-4-tokenizer token count of every tool output an agent would need to read, measured by scripted tool calls - across seven regimes (8K/16K/32K/64K/96K/128K/256K), while (per the paper) "keeping the task semantics fixed"; 15 seed agentic tasks x 5 environment seeds = 75 samples per regime, 525 total, over 7 mock MCP servers and 280 tools.
   - *narrowed*: The seven-regime / 75-sample / 280-tool construction and the environment-description-length definition are exact in both paper and code. The narrowing is the paper's own framing: "keeping the task semantics fixed" holds for the success RULE and fails for the required output cardinality, which scales with the same knob. Anyone citing a LOCA delta as pure context-length effect must state this.
   - anchor `Section 2.2, "Environment Description Length"` @ arXiv:2602.07962v1: "we run scripted tool calls that interact with the environment, collect and concatenate all tool outputs an agent would need to read, then tokenize this aggregated text and record the resulting token count as the metric... its token count under GPT-4's tokenizer is recorded as the Environment Desc..."
   - anchor `Section 2.3, Implementation` @ arXiv:2602.07962v1: "We automatically vary the environment states for 7 different environment description length spanning 8K, 16K, 32K, 64K, 96K, 128K, or 256K tokens. For each length, we use five random seeds to produce distinct environment states while keeping the environment description length fixed, leading to 75..."
   - anchor `task-configs/final_8k_set_config.json vs final_256k_set_config.json, CanvasArrangeExamS2LEnv env_params (answer cardinality is NOT held constant)` @ hkust-nlp/LOCA-bench @ 8b6fac49d9edd92922593e703b74ea255357c3ec: "8k: {"seed": 42, "num_courses": 2, "canvas_exam_rate": 0.8, "email_exam_rate": 0.2} -> 256k: {"seed": 42, "num_courses": 160, "canvas_exam_rate": 0.8, "email_exam_rate": 0.2}"
1. Scoring is a per-task, hand-written, rule-based script that compares the FINAL ENVIRONMENT STATE the agent produced against a generated ground-truth workspace; the outcome is binary 1/0 and no LLM judge is used anywhere.
   - *unaffected*: Deterministic, LLM-free, final-state, binary - confirmed in paper and in 15 independent verifier scripts. This is the property that makes LOCA columns immune to the same-family-judge concern intake-1316#record correctly flagged for BEAM and LongMemEval, and it survives verification intact. The gameability finding is about the harness's isolation, not about the verifier's logic.

But because the model is unfamiliar with the environment, it often uses these..."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + dive: this source was ingested and verified in one pass against arXiv:2602.07962v1, the pinned code at hkust-nlp/LOCA-bench @ 8b6fac49, the pinned leaderboard at LOCA-bench.github.io @ f28f232b, and - for the provenance question - the bearing paper arXiv:2608.21690v1 directly. Five things are now settled that were open. (1) TABLE 7 PROVENANCE IS MIXED AND ITS CAPTION IS WRONG FOR ONE ROW: GPT-5.2-Medium 38.7/21.3 and Claude-4.5-Opus 34.0/14.7 are LOCA Table 1; GPT-5.2-Medium+PTC 49.3 is LOCA Table 2 at 128K only; MiniMax M3 49.3 at 256K is MiniMax's own launch blog (Scroll's reference [20]), absent from the LOCA paper and from the leaderboard, and labelled self-reported with zero verified results by llm-stats. The same MiniMax table reports Claude Opus 4.7 at 57, so 49.3 is not the best number even in Scroll's own cited source. (2) THE 131,072 BUDGET IS ADMISSIBLE, and the confound must be restated rather than dropped: LOCA runs every model at its own window by design (130K to 1050K), so per-model C is protocol, not deviation; Scroll's defect is the static-catalog fallback, and LOCA independently prices the regime at roughly 3x accuracy (6.7 vs 21.3 at 256K). The released runner also hard-fails an episode when trimming cannot fit, which the paper never mentions. (3) THE VERIFIER IS DETERMINISTIC AND LLM-FREE - intake-1316#record's reading is exact - BUT THE HARNESS IS GAMEABLE: the ground truth and the mock-server SQLite files sit as siblings of the agent workspace, and python_execute (70 of 75 configs) runs model-authored code as an unrestricted host subprocess with cwd = agent_workspace. No evidence any published run exploited it; the loca-sandbox branch containerises the host but not the ground truth. (4) SCROLL'S SAME-BACKBONE 256K MARGIN IS ONE TASK OUT OF SEVENTY-FIVE (65/75 vs 64/75), 0.34 of a standard error, and 128K is a literal tie - a sharpening of intake-1316#record's "+1.4/tie" that makes the comparison unreproducible in sign. (5) LOCA'S OWN "keeping the task semantics fixed" is narrower than it reads: the length knob also grows the ground-truth answer cardinality (num_courses 2->160, num_students 20->700, num_exams 2->15), so every LOCA delta conflates long-context retention with aggregation size. Additionally: exactly one cell across Tables 1-3 (Claude-4.5-Opus @128K = 34.0) is not expressible as k/75, and it is one of the cells Scroll quotes; the public leaderboard is five months stale relative to Scroll, adds three unpublished models and silently revises Gemini-3-Flash upward by 13.4 pp at 128K; the 15 seed tasks are inherited from the authors' own prior benchmark (Toolathlon, arXiv 2510.25726, shared authors), which has zero index coverage. Locally: our entire long-context roster is single-step, GAIA is our only tool-use suite and has no growth axis, and LOCA-bench has zero repo coverage outside intake-1316#record. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): arXiv 2510.25726 - "The Tool Decathlon: Benchmarking Language Agents for Diverse, Realistic, and Lon; arXiv 2510.01051 - "GEM: A Gym for Agentic LLMs", Liu, Sims, Duan, Chen, Yu, Zhou, Xu, Xiong, Liu, T.

### Verdict

dive-verified. CARRY: (1) the environment-description-length construction as a design rule for any first-party agentic long-context measurement - scale the world, hold the success rule fixed, and ALSO hold the answer cardinality fixed, which is the control LOCA itself lacks; (2) deterministic final-state rule verification with per-field tolerances instead of an LLM judge; (3) always report trajectory length, tool-call count and tool-output tokens beside accuracy, because LOCA's Figure 3 is what turns "accuracy fell" into "the agent stopped exploring" - the failure mode a compaction A/B would otherwise score as a wash; (4) LOCA Table 2 as a free, independent, agentic prior FOR the open A/B's hypothesis - on GPT-5.2-Medium, LLM compaction (36.0) is worse than no compaction (38.7) while cheap tool-result clearing (40.0) is slightly better, agreeing with intake-274#record; (5) the roster fact - all five of our long-context suites are single-step, GAIA is our only tool-use suite and has no growth axis, so we own no instrument on the diagonal. DO NOT CARRY: (a) Scroll's "+37.4 over the best published long-horizon agent" - the comparator is MiniMax's own unverified product-blog number, is absent from both the LOCA paper and its leaderboard, and is beaten on that same page by Claude Opus 4.7 at 57; cite the same-backbone Table 3 result instead, and state that it is one task out of seventy-five; (b) "a 131,072-token budget is inadmissible at LOCA_256K" - it is admissible, LOCA ships a 130K-window model at all seven lengths, and the right claim is that the budget is unverifiable in Scroll and that the regime costs roughly 3x accuracy; (c) any LOCA delta as a pure context-length effect - the answer cardinality scales with the same knob; (d) any single-length difference under ~10 pp as real - n = 75, no repetitions, SE 4.6-5.8 pp, one task = 1.33 pp; (e) "the LOCA leaderboard" without a date and an artifact - the site was last pushed 2026-03-30, is not a copy of the paper, and revises Gemini-3-Flash by +13.4 pp at 128K; (f) any LOCA score, ours or a third party's, as evidence unless the run can assert the agent did not read ../groundtruth_workspace/ or ../local_db/ - the released main branch does not prevent it and does not log it. Cite arXiv 2602.07962 and the pinned SHA 8b6fac49, never the title alone.
adopt_patterns, with an explicit decline of the component. Three methodology patterns transfer at zero compute and are worth more to us than the suite: (1) scale the ENVIRONMENT, not the prompt - hold the task rule fixed and grow the state the agent must discover, which is the only construction that isolates context growth from task difficulty in an agentic setting; (2) verify the FINAL STATE with a deterministic rule script rather than an LLM judge, which removes the same-family-judge hazard our own benchmark methodology repeatedly hits; (3) report trajectory length, tool-call count and tool-output tokens ALONGSIDE accuracy - LOCA's Figure 3 / Tables 4-6 are what turn "accuracy fell" into "the agent stopped exploring". NOT adopt_component: standing the suite up on our host is a multi-week harness project (7 mock MCP servers, 280 tool schemas, Docker sandbox, agentic tool-calling reliability on locally served models), it is scored 0/1 with n=75 and no repetitions so its noise floor (~4-6 pp) is wider than the effects our A/B is chasing, and its verifier has an unfixed ground-truth-adjacency hole (below). NOT new_opportunity (the mechanism is a measurement instrument, not a serving lever), NOT superseded (nothing else does this axis), NOT not_applicable (MIT, self-hostable, drives any OpenAI-compatible /v1/chat/completions endpoint - i.e. llama-server - via LOCA_OPENAI_BASE_URL).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`
- updated `handoffs/completed/long-context-eval-datasets.md`

## intake-1343#record — Windows/ROCm on RX 9060 XT (gfx1200): packed GGUF working end-to-end (dense + MoE), root cause of the RDNA4 offload-decode TDR, benchmarks vs llama.cpp (FreeToken issue #122, with forks PialGhosh2233/FreeToken-rocm-gfx1200 and Maxritz/FreeToken-ROCm and antecedent issue #82)

- **URL**: https://github.com/FlashML-org/FreeToken/issues/122
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: moe_optimization, hardware_optimization, local_inference, inference_serving, benchmark_methodology, quantization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1318#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SAME-MACHINE SAME-GGUF ROCm HEAD-TO-HEAD (RX 9060 XT, gfx1200, 16 GB, Windows 11 build 26200, TheRock rocm 10.1.0a20260806, torch 2.11.0+rocm10.1.0a20260806, triton-windows 3.7.1.post27): gpt-oss-20b MXFP4 (MoE, fully GPU-resident, --moe-backend fused, graphs ON) FreeToken ~61 tok/s decode vs llama.cpp b10603 ROCm 83.5 and b10545 Vulkan 88.8; Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct Q4_K_M (dense) FreeToken ~93.6 vs llama.cpp ROCm 89.9 and Vulkan 113.0. The reporter isolates the MoE deficit to "the MXFP4 split-K GEMV/swiglu kernels, untuned for RDNA4".
   - *unaffected*: All six benchmark numbers exact in both artifacts. What must ride with them, always: three different llama.cpp builds (b10603/b10545/b10630), a ROCm binary built for 7.14 and run on a 10.1 runtime, server-with-sampling versus llama-bench-without, a prompt column the author himself disclaims as not pp512-equivalent, and n=1 with no error bars. Independent Linux data on the same card puts the Vulkan-over-ROCm tg gap at +1.6%/+8.8%, not +25.7%.
   - anchor `issue #122, "Benchmarks vs llama.cpp (same machine, same GGUF files)"` @ issue #122, updated 2026-08-27T07:18:05Z, read 2026-09-07: "llama.cpp numbers are `llama-bench -ngl 99 -fa 1` (pp512 / tg128); the ROCm build (`llama-b10603-bin-win-rocm-7.14-x64`) was fed the same TheRock runtime DLLs FreeToken uses. FreeToken numbers are measured through the running server (sampling/detokenization included)."
   - anchor `issue #122, Takeaways` @ issue #122, updated 2026-08-27T07:18:05Z: "The **MoE** decode gap (61 vs 83.5) is isolated to the MXFP4 split-K GEMV/swiglu kernels, untuned for RDNA4; gpt-oss prefill is already ahead of both llama.cpp backends."
1. ROOT CAUSE of the RDNA4 --moe-backend offload decode "unspecified launch failure" / driver TDR = two stacked bugs in python/freetoken/kernel/pinned.py, both reached only when the _pinned_tensor CUDA extension is absent: host_register() silently no-ops (so the "pinned" expert banks stay pageable mmaps and the fused fast_index_copy gather makes the GPU dereference unregistered host memory), and device_ptr() falls back to data_ptr() (the host VA) while on Windows/WDDM hipHostRegister-ed memory maps to a DIFFERENT device address (hipHostGetDevicePointer returns e.g. 0x200051000 for host 0x202b7c40000) even though hipHostMalloc / torch pin_memory allocations are unified. Fixed by ctypes fallbacks against amdhip64_7.dll: hipHostRegister(Portable|Mapped), hipHostGetDevicePointer translation, and an identity probe that registers an anonymous mmap buffer instead of a pin_memory tensor.
   - *unaffected*: Confirmed in prose AND in the diff, and independently corroborated on different silicon by Castoff995, who reached the same root cause and shipped a merged but differently-designed fix (fail closed to plain H2D copies) in Maxritz/FreeToken-ROCm PR #1. The root cause is the best-evidenced thing in this source.
   - anchor `PialGhosh2233/FreeToken-rocm-gfx1200 commit 83d24172d37e, python/freetoken/kernel/pinned.py, host_register()` @ commit 83d24172d37ef53742ffd23de3c9d1e7f80dee72, 2026-08-24T03:33:22Z: "Without the extension this used to be a SILENT NO-OP, which left the expert banks pageable -- the fused offload gather then dereferences unregistered host memory from the GPU and dies with `unspecified launch failure` (the RDNA4 offload-decode TDR). On ROCm, register through the HIP runtime instead."
   - anchor `same commit, _host_ptr_identity()` @ commit 83d24172d37e, 2026-08-24T03:33:22Z: "On Windows/WDDM+ROCm, registered memory maps to a DIFFERENT device address even though hipHostMalloc'd memory (torch pin_memory) is unified, so probing a pin_memory tensor here would wrongly report identity and hand the GPU host VAs (the offload-decode `unspecified launch failure`)."
2. Torch CUDA-graph capture AND replay work on gfx1200 for every path the reporter tested INCLUDING the MXFP4 MoE kernels, and graphs are where most of the speed comes from: dense 24.4 -> 93.6 tok/s, gpt-oss 20.7 -> 61 tok/s. The graph-replay MoE crash documented in the base fork's README did not reproduce and is characterised as gfx1201-specific; on gfx1201 the same README additionally records that the OFFLOAD backend "fails at capture time (PAL failed to finalize a command buffer)".
   - *narrowed*: The quote is exact; the scope is not what it reads. Graphs work on gfx1200 for dense HIP MMVQ, fused MXFP4 MoE and the gemma4 offload run. They do NOT work for offload on gfx1201 ("PAL failed to finalize a command buffer", the source's own README); PR #316 shows the offload fused multi-bank copy can fault under HIP capture generally; issue #350 shows --moe-backend cpu and hybrid are SILENTLY WRONG under replay on ROCm (rel err 1.36); and PR #260's ROCm MVP disables HIP graph capture outright. Never cite "graphs work on RDNA4" as a general ROCm graph-safety result.
   - anchor `issue #122, "What works now on gfx1200"` @ issue #122, updated 2026-08-27T07:18:05Z: "**CUDA graphs work on gfx1200 for everything tested.** The RDNA4 graph-replay MoE crash documented in the fork's README did **not** reproduce (it appears to be gfx1201-specific). Graphs are where most of the speed comes from (dense: 24 -> 93.6 tok/s; gpt-oss: 20.7 -> 61 tok/s)."
   - anchor `fork README @ a338f49b, "Known RDNA4 issue: MoE kernels inside CUDA-graph replay"` @ README @ a338f49b93e2, 2026-08-26T07:51:22Z: "`mxfp4_splitk_gemv` / swiglu Triton kernels run correctly eagerly but crash the worker when executed via CUDA-graph replay on gfx1201 (dense models' graphs are unaffected). ... The offload backend additionally fails at capture time (`PAL failed to finalize a command buffer`), so use `--moe-backen..."
3. NOT IN THE ISSUE BODY - only in the fork README at head a338f49b: the first and only real-model measurement of the offload/q* regime on any AMD GPU. Gemma-4-26B-A4B QAT GGUF, Q4_0 experts in ~12 GiB of pinned host RAM banks with the GPU DMA-fetching misses, --moe-backend offload --moe-cache-size 2048 --num-pages 4096, graphs ON, upstream's gemma4 adapter with zero code changes: ~62-73 tok/s decode, prefill ~690 tok/s, ~12 tok/s eager. Same file under llama.cpp b10630: 84.8 tok/s all-in-VRAM (Vulkan) but only 15.0 tok/s in its experts-in-RAM mode (--n-cpu-moe), i.e. "FreeToken's offload decode is ~4x faster in the config the offload design targets".
   - *narrowed*: Exact as written and it is the single most valuable row in the source - but it is a README row, not an issue row (commit a338f49b, 2026-08-26), so anyone citing "issue #122" for it is citing the wrong artifact. Two scope limits are mandatory: the model FITS in the 16 GB card (llama.cpp reaches 84.8 all-in-VRAM), so the ~4x is against a deliberately handicapped llama.cpp configuration; and the 15.0 tok/s baseline is 6 cores of dual-channel desktop memory, whereas our host decodes the same model family entirely on CPU at 48.5 t/s. Do not carry the "~4x" to this host.
   - anchor `fork README @ a338f49b, gfx1200 packed-GGUF table, Gemma-4 row` @ README @ a338f49b93e2, 2026-08-26T07:51:22Z: "**Gemma-4-26B-A4B QAT GGUF** (MoE) | Q4_0 experts in pinned-RAM banks | `--moe-backend offload --moe-cache-size 2048 --num-pages 4096` (graphs ON) | **~62-73 tok/s** | ... llama.cpp b10630 same file: 84.8 tok/s all-in-VRAM (Vulkan), but only **15.0 tok/s** in its experts-in-RAM mode (`--n-cpu-moe`)"

Upstream pinned.py at af71ba43 RAISES ImportError ("Reinstall FreeToken so the pinned tensor CUDA extension is built at install time") from _load_pinned_extension() and has no None-return path at all; the fork base at 45675e47 replaced it with a returns-None fallback carrying the comment "patched: ROCm/Windows fallback", which is what converts a loud install error into a silent no-op and a host-VA leak. Upstream's own device_ptr docstring already documents the Windows/WDDM different-device-address hazard verbatim.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 Stage-2b (born dive-verified). This entry did not exist before; what follows is what a reader of intake-1318#record alone would have got wrong. (1) STALE NEGATIVE OVERTURNED: intake-1318#record records that FreeToken's offload/q* path "has never been benchmarked on any AMD GPU" and was "only validated with a synthetic tiny MoE GGUF". True of the ISSUE, false since 2026-08-26 - the fork README at a338f49b carries Gemma-4-26B-A4B QAT Q4_0 under --moe-backend offload with ~12 GiB of pinned host banks at 62-73 tok/s against llama.cpp b10630 at 84.8 all-in-VRAM and 15.0 with --n-cpu-moe. A second real-model offload datapoint (gpt-oss-20b, graphs off, gfx1201) sits in issue #82 at 16.1-23.1 tok/s. (2) BUT THE 4x DOES NOT TRANSFER: it is set by a 6-core dual-channel baseline, and our host already decodes the same model family end-to-end on CPU at 48.5 t/s (3.2x that baseline) - consistent with, and no rescue from, intake-1318#record's +27% Amdahl ceiling. FreeToken also loses to fully-resident llama.cpp on the same card in EVERY configuration measured (0.73x gpt-oss, 0.83x dense vs Vulkan, 0.73-0.86x Gemma-4). (3) BOTH pinned.py BUGS ARE FORK-INTRODUCED, not upstream: upstream raises ImportError, the port replaced that with "return None". The lesson relocates from "FreeToken is NVIDIA-coupled" to "skipping a native extension gracefully is a dangerous porting shortcut". (4) THE CPU-MoE BRANCH IS BROKEN ON ROCm, SILENTLY, and it is exactly the surface intake-1318#record called a 22-line free port: issue #350 shows the cuStreamWriteValue/WaitValue probe returns False on ROCm, the cudaLaunchHostFunc fallback is not capture-safe, and --moe-backend cpu/hybrid produce rel err 1.36 under replay with no exception. The HIP APIs exist; the runtime does not take them. This NARROWS intake-1318#record's refutation of paper claim 5. (5) THE BINDING GATE ON ANY gfx90a PORT IS NOT KERNEL TUNING, IT IS A ROCm MAJOR VERSION: upstream pins torch>=2.11,<2.12 and triton==3.6.0 and every ROCm reporter runs ROCm 7.14/10.1 nightlies, while we are on ROCm 6.2 deliberately. That is an operator decision about the host under a frozen production kernel set. (6) "Graphs are where most of the speed comes from" is a Windows/Python launch-overhead artefact - our equivalent lever is banked at +5.9% - and the Vulkan-beats-ROCm framing shrinks to +1.6%/+8.8% on Linux and is moot for us because no Vulkan ICD enumerates gfx90a. (7) HOUSEKEEPING: Maxritz/FreeToken-rocm-test has been RENAMED to Maxritz/FreeToken-ROCm, and its merged PR #1 (2026-08-25) carries a DIFFERENT fix from Pial's fork, which branched before the merge. Stage-2b close-out (2026-09-07, operator): the following dive-surfaced ingest-and-dive candidates were DEFERRED by the operator to the Stage-3 expansion queue (not declined on merit): github.com/FlashML-org/FreeToken pull/316 (ErikBPF, open, 2026-09-01) plus its PoC github.com/ErikBP; github.com/Headmaster218/infr (surfaced in issue #122's first comment, 2026-08-24); github.com/UnsignedChad/windows-freetoken-mtp - "MTP (nextn) self-speculative decode over FreeToken'; zenn.dev/holy_fox/articles/53b82eed45f956 - "Trying FreeToken on the RTX 5090: Not Needed for 35B, b. Stage-2b (2026-09-07): intake-1345#record ingested+dived from this entry's dive-surfaced sources; effect on this entry: narrows (claims [2]) - Three effects, one corroborating and two narrowing. (1) CORROBORATES the use intake-1343#record makes of #350: its claim 2 ("CUDA graphs work on gfx1200 for everything tested") is correctly rated PARTIAL, and its scoping - graph capture works on gfx1200 for dense HIP MMVQ and the fused MXFP4 MoE path, but the cpu/hybrid backends are unsound under replay on ROCm - is confirmed at the code level and independently accepted by fix PR #378. Its claim_corrections directive "Never cite 'graphs work on RDNA4' as a general ROCm graph-safety result" stands and is strengthened. (2) NARROWS THE STATED MECHANISM, which intake-1343#record quotes in four places as "the cuStreamWriteValue/WaitValue memops probe returns False on ROCm" with the gloss "the APIs exist, the runtime probe rejects them". The runtime does not reject them - the code never asks it. cumemop_dlopen dlopens libcuda.so.1 / libcuda.so, the NVIDIA driver, and dlsyms cuStreamWriteValue64_v2 / cuStreamWaitValue64_v2 (cpu_moe_ext.cpp:579-604); ROCm 6.2's HIP documents hipStreamWriteValue32/64 and hipStreamWaitValue32/64, which FreeToken never calls. The correct statement is that FreeToken has no ROCm implementation of its fast path. This makes intake-1343#record's conclusion MORE adverse to a port, not less: the gap is a missing implementation, not a lagging runtime. Its would_settle (c) sentence "the probe never reaches HIP [CORRECTED 2026-09-07 by intake-1345#record: cumemop_dlopen dlopens libcuda.so (the NVIDIA driver) and dlsyms cuStreamWriteValue64_v2; ROCm 6.2 documents hipStreamWrite/WaitValue32/64 but FreeToken never calls them, so the fast path is UNIMPLEMENTED on ROCm, not unsupported; fix PR #378 (2026-09-04, draft) fails closed by disabling graph capture and gates its native path on HIP >= 7.14 - a third, harder leg of the version floor]" should be replaced. (3) NARROWS THE VERSION-FLOOR FINDING BY ADDING A HARDER LEG. intake-1343#record identified torch>=2.11,<2.12 + triton==3.6.0 from pyproject.toml as THE binding gate. There is a second, worse one: HIP stream memops are not recordable into a graph before HIP 7.14 (PR #378's own #if guard and block comment; hipGraphAddBatchMemOpNode absent from ROCm 6.2's HIP documentation and present in 7.15's). A dependency pin can be worked around; a missing runtime API on ROCm 6.2 cannot. NOT AFFECTED: intake-1343#record's claims 0, 1, 3 and 4, its verdict (adopt_patterns) and its DECLINE, all of which this entry hardens. ONE FACTUAL ADDITION IT COULD NOT HAVE HAD: a fix PR now exists (#378, 2026-09-04, Draft, unmerged, zero maintainer engagement) and it fixes the defect by DISABLING graph capture on ROCm - which is the same posture PR #260 and PR #374 reached independently, so intake-1343#record's observation that "the community's own ROCm baseline gives up the lever #122 credits with most of the speed" now has three converging instances instead of one.

### Verdict

Treat this as the AMD evidence file for FreeToken - decisive on mechanism, weak on magnitude, and a net argument against porting. Concretely: (1) DO carry the Gemma-4 offload row (62-73 vs 84.8 all-VRAM vs 15.0 --n-cpu-moe, b10630, same GGUF) as the first real-hardware prior for the unrun MI210 --n-cpu-moe probe, ALWAYS with both scope limits attached - the model fit in VRAM, and the 15.0 baseline is 6 cores of dual-channel memory against our 48.5 t/s. (2) DO carry the host-pointer capability contract as a reusable engineering pattern: never let a GPU kernel dereference host memory without a demonstrated nonzero mapped device pointer; run the identity probe with the same allocator the consumer will use; fail closed to a plain H2D copy. The same defect class appeared independently in three separate files of one project. (3) DO carry the ROCm/torch version floor (torch>=2.11,<2.12, triton==3.6.0, all reporters on ROCm 7.14/10.1) as THE binding gate on any FreeToken work here, superseding "untuned kernels" as the stated reason. (4) DO NOT carry "CUDA graphs work on RDNA4" as a general ROCm graph-safety result - offload capture fails on gfx1201, the fused multi-bank copy can fault under capture (PR #316), and --moe-backend cpu/hybrid are silently wrong under replay (issue #350, rel err 1.36). (5) DO NOT carry "graphs are where most of the speed comes from" into local roofline reasoning: that is Windows/Python launch overhead, and our HIP-graph lever is measured and banked at +5.9%. (6) DO NOT carry "FreeToken has a pinned-memory bug on AMD" - both defects were introduced by the community port's own graceful-skip patch; upstream fails loudly. (7) DO NOT read the ~4x as a reason to reopen expert offload on this host, and DO NOT read the Vulkan-beats-ROCm rows as actionable: no Vulkan ICD enumerates gfx90a, and on Linux the gap is +1.6% to +8.8%, not +25.7%.
Against adopt_component and against any port: see the dive's derived-actionables ledger - the hard gate is not kernel tuning, it is that FreeToken pins torch>=2.11,<2.12 and triton==3.6.0 (upstream pyproject.toml at af71ba43, lines 57 and 60) and every ROCm reporter runs torch 2.11-2.15 against ROCm 7.14 or 10.1 nightlies, while our host is ROCm 6.2 by deliberate choice (agentic-rocm-kernel-authoring.md:144 "We are on ROCm 6.2 so this does not bite today"). Against not_applicable: the source is squarely in domain and lands on an open local checkbox. What we DO adopt is patterns, and they are cheap and durable: the host-pointer capability contract (never let a GPU kernel dereference host memory whose mapped device pointer has not been demonstrated; probe with the same allocator the consumer uses, not a convenient one; fail closed to a plain H2D copy), and the reference datapoint that offload-style expert streaming beats a weak CPU expert path by ~4x and still loses to fully-resident execution by ~0.75x.

- updated `handoffs/active/fable5-window2-findings-05c-mi210-lever-category-matrix.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/gpu-acceleration-path.md`

## intake-1344#record — Stop Comparing LLM Agents Without Disclosing the Harness

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23950
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1341#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The Binding Constraint Thesis: for long-horizon tasks across models of comparable frontier capability, harness-induced variance HV(M)=Var_H[B(M,H)] is often comparable to or larger than model-induced variance MV(H)=Var_M[B(M,H)], so a leaderboard that reports B(M,H*) for a single undisclosed H* renders HV unmeasurable and makes cross-model comparison "incomplete and potentially misleading" (Abstract; Section 3, boxed thesis statement).
   - *narrowed*: The thesis is stated exactly as our index carries it and is independently corroborated in direction by 2605.27922 and 2606.12344. What must be narrowed is the QUANTIFIER: the paper says HV is "often comparable to or larger than" MV and "may dominate it", inside a scope restricted to long-horizon tasks and comparable frontier models. Local citations that read it as an unconditional "the harness matters more than the model" drop both hedges and the scope. Also note that Appendix E complicates the simple picture in the paper's own favour: cross-model variance was SMALLEST under the middle harness (1.17 pp^2) and LARGEST under the best one (4.22), so a better controller can either suppress or expose model differences depending on which mechanisms it implements.
   - anchor `Section 3, boxed "The Binding Constraint Thesis"` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "The Binding Constraint Thesis asserts that, in this regime, HV is often comparable to or larger than MV, and may dominate it in many current long-horizon agent evaluations."
1. The disclosure instrument is a Harness Card organised by a SEVEN-layer taxonomy named ETCSOVG - Execution, Tool, Context, Scheduling, Observability, Verification, Governance - with a per-layer minimum-disclosure field set given in Appendix A Table 3, explicitly "intended as an attention checklist rather than a design constraint" whose goal is "to make configuration visible, not to standardize it" (Section 5; Appendix A, Tables 3-4).
   - *unaffected*: The seven layers, the acronym ETCSOVG and the Table 3 field set are exactly as stated. Recorded as unaffected because it is the claim we govern by and it needed checking: the taxonomy is real, the card is real, and the paper's framing of it as "an attention checklist rather than a design constraint" is a direct quote. Our local ERROR is not in this claim but in the delta we asserted between it and intake-921#record's six-dimension list, and in the fact that our card instantiates none of Table 3's fields - see the (a) answer.
   - anchor `Section 5, "The harness card"` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "The seven layers cover Execution (runtime substrate, sandboxing, step and task budgets), Tool (tool list, schemas, error contract), Context (window cap, compression and retrieval policy, persistent memory), Scheduling (agent loop, retry and escalation rules), Observability (logged artifacts and t..."
   - anchor `Appendix A, Table 3 caption` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "ETCSOVG disclosure fields. A submission can provide more detail, but omitting any of these fields makes it difficult to separate model effects from harness effects."
2. Admissibility rule for cross-model comparison: it is valid ONLY under (i) a locked-harness protocol - one specified harness applied to all models - or (ii) a factorial protocol varying harness as a controlled factor with variance components reported. The minimal valid factorial design is a 2x2 model-by-harness grid with task order, execution environment, evaluation script, API parameters and stopping rules held constant; a harness difference counts as "meaningful" only if it changes at least one ETCSOVG layer in a way expected to affect stability, drift or control lag; and four statistics are to be reported alongside the headline score - HV per model and MV per harness, the aggregate HV/MV ratio, the model-pair ranking-reversal count, and partial eta-squared with three stated caveats (Section 1; Section 5).
   - *unaffected*: The two-regime admissibility rule, the minimal 2x2 design, the meaningful-difference test and the four statistics with the three eta-squared caveats are all present verbatim. Recorded as unaffected because it is the part of the paper we had NOT adopted and which is the most directly usable at zero compute. One thing a reader must not assume: this is an evaluation-protocol rule, not an agent-environment interaction protocol - the paper says nothing about how an agent may interact with a task.
   - anchor `Section 1, paragraph 3` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "Valid comparisons require either a locked-harness protocol, where a single specified harness is applied to all models, or a factorial protocol, where harness choice is varied as a controlled factor and variance components are reported."
   - anchor `Section 5, "Variance decomposition protocol"` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "The minimal valid design is a 2x2 model-by-harness grid with task order, execution environment, evaluation script, API parameters, and stopping rules held constant."
   - anchor `Section 6, "Standardization is enough"` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "Whether the harness is locked or varied, the position is that the configuration must be disclosed and treated as part of the experimental condition."
3. Controlled factorial result: 3 frontier models (GPT-5.4, Kimi K2.6, GLM-5.1, selected for tight clustering on the LLM Stats coding leaderboard) x 3 author-built harness configurations H1 Minimal / H2 Improved / H3 Full x 2 runs on a seed-42 difficulty-stratified 100-task subset of SWE-bench Verified gives mean HV = 18.48 pp^2, mean MV = 2.37 pp^2, a 7.80x ratio, and 6 ranking reversals out of 9 model-pair/harness-pair comparisons; per-run ratios are 8.72x and 6.76x (Section 4.2, Table 2; Appendix B, Table 7).
   - *narrowed*: Every number is exact and every variance, the mean, the ratio and the reversal count independently re-derive from Table 2. What is narrowed is what the ratio MEASURES: HV is variance over an author-built quality ladder and MV over three models chosen for being within 0.8 leaderboard points of each other, with n=2 per cell, no CIs, no significance test, and none of the three statistics the paper itself prescribes. The 7.80x is a demonstration that the protocol yields interpretable estimates, which is exactly how the paper frames it. Carry the finding; never carry the ratio as a general fact, and always carry the scope disclaimer with it.
   - anchor `Section 4.2, Findings (Table 2)` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "Average HV is 18.48 pp2 versus average MV of 2.37 pp2, a ratio of 7.80x. ... The interaction term is visible as six ranking reversals across the nine possible model-pair / harness-pair comparisons."
   - anchor `Section 4.2, Findings, final sentence - the scope disclaimer` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "We do not claim that the 7.80x ratio is universal. The grid demonstrates that harness variance can dominate model variance under controlled conditions and that the decomposition protocol produces interpretable estimates on a realistic task distribution."
   - anchor `Appendix B, model-selection footnote 2` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "The three models were selected before running the grid because they were tightly clustered on the LLM Stats coding leaderboard at selection time: on the April 23, 2026 snapshot, Kimi K2.6, GLM-5.1, and GPT-5.4 had coding scores of 45.4, 45.3, and 44.6, respectively."
   - anchor `Appendix B, Table 7 caption line` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "First run: mean HV = 20.67, mean MV = 2.37, HV/MV = 8.72x. Second run: mean HV = 16.52, mean MV = 2.44, HV/MV = 6.76x."
4. Control-theoretic framing: the harness is the controller C_H of a closed-loop discrete-time system and the LLM is an open-loop stochastic policy pi_theta with access only to c(s_t); three reliability quantities - stability (Lyapunov-like V(s_t), Definition 1), context drift (delta_t = D_KL(p_t || p_0) over context-window embeddings, Definition 2) and control lag (tau = t_c - t_d, Definition 3) - are properties of C_H and not of pi_theta. Three trajectory-level metrics operationalise them (Recovery Rate RR(k) over k in {1,3,5,10}, Context Retention, Control Lag), and the paper states outright "We do not estimate these metrics on the 3x3 grid" (Sections 2.2, 3.2, 5).
   - *unaffected*: The control-theoretic framing, the three definitions and the three proposed trajectory metrics are exactly as stated, INCLUDING the paper's own statement that it does not estimate them. Recorded as unaffected and load-bearing in the negative direction: a reader of our HS-6 row could reasonably assume the paper measured recovery, drift or lag. It did not measure any of them, and the perturbation stress test in Appendix C was never run.
   - anchor `Section 5, "Trajectory-level metrics"` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "We do not estimate these metrics on the 3x3 grid. Instead, we specify them as reporting requirements for future harness-aware evaluations."
   - anchor `Section 3.2, Definition 2 (Context Drift)` @ arXiv:2605.23950v1: "Drift is a property of the harness configuration H through its context construction policy c. No property of the model can prevent context drift when the controller fails to manage it."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 combined Stage-1 + dive on arXiv 2605.23950 (v1, the only version). This source had NINE prose citations across five index entries since 2026-07 and NO entry, while being the named source of an ADOPTED disclosure schema in an active handoff - so it was governing unverified. The dive establishes: (1) every figure our index carries from it is primary-verified EXACT - HV 18.48 / MV 2.37 pp^2 / 7.80x / six-of-nine reversals / GLM-5.1 13.0pp - and all six variances, the two means, the ratio and the reversal count independently re-derive from Table 2 as population variances; per-run ratios 8.72x and 6.76x also re-derive. Nothing in the nine index mentions needs a numeric correction. (2) The TWO places we govern by it BOTH misquote it. harness-selection-and-integration.md:164 and wiki/agent-architecture.md:2165 both say the seven-layer taxonomy "adds Observability and Governance" to the six-dimension decomposition; ETCSOVG is a different partition that adds FOUR layers (Execution, Scheduling, Observability, Verification) and drops two (generation control, memory management, the latter folded into Context). The HS-6 card at :182-191 implements that wrong delta and therefore has no Execution row and no Verification row. wiki:2165 adds a second error - "both propose a Harness Card" - when only this paper does; the six-dimension source is intake-921#record (MemoHarness), which proposes none. (3) Our card is a DIFFERENT ARTIFACT from the paper's: an editable-vs-code-owned source audit, instantiating zero of Appendix A Table 3's minimum-disclosure fields, so "adopt its Harness Card as our disclosure schema" is not yet true. (4) The 7.80x ratio does not survive as a general quantity: HV is variance over an author-built crippled-to-full harness ladder while MV is variance over three models SELECTED for being within 0.8 leaderboard points, with n=2 per cell and none of the eta_p^2 / omega^2 / bootstrap statistics the paper prescribes to others; MV's 1.54 pp SD sits inside the ~5 pp binomial noise of a 100-task pass@1. The paper itself disclaims universality. (5) NO CODE, NO DATA, NO ARTIFACT, no Limitations/Ethics/Reproducibility section - the disclosure-standard paper meets its own standard worse than intake-926#record does, and its headline Table 1 is populated partly from vendor marketing posts, a personal blog, one row that is an absolute score rather than a change, and one row whose Grok 4 figure is cited to an Anthropic post about Claude 3.5 Sonnet. (6) It measures OUTCOME ONLY - "belief", "world model" and "elicit" occur zero times - so intake-1341#record's central gap is real and this paper does not close it. Credibility set at 4/6 with the corroboration explicitly marked as direction-only.

### Verdict

dive-verified. This source is SOUND ENOUGH TO GOVERN BY on definitions and protocol, and NOT sound enough to govern by on magnitude. CARRY, without qualification: (a) the ETCSOVG seven-layer taxonomy stated CORRECTLY - Execution, Tool, Context, Scheduling, Observability, Verification, Governance - as a DIFFERENT PARTITION from intake-921#record's six-dimension list, never as a superset of it; (b) Appendix A Table 3's per-layer minimum-disclosure field set as the actual Harness Card schema; (c) the two-regime admissibility rule (locked-harness OR factorial), the minimal 2x2 design with its five held-constant controls, the "meaningful harness difference" test, and the four reporting statistics with the three eta-squared caveats; (d) the control-theoretic vocabulary - stability, context drift, control lag as CONTROLLER properties - as a way to say WHY a harness moves a number. CARRY WITH THE CAVEAT ATTACHED, NEVER ALONE: HV 18.48 vs MV 2.37 pp^2, 7.80x, six-of-nine reversals, 13.0 pp vs 3.0 pp. Every one is exact, and every one is a demonstration on an author-built harness LADDER against three models SELECTED for being 0.8 leaderboard points apart, n=2 per cell, no CIs. The paper says "We do not claim that the 7.80x ratio is universal" and that sentence must travel with the number. MUST NOT BE CARRIED: "the harness matters ~8x more than the model" as a general fact; any claim that this paper measured recovery, drift, control lag or robustness (it defines them and explicitly does not estimate them, and Appendix C was never run); any claim that it releases code, data or an artifact (it releases none, and no repository exists); any Table 1 row as a controlled harness swap - several are vendor blog posts, one is an absolute score in a table of changes, and the Grok 4 row is cited to an Anthropic post about Claude 3.5 Sonnet; any use of it as EVIDENCE in the HS-4 decision (it names no candidate and supplies a reporting contract, not a selection input); any reading of it as covering agent belief or behaviour state (zero occurrences of the vocabulary). MUST BE FIXED, as facts about US that this dive established and that stand whatever happens to the paper: harness-selection-and-integration.md:164 and wiki/agent-architecture.md:2165 both state the taxonomy delta wrongly; wiki:2165 additionally misattributes the Harness Card to intake-921#record, which proposes none; the HS-6 card at :182-191 has no Execution row and no Verification row - precisely the two layers where our taskset/NUMA pinning, timeouts and evaluation entry point (Execution) and our FAIL_TO_PASS oracle, SafetyGate and scorers (Verification) live; and agents/shared/HARNESS_RUN_POLICY.md:93 already requires every run report to name a Harness Card that does not yet exist in conformant form. Finally, DO read this paired with intake-1332#record (Harness-Bench): 1332 is the stronger empirical instrument with released code, this is the stronger normative one, and 2606.12344's OpenClaw ordering reversal is the reason neither grid's magnitudes are portable.
adopt_patterns, with the component explicitly declined and one already-taken adoption sent back for correction. Three patterns are worth carrying at zero compute and are the reason the paper was worth indexing: (1) the ETCSOVG taxonomy AS A SEVEN-LAYER PARTITION, correctly stated - our current adoption states the delta wrongly and drops two of its seven layers (see would_settle_answers); (2) Table 3's per-layer MINIMUM DISCLOSURE FIELD SET, which is the actual schema and which our card does not instantiate for a single field; (3) the two-regime admissibility rule plus the minimal-2x2 design and the eta-squared caveats, which is a clean, MEASUREMENT.md-compatible reporting contract for any future harness comparison we run. NOT adopt_component - there is no component: no code, no data, no artifact. NOT already_integrated - we adopted the NAME of the schema and built a differently-shaped table; two of seven layers are missing and none of the Table 3 fields are disclosed. NOT superseded - arXiv 2605.27922 Harness-Bench is the stronger EMPIRICAL instrument and 2606.12344 the stronger portability check, but neither carries a disclosure taxonomy or an admissibility rule, so neither supersedes this. NOT new_opportunity (no serving or kernel lever) and emphatically NOT not_applicable.

## intake-1345#record — [ROCm] CpuMoeExecutor decode is silently wrong under CUDA-graph replay (rel err 1.36, no exception) - FreeToken issue #350, plus two architecture-independent findings (qsa_block_topk tie-exactness, FREETOKEN_CACHE_DIR dead) and a ROCm kernel-suite census

- **URL**: https://github.com/FlashML-org/FreeToken/issues/350
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: moe_optimization, hardware_optimization, inference_serving, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1343#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. CORRECTNESS DEFECT: on ROCm, --moe-backend cpu and --moe-backend hybrid "produce numerically unrelated output during CUDA-graph replay, with no exception and no warning. Relative error is 1.36 ... Eager is correct. --moe-backend offload is unaffected." Reproduced on all four expert formats (bf16, mxfp4, ds_fp4, q4_0). Four pre-existing tests in the upstream suite catch it - 3 x *_cuda_graph_replay* in tests/moe/test_cpu_moe.py and 1 in tests/moe/test_cpu_moe_q4_0.py - and "as far as we can tell, have never been run on an AMD card in CI".
   - *unaffected*: The defect statement, the four tests and the four formats are all verified against the tree at the named commit, and the fix PR accepts it. This is the part of the report to cite.
   - anchor `issue #350, Summary` @ issue #350, created==updated 2026-09-02T09:43:03Z, read 2026-09-07: "On ROCm, `--moe-backend cpu` and `--moe-backend hybrid` produce **numerically unrelated output** during CUDA-graph replay, with **no exception and no warning**. Relative error is **1.36** ... Eager is correct. `--moe-backend offload` is unaffected."
1. MECHANISM AS THE REPORTER TRACED IT: CpuMoeExecutor.__init__ runs _cpu_moe.memops_probe(...); on ROCm it returns False and the executor logs "cpu-moe flag handshake unavailable: CUDA stream memory operations are not supported here (Windows WDDM / vGPU / old driver); using the cudaLaunchHostFunc sync". It then takes the cudaLaunchHostFunc fallback, and "That fallback is not capture-safe. The captured host nodes do not re-read the freshly written pinned routing buffers, so every replay recomputes from the capture-time values." Two things make it silent: it does not raise, and "raise_if_unhealthy() guards the flag-sync path - precisely the path ROCm cannot take. The watchdog covers everything except the branch that is broken here." The reporter also notes the log line is misleading on AMD because it blames Windows WDDM / vGPU / old driver.
   - *reattributed*: The branch-selection and watchdog halves are confirmed exactly; the CAUSE is reattributed. The probe does not fail because ROCm lacks stream memory operations - ROCm 6.2 documents all four hipStreamWrite/WaitValue functions - it fails because FreeToken resolves them by dlopening libcuda.so, the NVIDIA driver. And the "captured host nodes replay capture-time values" sentence is unproven and contradicts HIP's documented capture semantics for hipLaunchHostFunc. Cite "the fast path is unreachable on ROCm and the fallback is wrong under capture", never the reporter's causal sentence.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-09-07 Stage-2b (born dive-verified). This entry did not exist before. What a reader of intake-1343#record's quotation of #350 alone would have got wrong, and what changed: (1) THE MECHANISM IS REATTRIBUTED. "The memops probe returns False on ROCm because CUDA stream memory operations are not supported here" is the log line, not the fact. The probe fails because cumemop_dlopen dlopens libcuda.so.1 / libcuda.so - the NVIDIA driver - and dlsyms cuStreamWriteValue64_v2 / cuStreamWaitValue64_v2 (cpu_moe_ext.cpp:579-604, :612-613). ROCm 6.2 documents hipStreamWriteValue32/64 and hipStreamWaitValue32/64; FreeToken never calls them. The fast path is UNIMPLEMENTED on ROCm, not unsupported by ROCm. (2) THE CAUSAL SENTENCE IS UNPROVEN. "The captured host nodes do not re-read the freshly written pinned routing buffers" is supported by no artifact, is contradicted by HIP's documented capture semantics for hipLaunchHostFunc, is a near-restatement of the failing test's own intent comment, and has a retracted precedent on the same GPU family (ROCm/hip #3887). The SYMPTOM is established; the cause is not. (3) THE WATCHDOG FINDING IS THE SOLIDEST PART AND IS THE TRANSFERABLE ONE: _err is allocated only under the flag-sync branch, so raise_if_unhealthy is a no-op on exactly the platform that needs it, while engine.py:928 calls it every forward believing it prevents "silent corruption". (4) A FIX PR EXISTS THAT intake-1343#record COULD NOT HAVE SEEN: PR #378 (2026-09-04, Draft, unmerged, stacked on unmerged #132) accepts the diagnosis and fails closed by DISABLING graph capture. (5) THE ROCm 6.2 ANSWER IS NOW A PRIMARY-SOURCE FACT, AND IT IS A THIRD LEG OF THE VERSION FLOOR: HIP stream memops are not graph-recordable before HIP 7.14 (PR #378's own comment and its #if guard; ROCm 6.2's HIP docs have the four Write/WaitValue functions but not hipGraphAddBatchMemOpNode). On ROCm 6.2 the fix compiles its native path out and turns graph capture off for cpu/hybrid MoE. This is worse than the torch/triton pin intake-1343#record found: a packaging pin can be worked around, a missing runtime API cannot. (6) THE PORT-COST ESTIMATE IS RETIRED: intake-1318#record's "22-line CUDA surface" inventory is accurate but the community's realisation of it is ~750 lines across two unmerged PRs and still ends with the lever off. (7) OUR OWN EXPOSURE IS ZERO AND IS NOW ON RECORD: the frozen v9 tree's only cudaLaunchHostFunc is dead code inside #if 0 and there are no stream memops in ggml-cuda, so the +5.9% HIP-graph lever cannot be touched by this defect class. (8) THE TWO SIDE FINDINGS DO NOT SURVIVE INTACT: the topk one is misattributed (the failing shape does not split), and the FREETOKEN_CACHE_DIR one is confirmed as "read by nothing" but its stated source (a Dockerfile in this repo) does not exist at this commit.

### Verdict

Treat this as the correctness half of the FreeToken AMD evidence file, read alongside intake-1343#record's performance half. (1) DO carry the capability-probe failure pattern as a general engineering rule, filed together with intake-1343#record's host-pointer contract: a probe that resolves the OTHER vendor's driver symbols can only answer NO, so its negative is not evidence about your hardware; a fallback chosen by a probe must be validated in every execution mode the fast path was; and a health guard must be wired to the branch that actually runs - here _err is allocated only under the branch ROCm cannot take, so the watchdog is structurally blind on the only platform that needs it. (2) DO carry the ROCm-version fact as a THIRD, independent leg of the FreeToken version floor, and the strongest one: HIP stream memory operations are not recordable into a graph before HIP 7.14, so on ROCm 6.2 the community fix compiles its native path out and disables CUDA-graph capture for cpu/hybrid MoE outright. A torch/triton pin can be worked around; a missing runtime API cannot. (3) DO carry the corrected cause: the probe fails because FreeToken dlopens libcuda.so, not because ROCm lacks stream memory operations - ROCm 6.2 documents all four Write/WaitValue functions. Correct this wherever the old phrasing appears. (4) DO carry that our own HIP-graph lever is provably unexposed (ggml-cuda.cu:4221 is inside #if 0; no stream memops in the backend), so this finding never reopens the banked +5.9%. (5) DO NOT carry "the captured host nodes replay capture-time values" as a mechanism - no artifact supports it, HIP documents the opposite, and the same symptom on the same GPU family was retracted as user error in ROCm/hip #3887. Say "the fallback is wrong under HIP capture, cause undiagnosed". (6) DO NOT carry the defect to gfx90a/CDNA2 as established: it is n=1 architecture, n=1 HIP version, n=1 OS, and the software does not build on ROCm at all outside three unmerged RDNA-only PRs. What DOES transfer to gfx90a is the branch selection (the dlopen fails everywhere without an NVIDIA driver), not the miscomputation. (7) DO NOT cite the topk finding as a split-path exactness hole - at the pinned commit the failing shape does not take the split path - and DO NOT cite the 1471/27/78 census as a pass rate; it has no artifact.
Against adopt_component / any port: unchanged and hardened. FreeToken upstream at this commit CANNOT BUILD ON ROCm AT ALL - setup.py raises "CUDA_HOME is required", both extensions pass libraries=["cudart"], and cpu_moe_ext.cpp includes <cuda_runtime_api.h>. ROCm support exists only in unmerged PRs, and PR #132's hip_compat.h enumerates its supported targets explicitly as gfx1100, 1101, 1102, 1103, 1200, 1201 - RDNA3 and RDNA4 only, no CDNA, no gfx90a. Against not_applicable: it is squarely in domain (MoE serving, HIP graphs, host/device synchronization) and it is the evidentiary basis for a standing local decline. What we adopt is one pattern and one fact. THE PATTERN: a capability probe whose implementation can only ever answer NO on the platform you are porting to - here the probe dlopens libcuda.so.1, the NVIDIA driver library, so on ROCm it fails at symbol resolution and the HIP equivalents are never attempted - silently selects a fallback that is correct in one execution mode and wrong in another, while the health check is wired to the branch the platform cannot reach. Three independent defenses follow: a probe must report WHY it failed in platform terms, a fallback selected by a probe must be validated in every execution mode the fast path was validated in, and a watchdog must guard the branch that actually runs. THE FACT: HIP stream memory operations were not graph-recordable before HIP 7.14, which is a hard ROCm-version floor on any design that wants host-side control inside a captured graph on our host.
